With min 0 it equals `display()` (canonical) for every in-range value, so the existing compute tri-checks — all over `minFractionalDigits`-0 targets — are untouched. *(The JVM actual initially also applied the 15-digit `NumberConversion` cap, with JS eliding it as a "JS-edge" — IF92 removed the cap: the uncapped rule IS the kernel's store behavior, and the JS elision had been right all along. `toComputed` now rides the full `storedComputedNumber` store rule.)*

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ComputedNumberScaleDiffTest` — a QUAD-check (legacy-compute == modern-computeModern == java-static == interpreter, and all == the expected stored form) across `minFractionalDigits` 0/1/2 over `Sum`/`FirstFilledValue`, incl. the zero, whole-number, one-trailing-zero, exact-scale, and keep-extra-digits boundaries. Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS "a computed NUMBER stores PADDED to minFractionalDigits". Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF83 — `FirstFilledValue` over an EMPTY selection uses its operand kind's not-given value (NUMBER→0, DATE/STRING→CLEARED), not always `Num(0)` (FIXED 2026-07-08, graduated from the IG44 re-drill)

IF80 fixed the NUMBER `FirstFilledValue`-empty case (→ the fillable 0) but returned `Val.Num(0)` **unconditionally** for every kind. The kernel's `FirstValueCombiner` is a per-kind family (`FirstValueCombinerNumber`/`Date`/`DateRange`/`String`); on an empty selection each returns its OWN `notGivenValue()` — the fillable 0 (`NICHT_ANGEGEBENE_ZAHL`) only for NUMBER, a NOT-GIVEN value (`NOT_GIVEN_DATE`/`NICHT_ANGEGEBENER_STRING`/…) for the rest. So a DATE or STRING `FirstFilledValue` over an empty star returned `Num(0)` in the interpreter, which then hit `ValCompare.language(Txt(date/string), Num(0))` — the SAME crash class IF58 fixed for date min/max aggregates (a numeric identity where a date/string was expected, a type mixture no legal kernel input produces).

**This was a partial REGRESSION IF80 introduced**, and the DOMINANT crash class in the workspaces sweep: `IllegalStateException: incomparable values Txt(#-#-#/s#…) vs Num` — **85 instances** across `interp-eval` and `interp-compute` (a comparison to the aggregate in a rule condition or a computation precondition/operation). Before IF80, `FirstFilledValue`-empty returned `Val.Unknown` for all kinds — safe for date/string, wrong (should be 0) for number; IF80 flipped it to `Num(0)` for all, fixing number but breaking date/string.

**Kernel behavior (differential-confirmed):** `FirstFilledValue` over an empty selection — NUMBER → the fillable `0` (a VALUE; `< n` fires); DATE / STRING → NOT-GIVEN (the comparison does NOT fire; a computation into that target is CLEARED).

**The fix.** `firstFilledValue`'s empty identity is `firstFilledEmpty(f)` = `Val.Num(0)` iff the operand's declared kind is NUMBER, else `Val.Unknown`. It covers the whole non-number class (DATE, TIME, DATE_TIME, DATE_RANGE, STRING) — the IF58 sibling. The **predicted second instance** (STRING, alongside the reported DATE) was confirmed by the same fix.

**Sweep delta (workspaces):** the `incomparable values Txt vs Num` interpreter-crash class **85 → 0** (no interpreter throws on any workspace instance now — only 5+5 *kernel*-codegen rejections remain in the error tally). Because a crash previously **aborted a whole instance's** eval/compute (so its pointers never entered the differential), removing it UN-MASKS the pre-existing divergences on those 85 instances: visible `compute` runs 330 → 350, `validateFull` runs 465 → 530, total divergences 200 → 274, CVV 162 → 263. These are not new divergences — they are the true state the crash was hiding, now measurable and drillable. The headline is the **correctness gain**: 85 instances the interpreter could not evaluate at all now evaluate.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.FirstFilledValueKindDiffTest` — the crashing comparison (`Cutoff <= FirstFilledValue(empty dates)` / `Label == FirstFilledValue(empty strings)` → kernel/interp firing agrees, no crash), the compute outcome per kind (date/string empty → CLEARED, number empty → 0), and filled controls. Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS "FirstFilledValue over an EMPTY selection". Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF84 — a formally-invalid (constraint-violating) star cell is not-check-relevant in the fill tally + aggregates; `Document.resolveValue` must apply the FULL formal check, not just format (FIXED 2026-07-08, graduated from the IG44 re-drill)

The next IG44 re-drill minimal (`ModelShrinkTest`) reduced to a downstream `Helper = if AtLeastOneFieldFilled(Pricing* / Price) And FieldNotFilled(CurrentPrice) → True` (kernel CLEARED, interp VALUE|true). Root of the diverging conjunct: the `Pricing* / Price` cells were negative integers where `Price` (a shared typedef) declares `minFractionalDigits = 2`, so each value has too few fractional digits — formally invalid (`zahlFalscheAnzahlNachkommastellen`), hence **not-check-relevant**. The kernel excludes a not-check-relevant cell from the fill tally (`isWertAngegeben` — neither filled nor empty), so `AtLeastOneFieldFilled(Pricing* / Price)` over all-invalid cells is FALSE; the interpreter computed TRUE.

**The mechanism — two invalidity paths, one incomplete.** The interpreter has (1) the DmInterpreter `invalid` gate (`formalErrorAt`, the FULL constraint set) — used for DIRECT field refs (`evalField`) and the correlation-`Having` star tally; and (2) **`Document.resolveValue`**, which resolves a raw stored value and applied ONLY the FORMAT/scale check (`formalError` **without** the declared `FieldConstraints`). Path (2) feeds the no-`Having` starred fill tally (`fillScope`/`instCells`) and the aggregate cells (`starCells`). So a constraint-violating-but-parseable value (`"5"` for a min-frac-2 field parses as `Val.Num(5)`) resolved to a real value, not `Val.Unknown` — and `fillScope` counted it as filled. A malformed (parse-fail) value already resolved to `Val.Unknown` (excluded); a constraint-invalid one slipped through — the two forms of `nichtPruefungsrelevant` were treated differently.

**The fix.** Thread the model's `FieldConstraints` into `Document` and pass them to `formalError` in `resolveValue`, so a constraint-invalid value resolves to `Val.Unknown` — not-check-relevant — in EVERY resolution path (the `fillScope` fill tally AND the aggregate `starCells`), matching the direct-ref `invalid` gate and the kernel. A malformed and a constraint-invalid cell are now treated identically (both `nichtPruefungsrelevant` → Unknown), the kernel's one rule. Verified on BOTH consumers: the fill quantifier (all-invalid → excluded → `AtLeastOne` FALSE, `NoField` FALSE — invalid ≠ empty) AND the aggregate (`Sum` over an invalid element → non-evaluable → the referencing rule suppressed, matching the kernel's `NumberCombiner` poison, the `AggMalformedDiffTest` sibling).

**Sweep delta (workspaces):** CVV `kernel=CLEARED,interp=VALUE` **263 → 149 (−114)**; total divergences 274 → 264 (−10). The over-suppression `kernel=VALUE,interp=CLEARED` rose 134 → 160 (+26): NOT IF84 over-suppression (a `Sum`/quantifier over an invalid cell was verified to match the kernel), but the **co-occurring** residual reclassifying as the CVV side is fixed — dominated by the `FieldNotFilled(<cleared computed dependency>)` cascade divergence, then recorded as "the kernel reads the INPUT value, apparently contradicting IF77". *(That reading was later disproved: the real mechanism is the compute-time poison-on-read — IF85.)*

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.StarQuantifierInvalidCellDiffTest` — per-row constraint-invalid (min-frac) star cells across `AtLeastOneFieldFilled`/`NoFieldFilled` (all-invalid / one-valid / all-valid / all-empty) and `Sum` (invalid element → suppressed / all-valid → fires). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS. Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS "a not-check-relevant star cell is excluded from the fill tally".

### IF85 — compute treats formal invalidity as a POISON ON READ, not validation's tally exclusion; the poison cascades through dependents' reads (FIXED 2026-07-08, graduated from IG54)

**The IG54 divergence** — a `Helper = if AtLeastOneFieldFilled(Pricing* / Price) And FieldNotFilled(CurrentPrice) → True` computing on the interpreter where the kernel CLEARS — was recorded as "the kernel reads the compute-cleared dependency's INPUT value, apparently contradicting IF77". **That inference was WRONG: the kernel never evaluated the presence check at all.** The real mechanism (source: `CalculationCache.convertInvalidFieldsForCalculation` — *"During calculation … the invalid entities are converted so that nothing is treated as irrelevant, but causes exceptions to be thrown instead"* — + a 16-scenario differential over BOTH strategies):

- **Poison-on-read.** During `compute()`, ANY read of a formally-invalid field — a value read, a presence check (`isEntityFilled` throws), a quantifier scan visit, a `Having` filter read — throws the kernel's `CalculationFieldException`: the reading computation instance is marked **"fehlerhaft berechnet"** (silently, no message), its **remaining alternatives are skipped**, it produces **no value** (reported CLEARED when input-filled, absent otherwise), and — being invalid-marked — **its own dependents' reads poison transitively** (the errored-value case IF40 observed is the same mechanism with a message).
- **The poison is READ-driven, so evaluation order is observable and must match.** The fill-quantifier scans stop at their deciding cell (`AtLeastOneFieldFilled` at the first filled—an invalid cell before it poisons, one after the complete decision is never read; `NoFieldFilled` at the first filled; `AllFieldsFilled`/`NotAllFieldsFilled` at the first empty over the declared range; `MoreThanOneFieldFilled` at the second filled). `NotExactlyOneFieldFilled` and `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled` each restart the second component scan from the beginning, so each stage has its own stop. `And` short-circuits a clean not-TRUE left (FALSE **and UNKNOWN** alike—the generated code collapses each conjunct 2-valued, `isKnownAndTrue`), `Or` a clean TRUE left; a poisoned left aborts either expression. Value aggregates read all selected cells (any invalid one poisons). A compute-side `Having` keeps only TRUE rows, and a kept candidate receives the same full formal check as an unfiltered read.
- **The two clear-causes genuinely differ downstream — the IG54 row's suspicion confirmed, and IF77's boundary carved.** A **precondition-false / no-alternative clear** is SILENT: dependents read the target **EMPTY** (IF77 holds, unchanged — its lock's differential was a precondition clear). An **operand-invalidity "clear" is a POISON**: dependents' reads throw. IF76/IF78's observed self-outcome (CLEARED) was correct, but recording those targets in the *cleared* (empty-reading) cascade set made dependents read empty where the kernel poisons — the IG54 residual's root.
- **Why the isolated repro missed it:** the isolation used a false-commonPrecondition clear (mechanism 1 — agrees) and an unconditional quantifier over VALID cells; the workspace minimal's trigger was the *interaction* — an invalid cell read (in the quantifier scan or via the poisoned `CurrentPrice`) in a specific read-order position.

**The fix (clean-room, the whole class at the shared roots).** `Interpreter` gains a `computeMode`: the operand-invalidity gate THROWS `PoisonedReadException` (the consult IS the read), `evalField`/`filledTri` add row-correct `CellState` checks (a `Having` row judges ITS cell — `EvalContext.cellState`/`starScanStates`, backed by the `Document` formal check so a well-formed day-omitted date does NOT poison), the fill quantifiers evaluate as ordered SCANS with the kernel's per-operator stops (`computeFillQuantifier`), value aggregates poison on any invalid selected cell, and `And`/`Or` short-circuit. `DmInterpreter.computeInstances` catches the poison per instance (→ poisoned set + reported CLEARED), the static poisoned-dependency PRE-SKIP is removed (read-driven now — a dependent that never reads the poisoned target computes normally), and `OverlayContext` throws on poisoned reads (a poisoned star cell reads `Val.Unknown` → the scan/aggregate sites poison). Validation semantics are untouched (`computeMode = false`).

**Reporting (the IF40 refinement).** A poisoned instance reports **CLEARED** under the interpreter's report-all contract; the kernel reports it only when the input had the cell FILLED — `kernelComputeGranularity` projects the unseeded ones away, and IF40's "a dependent of an errored computation is absent from all buckets" is the *unseeded* case only (a seeded dependent IS reported, as CLEARED).

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ComputePoisonReadDiffTest` — 16 tri-checked scenarios (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter — **codegen-parity confirmed**): the read-order-dependent quantifier poison (both directions), the direct presence poison + empty control, the poisoned-vs-precondition-cleared dependency split, the seeded/unseeded errored-dependent reporting, the And/Or short-circuits (FALSE-left, UNKNOWN-left, TRUE-left), the poison skipping remaining alternatives, the aggregate + `Having`-filter poisons, and the compute-side TRUE-only keep-law. `interpreter … ComputePoisonTest` pins the outcomes on JVM + JS. `ComputeCascadeErroredDiffTest` refitted to the reporting contract. Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS "compute poisons on reading a formally-invalid field". Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

**Sweep delta (workspaces, from the post-normalization baseline):** total divergences **203 → 200**; CVV `kernel=CLEARED,interp=VALUE` **138 → 124 (−14)**; over-suppression `kernel=VALUE,interp=CLEARED` **160 → 143 (−17)** — the pair moved together (the IG54 signature), partially: the residual CVV/VCV bulk has other roots, and the ERRORED-class + iteration-scope atoms did not move (independent of the cascade). kernel-mm stays **0**. Residual classes: [INTERPRETER-GAPS](INTERPRETER-GAPS.md).

### IF86 — an aggregate / count over a starred INDEX field with a not-check-relevant cell is NON-EVALUABLE (index-field awareness generalized from the VLQ path to the aggregates) (FIXED 2026-07-08, graduated from IG55)

An index field's cell is **not-check-relevant** when it is a mandatory-EMPTY/absent cell (the auto-required index — KERNEL-FINDINGS §"index fields are auto-required") OR its value is **DUPLICATED** (the `uniqueIndex` error). The interpreter's index-field not-check-relevant classification existed **only in the value-list-quantifier path** (`Interpreter.vlqSide`/`classifyVlqCell`, IF60) — the aggregate/count path (`starOperandVals`/`filledFieldsValue`/`fillScope`) dropped an empty index cell and counted a duplicated one with no index-field awareness, so it **FIRED where the kernel SUPPRESSES**. The earlier NDV locks were blind to this — they compared at `== 2`/`>= 2`, which cannot distinguish "1" from "suppressed".

**The mechanism (clean-room, both strategies).** The kernel's shared aggregate loop `RuntimeController.combineFeldListe` — used by `summe`/`minWert`/`maxWert`/`anzahlUnterschiedlicheWerte` (`Sum`/`MinValue`/`MaxValue`/`NumberOfDifferentValues`) — returns `getNonRelevantValue()` (`NICHT_PRUEF_REL_ZAHL`) on the FIRST cell where `!isPruefungsRelevant`, so the whole aggregate is non-evaluable and the referencing rule is suppressed (even `NumberOfDifferentValues(Items* / Sku) >= 0` does not fire). `anzahlAngegebenerFelder` (`NumberOfFilledFields`) returns `NICHT_PRUEF_REL_ZAHL` on the first UNKNOWN cell (same suppression). The fill quantifiers (`alleFelderAngegeben`/`keinFeldAngegeben`/`mindestensEinFeldAngegeben`, via `isWertAngegeben` → UNKNOWN) count such a cell in NEITHER `t` nor `f` (the malformed-cell treatment, IF84), so `AtLeastOneFieldFilled` stops firing when EVERY cell is not-check-relevant (a fully-duplicated index) but still fires when a clean cell is present. `isPruefungsRelevant` → `EntityUtil.isIdWithoutSemanticIndexRelevant` → `isFehlerhafteEntity`: an index field's mandatory-empty cell (a PRELIMINARY autogen mandatory rule, IF64) and its uniqueIndex-duplicate cell are `fehlerhaft`. Both kernel strategies share these runtime methods ⟹ codegen-parity. **An index field can be NUMERIC** (differentially confirmed — `Sum` over an index field is authorable, so the numeric value aggregates are reachable siblings, not vacuous).

**The fix (at the shared root — a single index-not-check-relevant classification, reusing `duplicatedValues`).** `indexOperandNonEvaluable(c, path)` (any cell empty or duplicated for an index field) makes `starOperandVals` return `[Val.Unknown]` (→ `combine` suppresses the value aggregates) and `filledFieldsValue` return `Val.Unknown`; `indexStarFillScope(c, path)` classifies each instantiated index cell into the fill tally's NEITHER bucket (empty index cell + duplicated value → `hasUnknownInst`, like a malformed cell), consumed by the no-Having fill quantifiers via `fillScopeInfo`. A malformed cell is already not-check-relevant via `resolveValue` → `Val.Unknown` (IF84), so it is not re-counted. The `Having`-filtered fill tally (`naiveStarQuantTally`) is left index-unaware: the differential could not exercise it (a hand-written `Having` on a fill quantifier degrades in the interpreter parser regardless of the index — an unrelated, unconfirmed shape), so no untested logic was added there.

**Sweep note:** the class is differential-proven (kernel + JVM/JS + codegen tri-check) but rarely seeded — an aggregate over an index field with a duplicate/empty cell is uncommon in the corpus, and the seeded instances rarely hit one, so the value is the CORRECTNESS, not the footprint (the IF71–IF73 pattern). Found by the WI-5a `emptyOperand` facet-probe lane, not the sweep.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.IndexAggregateDiffTest` (NDV/Sum/MinValue/MaxValue/NumberOfFilledFields suppressed on a duplicate + empty index cell, fire on a clean one; `AtLeastOneFieldFilled` no-fire on a fully-duplicated / all-empty index, fire with a present cell; a static-JAVA codegen tri-check on the suppress + fire cases — supersedes the quarantined `NdvIndexFieldEmptyCellDiffTest`) and multiplatform `interpreter … IndexAggregateTest` (JVM + JS). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §4 supplement "…and it makes an AGGREGATE / COUNT over a starred index field non-evaluable too". Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF87 — a field targeted by MULTIPLE computations: the FIRST NON-EMPTY outcome wins in document order (empty/clearing is a no-op) (FIXED 2026-07-08, graduated from the IG44 residual)

The IG44 model-#26 minimal (WI-T re-drill on the post-IF85 baseline) root-caused NOT to any of the pinned candidates (the `Today`-clock guard, the division, the 5-group nesting — all incidental) but to a computed field (`CurrentPrice`) targeted by **TWO computations**: a **base** calc (in group `Common`, guarded by `FieldNotFilled(Helper_…SubTypeCalc)` — FALSE in the instance → clears) and a **subtype** override (in group `BundleProperties`, unconditional `RoundAccounting([PricingSumOfItemPrices] * {1 + [PricingPremium]/100}, 2)` whose money operand is formally invalid → poisons). Both engines cleared `CurrentPrice`, but a dependent `Helper = if … ∧ FieldNotFilled(CurrentPrice) → True` diverged: the kernel POISONED `CurrentPrice` (the subtype calc), so the dependent's read poisoned → CLEARED; the interpreter had kept the base calc's EMPTY clear, so the dependent read `CurrentPrice` empty → fired.

**The mechanism (clean-room, both strategies, differential + source).** A single **flat** model REJECTS two computations for one field (`MVK` codegen error — differentially confirmed); the shape arises ONLY from model COMPOSITION (includes / subtypes, different groups), which the kernel accepts. The combination: a field's computations run in **DOCUMENT (group-declaration) order**, and `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert` SETS the value **unless it is empty/not-relevant** — *"for empty values, do like no calculation was done"* (a no-op). So the **FIRST computation that yields a NON-EMPTY outcome** (a VALUE, or a POISON from an invalid-operand read) wins; an empty/clearing one is skipped and control falls through; all-clear → CLEARED. Differentially nailed with two sibling groups both computing one field (8 cases): value-first wins over a later value AND a later poison; an empty base calc falls through to a later value or poison; poison-first wins over a later value; the winner tracks the earlier GROUP, not the injection order or the value. Both kernel strategies share the runtime calc controller ⟹ codegen-parity (static-JAVA tri-checked).

**The fix (at the shared root — no dedup, first-non-empty-wins).** The former whole-model dependency-order builder **dedup-ed by `targetPath`** (`associateBy` + a `done` set), keeping only ONE computation per field and using its outcome *even when EMPTY* — so an empty base calc masked a later value/poison, and the kept computation was order-dependent (the `byTarget` last-wins + the dependency-visit order — fragile). The shared `orderedFrom` builder now **groups** a field's computations by target (document order preserved), dependency-orders the GROUPS, and returns ALL of them; IF171 caches that model-static result. `computeInstances` applies **first-non-empty-wins per cell**: a `decided` set skips a cell once a VALUE/POISON claims it; an empty outcome is a tentative clear (dependents read it EMPTY, IF77) that a later computation can still override; the winning outcome overwrites the tentative clear in an insertion-ordered `outcomeByCell` map. Single-computation behavior is unchanged (one non-empty or empty outcome → same as before).

**Sweep delta (workspaces):** total divergences **200 → 195** (−5); CVV `kernel=CLEARED,interp=VALUE` **124 → 121** (−3); over-suppression `kernel=VALUE,interp=CLEARED` **143 → 118** (−25) — the dominant footprint, exactly the empty-first-masks-a-later-value over-clearing this fixes. The iteration-scope (11/11) + ERRORED-class (10/4) atoms did NOT move (independent — IG47). No regression (full `:adapter:test` + `:interpreter:check` green).

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.MultiComputationFieldDiffTest` (value-first-wins, empty-first-falls-through-to-value, empty-second-no-op, value-first-over-later-poison, poison-first-over-later-value, and the empty-base + poisoning-subtype cascade to a `FieldNotFilled(target)` dependent; groovy == java-static == interpreter tri-check) and multiplatform `interpreter … MultiComputationFieldTest` (JVM + JS). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §"Multiple SEPARATE computations can target ONE field". Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF88 — a date value below the kernel's always-on Gregorian floor (1583-10-16) is a formal error; a computed date below it is ERRORED (FIXED 2026-07-09, graduated from the IG47 ERRORED-classification atom)

The IG47 ERRORED-classification atom (`kernel=ERRORED,interp=VALUE` — the kernel flags a computed value formally invalid where the interpreter returns a plain VALUE) was root-caused with the new **raw-outcome isolator** (`adapter.laws.RawAtomShrinkTest`, the ITEM-1 tool: hunt + shrink on the RAW interpreter outcome, no granularity projection). It shrank a real composite model to a minimal interaction: **one unconditional `Date(day, month, century, decade)` computation** producing an ancient year. Both engines computed the *identical* value (probe: groovy-dynamic AND java-static both `1000-01-31`, errored=true; interpreter `VALUE|1000-01-31`) — so the divergence is a **formal check on the computed date VALUE**, not a value difference.

**The mechanism (clean-room, both strategies, source + differential).** The kernel applies an **always-on lower bound** to every date value: `FormatDefinitionDatum.validiereFormat` → `dateInvalid(date) = date.before(Constants.DATE_FROM)` where `Constants.DATE_FROM = "16.10.1583"` ("the begin of the gregorian calendar") — unconditional, after the format/calendar parse. A real date before 1583-10-16 is `datumFalsch` (FEHLER_DATUM). This is **distinct from** the opt-in "< 1900" additional check (`DATE_FROM_FOR_ADDITIONAL_CHECK = 01.01.1900`, gated on `Zusatzpruefung`, default false) — so the boundary is 1583-10-16, not 1900 (differentially pinned: 1899 valid, 1583-10-15 invalid, 1583-10-16 valid — strict `Date.before`). A **DATE_RANGE** floors its **start** the same way (`FormatDefinitionDatumBereich extends FormatDefinitionDatum`; `datumBereichDatumFalsch`; end ≥ start covers the end). The kernel then buckets a computed cell as ERRORED iff a formal error targets it (`DocumentComputationResultImpl` maps `formalErrors.getMessages()` by error-field pointer → `getComputedFieldInstancesWithErrors`), so a computed date below the floor is ERRORED. Both strategies share the runtime format-definition (the codegen `CodeGenDateTypeImpl` wraps it) ⟹ codegen-parity (java-static tri-checked). KERNEL-FINDINGS §3-supplement "a DATE value has an always-on lower bound".

**The fix (at the shared root — the date `formalError`).** The interpreter's date formal check (`eval/FormalError.kt`, `Kind.DATE`) only tested format + real-calendar validity, so `computedOutcome` (which runs the SAME `formalError`) returned VALUE for a pre-1583 date. Added `beforeGregorianStart(year, month, day)` (`eval/DateMath.kt`) and, after confirming a value resolves to a real date (all three precisions — FULL / DAY_OPTIONAL / FRAGMENT), a below-floor date now yields the new code `DATE_TOO_EARLY` (`datumFalsch`); a DATE_RANGE whose start is below the floor yields `DATE_RANGE_TOO_EARLY` (`datumBereichDatumFalsch`). Because the fix is in `formalError`, it covers the WHOLE class at once — the compute path (→ ERRORED, the atom), the validate path (a stored pre-1583 date → `datumFalsch`, suppressing a referencing rule), and every date-producing operator (`Date`, `AddYears`, `ValueAsDate`, …), not just the reported `Date(...)` case.

**Sweep delta (workspaces):** total divergences **195 → 190** (−5); the targeted `kernel=ERRORED,interp=VALUE` **10 → 1** (the residual is a DIFFERENT class — a `RoundX(product-of-many, n)` whose interpreter result loses the target fractional scale — the R1 arithmetic residual on IG44's dry-pass row, NOT this floor). Because the fix is at the `formalError` root it also fed the compute poison gate + the validate suppression: a pre-1583 date OPERAND now correctly poisons a computation over it (matching the kernel's not-check-relevant clear, IF78/IF85), so `kernel=CLEARED,interp=VALUE` **121 → 42** and the iteration-scope `kernel=CLEARED,interp=absent` **11 → 0** moved with it; over-suppression `kernel=VALUE,interp=CLEARED` held at **118** (no new over-clearing). No regression (`:interpreter:check` + `:adapter:test` green).

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ComputedDateGregorianFloorDiffTest` (the compute path across the boundary — modern/ancient/floor−1/floor; groovy == java-static == interpreter tri-check — **plus the stored-value DAY_OPTIONAL completion-order pin**, added at the 2026-07-09 review gate per §5 criterion (a): the kernel completes an omitted day to 01 BEFORE the floor check (`PartiallyKnownDatesUtil.replaceUnknownDateParts` runs ahead of `dateInvalid`), so `00.10.1583` is `datumFalsch` while `00.11.1583` is valid — kernel-differential-pinned, matching the interpreter's `dayOptionalParts` day-0→01 mapping) and multiplatform `interpreter … DateGregorianFloorFormalTest` (JVM + JS — the DATE + DATE_RANGE formal codes, format-aware, the DAY_OPTIONAL completion cases, and that the floor neither shadows a format error nor is the opt-in "< 1900" check). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF89 — a computation iterates where it WRITES (the target's scope), not where it is DECLARED; an all-non-repeatable computation computes its single instance regardless of instantiation (FIXED 2026-07-09, graduated from the IG47 iteration-scope atom)

The IG47 iteration-scope atom (`kernel=VALUE,interp=absent` — the kernel computes an instance the interpreter does not) was isolated by `RawAtomShrinkTest` to a minimal interaction: **a constant computation (`→ -1`) declared in a separate, uninstantiated container group (a `*_Rules` group) targeting a field in another subtree whose ancestors are ALL non-repeatable**. The kernel computes the single instance (a `VALUE`); the interpreter reported nothing.

**The mechanism (clean-room, both strategies, source + differential).** Two interpreter faults compounded: (1) `scopeFromRefs` filters a computation's refs+target to their **repeatable** ancestors and, finding none for an all-non-repeatable target, fell back to `comp.group` — the *declaring* group. But a computation iterates where it **writes** (the target), not where it is declared; the declaring group only matters for resolving relative ref paths (already absolutized). With the target in a different subtree, the scope resolved to the empty `/Rules` container. (2) Even with the right scope, `contextsFor` yields rows only for **instantiated** groups, whereas the kernel treats a **non-repeatable group as implicitly present** (index 1) — so an all-non-repeatable computation must compute its single instance even on an uninstantiated target (kernel-confirmed in BOTH strategies: groovy-dynamic + java-static both compute the constant on an instantiated AND on an empty document). KERNEL-FINDINGS §"a computation is scoped to its computed field; non-repeatable groups are implicitly present".

**The fix (at the compute root, compute-local — no validation-path change).** The compute scope's fallback is now the **target's parent group** (`parentGroup(comp.targetPath)`), so the iteration is anchored in the target's subtree. And a new `Document.computeContexts(scope)` yields the real rows when the scope is instantiated but ONE implicit context at reps `[1,…]` when the scope is all-non-repeatable and uninstantiated — the kernel's implicit-presence, scoped to compute only (validation's `contextsFor` is untouched, so no broad behavior change). A REPEATABLE scope with zero rows still computes nothing (no instance).

**Sweep delta (workspaces):** total divergences **190 → 175** (−15). Both iteration-scope "absent" atoms drained — `kernel=VALUE,interp=absent` **11 → 0** (the ITEM-2b target) AND `kernel=ERRORED,interp=absent` **4 → 0** (the same all-non-repeatable-scope mechanism: the kernel's computed instance was ERRORED, the interpreter didn't produce it at all → now it produces it and matches). No regression: `kernel=VALUE,interp=CLEARED` held at **118**, `kernel=CLEARED,interp=VALUE` at **42**, and NO new `interp-extra` / `kernel=absent,interp=VALUE` (no over-computation — the interpreter computes only the instances the kernel does). `compute: kernel-only (interp misses)` 10 → 0. Current baseline: 175 / CVV 42 / VCV 118 / ERRORED 1 (the arithmetic residual) / iteration-scope 0.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.CrossGroupConstantComputeScopeDiffTest` (the cross-group constant computation on an instantiated AND an empty document; groovy == java-static == interpreter tri-check) and multiplatform `interpreter … CrossGroupConstantComputeScopeTest` (JVM + JS). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF90 — a cascade-overlaid computed number is read back in its STORED (minFractionalDigits-padded) form, so a whole-number computed operand does not falsely poison a downstream computation (FIXED 2026-07-09, graduated from the IG44 VCV over-suppression residual)

The dominant IG44 VCV atom (`kernel=VALUE,interp=CLEARED` — the interpreter clears where the kernel computes) shrank (via `ModelShrinkTest -Dig44.vcv`) to a cascade whose minimal interaction is: a computed field valued at a **whole number** (a constant `10`) into a `minFractionalDigits=2` field, READ by a downstream computation. Both engines agreed on every upstream field (`Shipping=10.00`, `TotalPrice=-13.42`, …); only the downstream `[TotalPrice] + [ShippingCosts]` diverged (kernel `-3.42`, interp CLEARED).

**The mechanism (clean-room).** A computed NUMBER is STORED padded to its target's `minFractionalDigits` (IF82) — the kernel stores `10` as `"10.00"`, and a downstream read sees that valid stored form. The interpreter's cascade `OverlayContext` (IF77) carried the numeric `Val.Num(10)` correctly for **arithmetic**, but its `displayValue` stringified it with the RAW scale — `"10"` (0 fractional digits). The downstream computation's **poison gate** (`operandNotCheckRelevant` → `computeFormalErrorAt`) formal-checked that `"10"` against the min-2 field → `zahlFalscheAnzahlNachkommastellen` (too few fractional digits) → threw a poison → the downstream field CLEARED, where the kernel computes over the valid stored `"10.00"`. So the cascade overlay was value/presence-aware (IF77/IF85) but its *display* was not made **stored-form**-aware. (A genuinely-ERRORED computed operand is caught by the `poisoned` set *before* the format check, so padding the display only un-breaks VALID computed operands — it cannot mask a real error.)

**The fix (compute-local).** `OverlayContext.displayValue` now renders an overlaid `Val.Num` as `v.v.display(minFractionalDigits(path))` — the SAME stored form `toComputed` reports (IF82) — via a `minFracOf` renderer threaded into the overlay. Arithmetic still reads the exact `Val.Num`; only the string the formal check sees changed.

**Sweep delta (workspaces):** total divergences **175 → 172** (−3); over-suppression `kernel=VALUE,interp=CLEARED` **118 → 86** (−32 — the whole-number-computed-operand-poison class, the dominant VCV footprint). No regression: CVV `kernel=CLEARED,interp=VALUE` held at **42**, ERRORED at **1**, no new `interp-extra`. Current baseline: 172 / VCV 86 / CVV 42 / ERRORED 1 (the arithmetic residual). The VCV bulk stays heterogeneous — a fresh `ModelShrinkTest -Dig44.vcv` minimal is the next re-drill.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.CascadeComputedOperandScaleDiffTest` (the two-step cascade — a constant into a min-2 field, read by a downstream sum; groovy == java-static == interpreter tri-check) and multiplatform `interpreter … CascadeComputedOperandScaleTest` (JVM + JS). Cites IF82 (the stored-form fact) + IF77 (the overlay). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF91 — whitespace WITHIN a `..` up-run is insignificant (the parser is fully whitespace-insensitive like the kernel's ANTLR lexer) (FIXED 2026-07-09, graduated from IG49)

The IF75 sibling: IF75 made a path's `/` separator and `*` wildcard whitespace-insensitive (`skipWsAndMatch`) and let a `[…]` value ref tolerate a leading interior space, matching the kernel's ANTLR lexer (whitespace on a HIDDEN channel — the gold standard; the kernel fact is homed: KERNEL-FINDINGS "Condition lexing"). The residual (IG49) was whitespace **within a `..` up-run**: `readParentUps` inspected `peek()` immediately after consuming a `..`, so `.. /seg` (a space before the descent) was misread as a **bare `..` terminal** — silently dropping the `/seg` descent and resolving to just the ancestor group — and `.. Name` (a space before the turning-point label) left `Name` unconsumed (a trailing-input error).

**The fix.** `readParentUps` now `skipWs()` after consuming each `..` (skipping whitespace + `;;` comments, both hidden-channel), so the decision "descent `/` vs turning-point label" is made on the next SIGNIFICANT char: `.. /seg` ≡ `../seg` (descent) and `.. Name` ≡ `..Name` (the label consumed + discarded, an ancestor-group ref). The AST is byte-identical to the space-free form. The adjacent forms (`../seg`, `..Name`, multi-level `../../X`) are unchanged. Rare — the `DslRenderer` emits `../` adjacent, so no corpus divergence was attributed to it (a read-tolerance nicety, like IF75); a spaced multi-up `.. / ..` remains out of scope (the renderer never emits it). *Locked:* multiplatform `interpreter.ParentUpWhitespaceParseTest` (JVM+JS: spaced descent ≡ adjacent, spaced label ≡ adjacent, bare-up, bracket-ref interior, and the adjacent/multi-level regressions). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.
### IF92 — a computed NUMBER's stored form is NEVER re-rounded to fit the 15-digit check: uncapped fit render, length-bounded no-fit arm, scale-19 store pre-round (FIXED 2026-07-09, graduated from the IG44 R1 "arithmetic" residual)

The IG44 R1 residual — filed as "the `RoundX(product-of-many, n)` **arithmetic precision/scale** drill", the last ERRORED sweep atom (`kernel=ERRORED,interp=VALUE`) — turned out to be **no arithmetic divergence at all**. The `RawAtomShrinkTest` minimal (a `RoundAccounting(F*F*F*F*F, 2)` into a maxFrac-2 field) computes the bit-identical numeric value in both engines (offline-verified against the corpus atom: `840694776238843.82` on both); the divergence is purely the **stored-form rendering**. The IG44 suspect list (multiplication precision / Round-over-large-operands / the `MathContext(50)` cap) is DISPROVEN — the arithmetic was already exact.

**The mechanism (clean-room, both strategies, source + quad-differential).** The kernel's computed-number store is `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert(VkBigDecimal, id)` — shared runtime code both strategies' codegen templates call: (1) store-time `setScale(19, HALF_UP)` (`VkBigDecimal.DEFAULT_SCALE`); (2) **fit** (natural stripped scale ≤ the target's `maxFractionalDigits`): render at `max(naturalScale, minFractionalDigits)` — **NO length cap** — then the formal check runs on that string, so a 17-digit `354027231847478.01` stores in FULL, trips `stelligkeitZuLang`, and the cell is ERRORED *with the full value*; (3) **no-fit**: render length-bounded to 16 significant digits (`BigDecimalUtils.toStringWertLaengenBeschraenkt(v19, 16)`) and mark "fehlerhaft berechnet" — ERRORED unconditionally, feeding the poison set (IF85). The no-fit arm is reachable on a legal model ONLY via the computation's `errorCodesToSuppress: ["MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES"]` (`IComputation.getErrorCodesToSuppress`); without it the codegen REJECTS a scale-mismatched assignment (differentially pinned: scale-2 `[A]*[B]` is static scale 4, a bare division "?" — both rejected into a scale-2 target). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §11 "the computed-NUMBER store path in full" (+ the IF82 paragraph's locus correction).

**The interpreter's fault.** `Dec.display(minFractionalDigits)`'s JVM actual implemented the **V2 `NumberConversion.determineFractionalDigits`** rule — a formatter NEITHER kernel compute strategy uses at this edge — whose `min(…, 15 − integerDigits)` cap silently RE-ROUNDED a 15+-integer-digit value to a smaller scale (`…843.82` → scale-0 `…844`), so the 15-digit value passed the digit check and reported VALUE where the kernel is ERRORED with the full 17-digit form. The predicted-and-confirmed **second instance** (the drill's criterion): a 14-integer-digit scale-3 `RoundAccounting(A*B, 3)` — the cap clips to scale **1** (`15 − 14`) instead of 0, a distinct arm of the same class (`ERRORED|97263364273283.796` vs the old `VALUE|97263364273283.8`); plus the ERRORED-vs-ERRORED **value drift** arm (16 integer digits, min-2 pad: both engines ERRORED but the old render stored the re-rounded whole where the kernel stores `9726336238030108.21`) — invisible to a kind-only compare, pinned by carrying the ERRORED stored value in the lock's sig.

**The fix (at the store root, compute-local).** `eval/ComputedNumberStore.storedComputedNumber(value, minFrac, maxFrac)` (commonMain, pure runtime layer) reproduces the kernel's three store steps off three new `Dec` primitives (`fractionalDigits()` = stripped scale incl. negative-for-whole, `strippedPrecision()`, `displayAtScale(scale)` = `setScale(scale, HALF_UP).toPlainString`); `toComputed` renders `Val.Num` through it and reports a no-fit cell ERRORED (→ the IF85 poison set, like any errored computed cell). `Dec.display(minFractionalDigits)` lost the JVM-only cap (the JS actual had always elided it as a documented "JS-edge" — the elision was the CORRECT kernel behavior); the overlay read-back (IF90) keeps riding it, which stays exact because a >15-digit or no-fit upstream is ERRORED → poisoned before any downstream read. The 15-digit-boundary control pins that the uncapped render does not over-ERRORED.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ComputedNumberDigitOverflowDiffTest` — a QUAD-check (groovy-legacy == groovy-modern == java-static == interpreter, sig carrying ERRORED values) over the R1 shape, the cap-to-1 second instance, the ERRORED-value-drift arm, the negative-sign arm, the 15-digit boundary control, the three suppressed no-fit arms (plain / length-bounded-16 / scale-19-pre-rounded quotient), and the rejected-without-suppression reachability control (`RuntimeLaws.withComputation` gained the `errorCodesToSuppress` overload) — and multiplatform `interpreter … ComputedNumberDigitOverflowTest` (JVM + JS, 8 cases). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS. (Authoring gap recorded: modelkit/edit cannot author `errorCodesToSuppress` — DEV-API-GAPS.)

**Sweep delta (workspaces):** total divergences **172 → 171** (−1); the targeted `kernel=ERRORED,interp=VALUE` **1 → 0** — the LAST ERRORED atom, the class is drained. No regression: `kernel=VALUE,interp=CLEARED` held at **86**, `kernel=CLEARED,interp=VALUE` at **42**, no new atom class; kernel-mm stays **0**. (The small sweep footprint is expected — the class needs a ≥ 15-integer-digit computed value; the value is the correctness of the whole store path, incl. the corpus-invisible suppressed no-fit arm.)

### IF93 — the star-binding anchor: a bare star's OWN level always iterates fully — a same-group / ancestor star from an iterating row spans ALL rows (FIXED 2026-07-09, graduated from the IG44 R2 mechanism)

**The characterized R2 was a misattribution.** The IG44 row characterized R2 as "the compute-mode aggregate treats a poisoned star cell as skippable" — a faithful own-domain re-expression of that description (root-level `MaxValue(Pricing*/Helper)` operations AND preconditions over a poisoned cell) **passed pre-fix**: the compute aggregate's poison machinery (IF85/IF86) was already correct. The `RawAtomShrinkTest` CVV minimal exposed the real trigger: the dependent is **PER-ROW** (`Helper_IsHighestPrice = if MaxValue(Pricing*/Helper) == [Pricing/Helper] → True`, per Pricing row). Row 2's dependent poisoned correctly (it reads its OWN poisoned `[Helper]`), but **row 1's** did not — its star read never SAW row 2's cell.

**The mechanism (clean-room, both modes, both strategies).** `Document.RowContext.starRows` bound the shared prefix of the starred group and the context's group at the FULL common length — for a same-group star (`repGroup == group`) that binds the starred level itself, collapsing `Pricing*/Helper` to the current row (and an ancestor star to the row's own ancestor). The kernel instead binds only the levels **strictly above** the `*`-marked group — the starred level always iterates fully; `$` (inside a `Having`) is the only outer-row correlation (KERNEL-SEMANTICS §9). The collapse was therefore wrong in **VALIDATION too**, not just compute — the second instance (predicted from the mechanism, reproduced pre-fix): a per-row rule `MaxValue(../Pricing* / Helper) == [Helper]` fired on **every** row in the interpreter where the kernel fires only on the max row. Third arm (also pre-predicted, kernel-confirmed): an ancestor star from a nested row (`MaxValue(../../Pricing* / Helper)` per Tier row) spans ALL ancestor rows. Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §9 "the star-binding anchor".

**The fix (one line at the shared chokepoint).** `starRows` caps the shared prefix at the starred group's **parent** (`min(commonPrefixLen(repGroup, group), len(repGroup) − 1)`) — exactly the anchor `rowContexts` (the `Having`/`$` path) always used, making the two consistent. Every star consumer inherits it (`star`/`starAll`/`starCells`/`starScanStates`/`starOmission`/`indexedCell` all ride `starRows`; `ParallelRowContext` rejects stars — no sibling site). The nested-below case (`commonPrefix < cap`) is bit-identical to before; the R2 poison consequence then falls out of the EXISTING IF85 machinery — row 1's `starScanStates` now includes the poisoned row-2 cell → `PoisonedReadException` → CLEARED. No compute-local carve, and validation changed only where the kernel says it must: the full `:interpreter:check :adapter:test` suites (every star/aggregate/quantifier/correlation lock) hold green over the fix. **Perf-gate consequence fixed in the same pass:** the semantic-index memo (`[F For "v"]` → row map) was cached PER ROW CONTEXT — under the all-rows span that duplicated one identical O(rows) map across O(rows) contexts (O(rows²) MEMORY; the gate's 20 000-row semantic-index scenario OOM'd the test JVM). The memo is hoisted to the document-level `Index.indexRowByValue`, keyed by `(indexField, star-scope prefix)` — one map per scope, shared by every row context and sibling target; the perf gate (all ratio ceilings + sub-quadratic guards) is green over the fix.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ComputeStarAggregatePoisonDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter, per-cell `path@pointer` sigs): the poisoned-star MaxValue + Sum operations, the root-level + per-row + parent-declared aggregate preconditions (the corpus minimal's exact shape), the all-valid control, the per-row VALIDATION fire-set probe, and the ancestor-star fire-set probe — and multiplatform `interpreter … SameGroupStarBindingTest` (JVM + JS: the validation fire set, the R2 poison consequence, the all-rows compute control). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

**Sweep delta (workspaces):** total divergences **171 → 170**; the compute atoms **128 → 118** — over-suppression `kernel=VALUE,interp=CLEARED` **86 → 70** (−16), `kernel=CLEARED,interp=VALUE` **42 → 48** (+6 — net movement within the heterogeneous CVV population, the IF76/IF85 precedent: units previously mis-suppressed now compute and land on the residual mechanisms' side). validateFull `kernel-only (interp misses)` **62 → 41** (−21), reclassified into `both-sides-differ` (44 → 65): the interpreter now FIRES per-row star rules it previously missed entirely — those units still differ on other mechanisms but the fire sets moved toward the kernel. kernel-mm stays **0**; full suites green (no lock depended on the collapse).

### IF94 — `NumberOfFilledFields` honors a `Having` filter, and a MULTI-star operand binds at the FIRST `*`-marked level (FIXED 2026-07-09, graduated from the IG44 A3 re-drill's CVV minimal)

The A3 re-drill's CVV minimal (a per-row "count my duplicates" computation — `NumberOfFilledFields(Sections* / Items* / Code Having ([Code] == [$Code] And [Grade] == [$Grade])) − 1`, guarded by `> 1`) compounds TWO mechanisms; the replay isolated them (all-distinct rows: the kernel counts 1 per row → precondition false → all CLEARED; the interpreter computed `|my own section's rows| − 1` — the filter ignored AND the double star bound to the current section).

**Mechanism 1 — the count's filter (clean-room, both strategies).** The kernel's `anzahlAngegebenerFelder` rides the same filtered `EntityIterator` as every field-list aggregate, so a `Having` on the count's starred operand drops rows FIRST (a malformed cell in a dropped row never suppresses — kernel-pinned) and a KEPT row's malformed cell makes the count non-evaluable. The interpreter's `filledFieldsValue` silently IGNORED `Star.having` — the exact "silently ignored filter computes the wrong answer" defect its sibling `rejectHaving` guards against elsewhere. Fixed with an explicit filtered count (naive per-row loop — the `$`-correlated hash-join stays quantifier-only; a kept malformed cell → `Val.Unknown`/poison; left index-unaware like the other `Having` paths, IF86's no-untested-logic call).

**Mechanism 2 — the multi-star bind depth (refining IF93).** IF93's cap anchored the binding at the LEAF group's parent — right for a single star, wrong for `A* / B* / F`: the kernel binds only the levels strictly above the FIRST `*`-marked group (kernel-pinned: a per-Items read of `Sections* / Items* / X` spans ALL sections — both the filtered count AND an unfiltered `Sum`). The parser now records `Expr.Star.bindDepth` (segments strictly above the first star; −1 ⇒ leaf parent for hand-built nodes), threaded through the `EvalContext` star family (`star`/`starAll`/`starCells`/`starScanStates`/`starOmission`/`rowContexts`) into `Document.RowContext`'s scope prefix and the `OverlayContext` forwarders. The multi-star `$`-join takes the naive scan (the bucket cache is single-star-scoped; correct-but-slower needs no new join logic).

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.MultiStarFilteredCountDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy == java-static == interpreter): the all-distinct clear, the CROSS-section duplicate (the bind-depth discriminator), the same-section control, the unfiltered double-star `Sum` span, and the malformed-in-kept-row vs malformed-in-dropped-row pair — and multiplatform `interpreter … MultiStarFilteredCountTest` (JVM + JS). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

**Sweep delta (workspaces, IF94 + IF95 jointly — one re-drill round):** total divergences **170 → 108** (−62): CVV `kernel=CLEARED,interp=VALUE` **48 → 2**, over-suppression `kernel=VALUE,interp=CLEARED` **70 → 30**, validateFull `kernel-only` **41 → 33**, `both-sides-differ` **65 → 44**; kernel-mm stays **0**. The filtered-count/`$`-duplicate idiom (IF94) is pervasive in the real workspaces — the two mechanisms covered over a third of the whole residual. Full suites + the perf gate green over the round.

### IF95 — the date-component extractors and date diffs accept a DATE_TIME operand and use its DATE part (FIXED 2026-07-09, graduated from the IG44 A3 re-drill's VCV minimal)

**Superseded detail (2026-07-20):** the extractor result remains correct. The original blanket “date diffs accept DATE_TIME” claim was too broad: `DifferenceInMonths`/`DifferenceInYears` reject DATE_TIME at code generation; only `DifferenceInDays` accepts date-time and mixed date/date-time pairs, with the model-zone calendar-step rule now recorded by IF186. The historical implementation account below is corrected to that exact legal boundary.

The A3 re-drill's VCV minimal: `Day = DayFromDate(Source)` over a **DATE_TIME** field (`yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss`) — the kernel computes the day where the interpreter CLEARED. The kernel has **no datetime-specific format definition**: a DATE_TIME field rides `FormatDefinitionDatum` with a time-bearing format string, so `feldWertAlsDatum` parses the full value into its `VkDate` and `getTagAusDatum`/`getMonatAusDatum`/`getJahrAusDatum`/`getQuartalAusDatum` extract from the DATE part. Of the date differences, only `DifferenceInDays` accepts DATE_TIME (including mixed date/date-time); it retains the clock and uses IF186's calendar-step rule. `DifferenceInMonths`/`DifferenceInYears`, `AddMonths`, and `AddYears` reject DATE_TIME at code generation; `AddDays` is the one date arithmetic operation that accepts it and preserves the date-time result family. The interpreter's date component path originally accepted only strict ISO `yyyy-MM-dd`, so a datetime operand resolved `Val.Unknown` → CLEARED.

**The historical fix.** A `dateOnly(v)` normalization (the substring before `T`; a plain date unchanged; garbage stays garbage) was applied to the extractors and the then-current difference paths. The month/year DATE_TIME path was unreachable for legal models and is no longer exposed by the typed authoring API; IF186 replaced the day-difference implementation with exact calendar stepping.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.DateFunctionsOverDateTimeDiffTest` — tri-check: the date and time component extractors over a datetime, the empty-datetime fillable 0 (IF73 semantics), `DifferenceInDays` over datetimes, and the AddYears-rejected reachability control — plus `CalendarStepDifferenceInDaysDiffTest` for date-time/mixed day stepping and multiplatform `interpreter … DateFunctionsOverDateTimeTest` (JVM + JS). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

**Sweep delta (workspaces):** measured jointly with IF94 (one re-drill round) — see IF94's delta.

### IF96 — PARALLEL ITERATION for computations: sibling-group operands join by the shared index field; an invalid index anywhere clears every instance (FIXED 2026-07-09, graduated from the IG44 A3 round-2 CVV minimal)

The A3 round-2 CVV minimal: a per-row `Total* / Amount = [../A/Amount] + [../B/Amount] + [../C/Amount]` where A/B/C/Target are sibling repeatable groups ALL carrying the same-named index field. The kernel runs the **index-field parallel iteration** (KERNEL-SEMANTICS §9's outer join) **for the computation**: the Total row keyed K reads each sibling's row with the SAME key. The interpreter's compute path never engaged its (rule-side) parallel machinery — the common-prefix rule read row 1 of every sibling for EVERY target row, computed everywhere, and mis-summed.

**The mechanism (clean-room, both strategies, source + differential).** Three kernel-pinned rules on top of the rule-side join: **(1) join-by-key, never physical position** (a scrambled side's rows still join by their key); **(2) an unmatched SIDE reads EMPTY** — empty-as-0 for a missing key row AND for a wholly-uninstantiated side group (my first two hypotheses — "positional join" and "missing side clears" — were both OVERTURNED by the differential, the minimize-before-believing drill criterion at work); **(3) an INVALID index in ANY joined group (a duplicated or empty key cell — on the target OR a side) marks EVERY instance of the computation "incorrectly calculated"** — the kernel's `CalculationCache.markFieldsUsingInvalidIndexGroupsAsIncorrectlyCalculated`, deliberately *"marking more field instances as invalid than strictly necessary"* — poisoned + CLEARED, even instances whose own key is clean (the corpus minimal's kernel-CLEARED came from its duplicated target keys, not the missing side). And the calc **never creates a repetition** (`isWiederholungGesetzt` — "no additional repetitions should be created by calculation"): a side-only key computes nothing.

**The fix (compute-local, reusing the rule-side machinery).** `computeInstances` detects out-of-scope repeatable ancestor groups among the computation's non-starred refs and, when present, anchors the join on the TARGET's own scope (the calc iterates where it WRITES — IF89; `scopeFromRefs` over all refs may resolve to a referenced side, collapsing every join row onto one target key — the bug the first implementation had) and iterates `parallelIterationContexts` (the rule-side builder, unchanged): a join row whose target reps carry `UNKNOWN_REP` is skipped (rule 2's no-new-repetitions half), an unmatched side reads empty through `ParallelRowContext` (rule 2), and a new `invalidIndexGroup` guard (duplicate or empty key in any joined group) short-circuits ALL instances to poisoned + CLEARED (rule 3). The per-instance evaluation body is extracted (`computeAt`) and shared with the plain arm — overlay/poison/first-non-empty-wins semantics identical.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ComputeParallelIterationDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy == java-static == interpreter): the scrambled-order key join, the missing-key and uninstantiated-side empty-as-0 arms, the matched-row empty-Amount arm, and the duplicate-target-key + duplicate-side-key invalid-index arms — and multiplatform `interpreter … ComputeParallelIterationTest` (JVM + JS). The round-2 corpus minimal replays to an exact match. Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

**Sweep delta (workspaces):** total divergences **108 → 89** (−19), and the compute KIND-atom classes are **DRAINED**: `kernel=VALUE,interp=CLEARED` **30 → 0**, `kernel=CLEARED,interp=VALUE` **2 → 0** (the round-3 dry-check hunts confirm: no CVV and no VCV atom in all 71 computation-bearing workspace models — the IG44 class is dry). validateFull `kernel-only` **33 → 23**; the residual 21 compute divergence units are VALUE-vs-VALUE only (kind-equal value-string diffs — a different signature, tracked as a fresh row). kernel-mm stays **0**; full suites + the perf gate green.

### IF97 — a CROSS-SUBTREE group star from an iterating row binds along the SHARED named ancestry, never positionally (FIXED 2026-07-10, graduated from the IG56 E1 drill — the first kernel-mm divergence)

The first-ever kernel-mm divergence (a hostile-mode validateFull kernel-only unit) was isolated by the new `ValidateUnitShrinkTest` (E0) to a 2-field/5-group/1-rule minimal: the kernel team's **"an error text per supported language"** metamodel rule — per `Rules` row, `NumberOfFilledGroups(ErrorMessages*) > 0 And NumberOfFilledGroups(ErrorMessages*) < NumberOfFilledGroups(/MVK/ModelProperties/SupportedLanguages*)` — where the second operand is a starred group in a **different subtree** (shared ancestry only at the root). The kernel fires the rule on EVERY `Rules` row; the interpreter fired only on row 1. **The hostile seeding was incidental** — the minimal's placements carry no invalid number cell at all (all strings); hostile mode merely shifted the RNG into an instance shape (2 Rules rows × 1 message + 2 languages) the five nice seeds never produced.

**The mechanism (source + tri-check differential).** The star-binding anchor (IF93) caps an iterating row's bound prefix at the starred group's parent AND intersects it with the **named shared ancestry** (`commonPrefixLen`) — but only on the FIELD-star path (`starRows`/`starScopePrefix`). The GROUP-star row scope — `RowContext.rowContexts`, feeding `NumberOfFilledGroups`, `AtLeastOneGroupFilled`/`NoGroupFilled` (`starGroupTally`), the `Having` row contexts, and `correlationBuckets` — took `rowReps.take(cap)` with **no common-prefix guard**, applying the row's rep vector POSITIONALLY to the starred chain: from `Claims[2]`, a `/Insurance/Settings/Regions*` scope prefix `[1,2]` addressed the nonexistent `Settings[2]` → 0 rows. Consequences, all reproduced own-domain pre-fix: the count/presence under-fired from every row ≥ 2 (validateFull **kernel-only** units), and the reversed polarity `NoGroupFilled` **over-fired** from row ≥ 2 (**interp-extra** units). The kernel binds only along the shared named ancestry in BOTH strategies (groovy-dynamic == java-static tri-checked); kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §9 supplement "a CROSS-SUBTREE star binds only along the SHARED named ancestry".

**The fix (one chokepoint).** `RowContext.groupScopePrefix(groupPath, bindDepth)` now owns the anchor rule — parent cap (IF93), multi-star `bindDepth` cap (IF94), AND the `commonPrefixLen` shared-ancestry guard — with `starScopePrefix` reduced to its leaf-group adapter, and `rowContexts` + `correlationBuckets` riding it. Same-group, ancestor, and nested-below stars are bit-identical to before (their chains coincide positionally with the shared ancestry); only the diverging-chain case changes. *(Drive-by infra fix: `RuntimeLaws.withRuleMessages` JSON-escapes control characters, so a stored condition carrying embedded newlines — as the MVK metamodel's does — can be spliced; the lock pins that exact stored form.)*

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.CrossSubtreeGroupStarDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter): the count fires per-row on ALL rows (typed-DSL + the EXACT corpus stored form incl. the newline-indented absolute operand), the per-row `Notes` count stays row-scoped, the single-region control, the predicted second instance (`AtLeastOneGroupFilled` cross-subtree), and the `NoGroupFilled` no-over-fire + all-rows control — and multiplatform `interpreter … CrossSubtreeGroupStarBindingTest` (JVM + JS). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

**Sweep delta:** kernel-mm hostile **1 → 0** (the corpus is clean again in BOTH modes); workspaces deltas recorded on the IG56 close-out row.

### IF98 — a downstream `Having` filter reads a COMPUTED dependency through the cascade: dependency-ordered and overlay-wrapped (FIXED 2026-07-10, graduated from the IG56 E2 drill's first value-string minimal)

The first kind-equal VALUE-vs-VALUE unit (hunted by the new `-Datom.valuediff=true` isolator, E0) shrank to a 6-field/7-group/2-computation cascade: an upstream per-row boolean (`IsCurrentMember… = if [StartDate] <= Today And (FieldNotFilled(EndDate) Or [EndDate] >= Today) → True`) read by a downstream `Sum(/…*/TimeShare Having [/…/IsCurrentMember…] == True)`. In the diverging instance the upstream CLEARED (dates out of range) yet the interpreter's Sum kept the row — it read the **stale seeded** flag (`kernel VALUE|0` vs `interp VALUE|100`). The own-domain re-expression pinned it in BOTH directions on one instance: a stale-"true" ended row was kept AND a stale-"false" active row was dropped.

**The mechanism — TWO compounding faults, one read-path class.** (1) **Cascade dependency ordering was blind to filter refs:** `referencedFieldPaths(Expr.Star)` returned only the star's own path (dropping `having`), and `computationDepRefs` collected a precondition's refs with the star-free SCOPE walker — so a downstream computation whose ONLY dependency sits in a `Having` filter (or a precondition's filtered quantifier) could run BEFORE its upstream, against an empty overlay. (2) **The overlay didn't reach a filter's rows:** `OverlayContext` delegated `rowContexts` to the raw document contexts, so every `Having` filter read (`starOperandVals`, `filledFieldsValue`, the fill quantifiers) and its kept-row reads bypassed the cascade — stale stored values instead of cleared-as-EMPTY (IF77) / poison-on-read (IF85). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §11 supplement "both cascade read-rules reach INSIDE a `Having` filter".

**The fix (both halves, compute-local).** `referencedFieldPaths` descends a star's `having` via the new full-Cond walker `condDepRefs`, which `computationDepRefs` now also uses for preconditions; `OverlayContext.rowContexts` wraps every row context in the same overlay (so a filter's reads see overlaid values, cleared-as-empty, and poison-on-read), and `correlationBuckets` rebuilds the `$`-join buckets from the wrapped rows via the extracted shared builder `correlationBucketsOf` (the Document-side cache stays raw-read-scoped).

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.HavingOverComputedDependencyDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter): the both-directions stale read (ended-stale-true dropped, active-stale-false kept), the cleared-flag Sum-0 arm, the POISONED-flag arm (the filter's read poisons the sum — the predicted second instance), and the precondition-quantifier-`Having` arm — and multiplatform `interpreter … HavingOverComputedDependencyTest` (JVM + JS, incl. a downstream-declared-first model pinning the dependency order). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF99 — a value aggregate over a GROUP star expands to the contained fields, recursively (FIXED 2026-07-10, graduated from the IG56 E2 drill's second value-string minimal)

The second value-diff minimal (2 fields / 4 groups / 1 computation — a real workspace calculator's `RoundAccounting(Sum(NumbersSum*), 2)`): the aggregate's operand is a **GROUP star** — the `*` on the group itself, no field segment. The kernel expands a group operand to its contained FIELDS exactly as the fill quantifiers do (`ExpandService` → `getAllContainedFields`, recursively through nested subgroups — IF49/IF51's rule, previously implemented only on the quantifier path) and feeds every field's cells to the combiner (`kernel VALUE|617`); the interpreter read cells at the group PATH itself — no field declared there — so `Sum` computed the empty-selection 0 (`VALUE|0`, a kind-equal value-string unit). Kernel-pinned extent: the expansion covers MULTIPLE contained fields AND nested subgroups (`Sum(Charges*)` over `{Fee, Tax, Extras{Surcharge}}` = all four columns); a `Having` on a group star is REJECTED at codegen (`MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`) so the filtered arm is parity-by-construction; `NumberOfDifferentValues` needs a scale-0 target (its static scale is 0). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §-supplement "a VALUE AGGREGATE accepts a GROUP operand".

**The fix (one chokepoint, shared by validate + compute).** `Interpreter.starOperandVals` detects a group star (`declaredKind(path) == null` and `containedFields(path)` non-empty — the new `EvalContext.containedFields`, memoized per group in the Document `Index`) and flat-maps the per-field star semantics over the contained fields (bindDepth preserved). The poison (IF85), index-field (IF86), and empty-selection (IF80/IF83) rules ride the per-field recursion unchanged; validation rules over a group-star aggregate share the same path.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.GroupStarValueAggregateDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter): the corpus single-field shape, the multi-field + nested-subgroup extent, `MinValue`/`MaxValue`/`NumberOfDifferentValues`, the malformed-cell compute poison, the empty group, a VALIDATION-side rule over the same operand, and the `Having`-rejection reachability control — and multiplatform `interpreter … GroupStarValueAggregateTest` (JVM + JS). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF100 — the implicit computation-validation anchors at the TARGET, not the declaring group (FIXED 2026-07-10, graduated from the IG56 E3 drill's first validateFull minimal)

The first fired-code minimal (hunted by the new `ValidateUnitShrinkTest`, E0): **0 rules, 1 computation** — a real workspace computation declared in an EMPTY `*_Rules` container ROOT (`/CDM_ContractRules`), targeting `/Person/WeeklyWorkhours` in ANOTHER root, guarded on a filtered "no active contract" quantifier with term −1. The kernel's rule-from-computation (IF32/IF65) fired `WeeklyWorkhoursComp|OMISSION|/Person[1]/WeeklyWorkhours` (stored 945.98 ≠ −1); the interpreter fired NOTHING: its synthetic `RuleDef` anchored at the computation's DECLARING group, and with no `/CDM_ContractRules` instance content the row-gate excluded the rule from evaluation entirely — a `kernel-only` unit. This is the validation twin of IF89 ("a computation iterates where it WRITES"): the kernel anchors the implicit validation at the **error entity — the computed field** (kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §11-supplement "the generated rule is ANCHORED AT THE TARGET"). A cross-group computation into a **repeatable** target is parity-by-construction (codegen-rejected `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_IN_RULEGROUP`, even star-free — differentially pinned), so the anchor move changes no per-row iteration.

**The fix (one line-pair).** `implicitComputationRuleDef` anchors the synthetic rule node at `parentGroup(comp.targetPath)` (the error-entity relpath recomputed from there); the declared-in-target common case is bit-identical (declaring group == target parent).

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ImplicitComputationValidationScopeDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter): the empty-container cross-root fire (OMISSION at the target), the value-matches-term and tier-not-applicable silences, and the repeatable-cross-group rejection control — and multiplatform `interpreter … ImplicitComputationValidationScopeTest` (JVM + JS). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF101 — a fired fill quantifier over a Having-filtered star is UNCONDITIONALLY OMISSION (FIXED 2026-07-10, graduated from the IG56 E3 dominant polarity-flip class)

After IF100 made the corpus minimal's implicit validation FIRE, its polarity flipped (`kernel OMISSION` vs `interp VALUE`) — the dominant E3 histogram class (`polarity-flip OMISSION→VALUE`, 73 one-sided sigs). The flip rides the precondition conjunct `NoFieldFilled(star Having <currently-active>)`: the interpreter's `Having` path typed fillability over the KEPT rows only (nothing kept → `(t=0, f=0)` → "nothing to fill" → VALUE). **The differential OVERTURNED my first model too** (the add-a-row fillability — criterion (b), minimize-before-believing): the kernel escalates a fired `Having`-filtered starred fill quantifier to `TRUE_AF` → OMISSION **unconditionally** — `AtLeastOneFieldFilled(star Having …)` fired over a fully-kept, FILLED row is ALSO OMISSION, where any fillability model would say VALUE. The fill-quantifier twin of the §8 value-list escalation rule; kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §8 supplement "the `Having` escalation holds for the FILL quantifiers as well".

**The fix (one line).** `predType` returns OMISSION for a fired single-star quantifier carrying a `Having`, before the kept-rows fillability; the unfiltered paths (fillScope / group-star / fixed-arity) are untouched.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.HavingQuantifierPolarityDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter): both escalation directions (`NoFieldFilled` fired at zero-kept and at zero rows; `AtLeastOneFieldFilled` fired over a fully-kept filled row), the kept-empty-cell arm, and the silent control — and multiplatform `interpreter … HavingQuantifierPolarityTest` (JVM + JS). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF102 — the Round family's decimal-places argument is OPTIONAL on both forms; the 1-arg operation form crashed (FIXED 2026-07-10, graduated from the IG56 E3 drill's crash class)

The post-IF100 sweep surfaced a NEW `interp-eval: IndexOutOfBoundsException` skip class (5 instances) — IF100 unmasked it: implicit computation-validations in previously-unevaluated empty containers now evaluate, and one real workspace validation carries a **single-arg `RoundDown([X])`**. The new `CrashProbeTest` drill aid (the sweep only tallies a crash class; the probe finds the first crashing model+seed and prints our stack frames) pinned it to `roundExpr` reading `args[1]` unconditionally. The kernel grammar makes the digits argument OPTIONAL on **both** forms (`rundenOperOp`/`rundenOp` — `(',' ZiffernFolge)?`): an absent digits is the INTEGER round (places 0 — also confirmed by the assignment scale gate: a 1-arg Round has static scale 0), and the field form takes a PRESENT digits argument too — the second, predicted sibling: `roundValue` hardcoded 0 places, so `RoundAccountingValue(Amount, 2)` over `5.125` returned `5` where the kernel stores `5.13`. Kernel facts: KERNEL-FINDINGS rounding entries (two stale "no decimal-places argument" claims corrected in the same pass).

**The fix.** `roundExpr` handles the optional second argument (absent → 0) and the `*Value` dispatch routes through it; the separate 0-hardcoded `roundValue` is deleted.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.SingleArgRoundDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter): the three 1-arg operation forms, the digits-present and digits-absent field forms, and the crash context itself (a 1-arg Round inside a rule comparison) — and multiplatform `interpreter … SingleArgRoundTest` (JVM + JS). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF103 — a rule with no repeatable ref evaluates ONCE at its error entity; an empty declaring container never silences it (FIXED 2026-07-10, graduated from the IG56 E3 hunt-3 minimal)

The next kernel-only minimal (4 fields / 7 groups / 1 rule): a real workspace `*_Rules` container ROOT declaring "the membership end is covered by no contract" — `FieldFilled(/A/…) And AtLeastOneFieldFilled(/B star /…) And NumberOfFilledFields(/B star /… Having …) == 0` — with every reference and the error entity in OTHER roots. The kernel fired it once, OMISSION, at the error entity; the interpreter never evaluated it: `iterationContexts`' scope fallback was the DECLARING group (`scopeFromRefs(refs) ?: rule.group`), and the container had no instance content → `contextsFor` yielded nothing. Kernel iteration is REFERENCE-driven (KERNEL-SEMANTICS §9) — with no repeatable non-starred ref there is exactly ONE evaluation, and the declaring group matters only for resolving relative refs (the ordinary-rule sibling of IF100's implicit-validation anchor). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §9 supplement "a rule's iteration is REFERENCE-driven".

**The fix (conservative — no currently-anchored rule changes).** When a no-repeatable-ref rule's declaring scope yields NO contexts, `iterationContexts` re-anchors at the error entity's parent group; every rule that had contexts before is bit-identical.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.CrossRootRuleScopeDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter): the empty-container cross-root fire (once, OMISSION, at the error entity), the covered-silence control, and the truly-empty-document silence — and multiplatform `interpreter … CrossRootRuleScopeTest` (JVM + JS). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF104 — a fired comparison consuming an encountered Having-filtered star is unconditionally OMISSION (FIXED 2026-07-10; positionally refined by IF184)

The hunt-4 minimal (6 fields / 8 groups / 1 computation): a per-row implicit computation-validation `[F] != RoundDown([Share]/100 * FirstFilledValue(star Having <active-window>), 0)` fired with a per-row polarity SPLIT — the row with NO lease rows agreed (OMISSION), the row whose filter DROPPED its only lease flipped (kernel OMISSION, interp VALUE). The probe arm then OVERTURNED the kept-set fillability model too: the kernel fires OMISSION **even when the filter KEEPS a filled row** whose value merely disagrees. One mechanism with IF101: the kernel combiners' `getValue(containsFilter)` marks ANY filtered result "not specified" (the DateCombiner note in `starFill`'s doc had already recorded this for dates — it is combiner-generic), so every fired consumer of a `Having`-filtered star escalates to `TRUE_AF`. Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §8 supplement "…and it holds for COMPARISONS consuming a filtered star too".

**The fix (the shared root, refined).** `fill()` — the directional polarity analysis every fired comparison uses — returns fillable-both-ways for an expression tree consuming a reached `Having` star (`hasHavingStar`, recursing through Func/Arith operands), before the per-operator arms; `leafOmission` mirrors it for the symmetric fallback paths. IF184 later supplied the positional exception the original single-slot evidence could not expose: inside a multi-operand `FirstFilledValue`, `hasHavingStar` consults the same ordered scan and therefore ignores filters after the terminal slot.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.HavingStarComparisonPolarityDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter): the dropped-all-rows arm (per-row split — a no-rows sibling row in the same instance), the KEPT-filled-row arm, and the value-matches silence — and multiplatform `interpreter … HavingStarComparisonPolarityTest` (JVM + JS). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF105 — a Having-filtered quantifier's invalidity check is per-KEPT-ROW, never the enclosing row's (FIXED 2026-07-10, graduated from the IG56 E3 `row≥2` class minimal)

The `row≥2` hunt (the isolator's `-Dvf.sig='[2]'` pointer filter) shrank to the real-workspace per-row SELF-CORRELATED period-overlap idiom: `FieldFilled(From) And AtLeastOneFieldFilled(../Offers* /Price Having (CurrentRepetition(RuleGroup) != CurrentRepetition($RuleGroup) And <overlap disjunction>))`. In the diverging instance the FIRING row's own `Price` is formally invalid — the kernel's filter drops that row (self-exclusion) before its cell is read, keeps the valid sibling, and fires; the interpreter fired NOWHERE. The own-domain re-expression isolated the discriminator (all-valid passes; invalid-own-cell RED): `naiveStarQuantTally`'s filled-but-invalid arm called `invalid(star.path)` — a PATH-ONLY gate that resolves at the ENCLOSING rule row's reps — so the iterating row's invalid cell poisoned the tally of every kept row. The `$`-join bucket builder had the MIRROR gap (no invalid arm at all — a kept invalid cell counted as filled). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §8 supplement "a `Having`-filtered quantifier tallies each KEPT row's OWN cell".

**The fix (both tally paths).** The naive tally judges the kept row's own cell (`rc.field → resolveValue → Val.Unknown`, the IF84/IF94 machinery); `correlationBucketsOf` gains the same filled-but-invalid neither-bucket arm — the WI-4 property-6 seam (hash-join ≡ nested-loop) keeps the two permanently equal.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.SelfCorrelatedOverlapQuantifierDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter): the invalid-own-cell arm (the corpus instance), the all-valid control, and the disjoint-periods silence — and multiplatform `interpreter … HavingTallyRowCorrectInvalidityTest` (JVM + JS). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF106 — a bare GROUP reference anchors the rule's iteration (ancestor-or-self); per-row judgment of the row's cells (FIXED 2026-07-10, graduated from the IG56 E3 interp-extra minimal)

The interp-extra hunt shrank to a 1-field/2-group/1-rule minimal: the real-workspace `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled(OrderInformation)` — a bare (un-starred) reference to an INDEX-keyed repeatable group, error field inside it, both instantiated rows filled — where the kernel is SILENT and the interpreter fired OMISSION at row 1. Three kernel gates/rulings pinned along the way: the bare reference is legal only on an index-keyed group (`MVK_NO_WILDCARD` otherwise), the rule iterates PER ROW of the referenced group judging the CURRENT row's cells (fires exactly on individually-partial rows, at each row's own pointer; the declared tail is irrelevant), and — via the kernel-captured corpus replay cases, which REFUTED my first fix — the ERROR ENTITY is NOT an iteration anchor (a rule whose error field sits deeper-repeatable than its condition refs keeps the refs' scope). The interpreter's `scopeFromRefs` only considered a reference's ANCESTORS, so the group-ref rule anchored at the declaring group and its group fillScope spanned all rows + the declared range. Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §9 supplement "a bare GROUP reference is an iteration anchor of its own".

**The fix (one flatMap).** `scopeFromRefs` maps each reference to its repeatable ancestors OR ITSELF when the reference is a group path; with the scope per-row, the existing common-prefix `fillScope` resolution is already row-correct. (The first attempt — adding `resolveErrorPath` to the scope refs — was overturned by `CorpusReplayTest` within minutes: the committed kernel-captured cases are exactly the net that catches a plausible-but-wrong generalization.)

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.CollectivelyFilledGroupScopeDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter): every-row-complete silence (the corpus shape), the partial-row per-row fire + pointer, the all-empty-row silence, the no-rows silence — and multiplatform `interpreter … GroupRefRuleScopeTest` (JVM + JS). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF107 — a semantic-index COMPUTE read poisons on an invalid index COLUMN; a clean column stays empty-as-0 (FIXED 2026-07-10, graduated from the IG56 E4 hostile CVV minimal)

The hostile CVV hunt shrank to a 4-field/2-group/1-computation minimal: a real workspace `[OrderInformation/Price For SemIndex]` whose index column carries a hostile 16-digit cell — `kernel CLEARED` vs `interp VALUE|0.00`. The probe matrix pinned the whole rule (and DISPROVED my first no-match reading — I initially misread the run-1 assert and over-fixed the no-match to CLEARED; the differential's rerun corrected it, criterion (b) at work): the kernel poisons every reading computation when the index COLUMN carries ANY invalid cell — malformed, DUPLICATED pair, or EMPTY cell — even when a clean row matches the key (the IndexFieldCache marking, IF96's semantic-index analog); over a CLEAN column the compute read equals the validation read (no-match and matched-but-empty both empty-as-0 — the interpreter had been right there all along). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §11 supplement "a SEMANTIC-INDEX lookup in a computation poisons on an INVALID index COLUMN".

**The fix (compute-scoped, one guard).** `evalSemanticIndex` in compute mode checks the index column's cells (`starCells` + `duplicatedValues`) and throws `PoisonedReadException` on any empty/malformed/duplicated cell, before the ordinary lookup; validation semantics untouched.
