*Locked:* `adapter.laws.SemanticIndexComputeNoMatchDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter): the clean-column no-match empty-as-0, the clean match, the corpus invalid-cell arm, the clean-match + malformed/duplicated/empty-cell poison arms, and the matched-empty-cell empty-as-0 — and multiplatform `interpreter … SemanticIndexComputeInvalidColumnTest` (JVM + JS). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF108 — a semantic-index COMPUTE read of a MATCHED formally-invalid cell poisons, through BOTH read sites (FIXED 2026-07-10, graduated from the IG56 E4 hostile residual)

The post-IF107 hostile CVV hunt shrank to a 5-field/2-group/2-computation minimal: a concat computation `"…" + [OrderInformation/Product For MaxNumber] + "…" + FieldValueAsString(OrderInformation/Quantity For MaxNumber) + "…"` whose semantic-index **matched row carries a formally-invalid Quantity** (hostile 1-frac on a 0-frac field) — kernel CLEARED, interp `VALUE|… was ordered  times.` (the invalid cell concatenated as `""`). Two gaps compounded:

1. `evalSemanticIndex` had IF107's invalid-COLUMN gate but returned a matched-but-malformed cell as `Val.Unknown` — which the concat swallows as `""` (`concatText`), and the IF81 filled-invalid gate inspects only `Expr.Field` operands, so an `Expr.SemanticIndex` (or a `FieldValueAsString` func) operand slipped through. The kernel-pinned rule: a compute-mode indexed read of a matched INVALID cell is a poisoning read — the indexed analog of the direct-field rule (IF85). Second instance predicted+reproduced before fixing: a direct `[Items/Label For LookupKey]` string operand over a too-long matched Label — kernel CLEARED, interp computed. The poison is **per read cell, not per row**: a sibling computation reading the CLEAN Price of the same matched row stays VALUE (kernel-pinned in the same lock).
2. `fieldValueAsString`'s SemanticIndex arm called `semanticIndexCell` directly, bypassing **both** gates — so even IF107's column marking never reached a standalone `FieldValueAsString(ref For key)` read (second instance #2, kernel-pinned CLEARED vs interp VALUE).

**The fix.** One shared guarded read — `computeGuardedIndexCell` (the IF107 column gate, then the matched-invalid poison; both compute-mode-only) — used by `evalSemanticIndex` AND `fieldValueAsString`, so the two sites cannot diverge again. Validation semantics untouched (a matched-but-malformed cell stays `Val.Unknown`). An invalid target cell in an UNMATCHED row is never read and does not poison (kernel-pinned control arm).

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.SemanticIndexMatchedInvalidCellDiffTest` (tri-check, 7 scenarios × 3 computation shapes: concat, direct numeric copy, standalone FieldValueAsString) + multiplatform `interpreter … SemanticIndexMatchedInvalidCellTest` (JVM + JS). Kernel facts: KERNEL-FINDINGS §11 supplement "a matched formally-invalid indexed cell poisons the reading computation". Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF109 — `FieldValueAsString`'s NOT-GIVEN read is `Val.Unknown`, not `Txt("")`; invalid plain reads poison in compute mode (FIXED 2026-07-10, surfaced by the IF108 probe matrix)

`fieldValueAsString` mapped an empty/absent read to `Val.Txt("")` — a REAL empty-string value. Observationally identical in validation (a `""` comparison is suppressed exactly like Unknown — the `vergleicheSTRING` empty rule), but wrong in a standalone computation: the kernel's `handleBerechnetenWert(VkString)` returns early on an empty value ("do like no calculation was done") → **CLEARED**, while the interp stored `VALUE|""`. And the plain-field arm resolved a formally-invalid source to `Val.Unknown` without the compute-mode poison — the same concat-swallow class as IF108 (predicted as the class sibling, kernel-pinned CLEARED before fixing).

**The fix.** Both arms of `fieldValueAsString` return `Val.Unknown` for a not-given read (each context then resolves it kernel-exactly: standalone store → CLEARED via `toComputed`, concat → `""` via `concatText`, comparison → suppressed), and the plain arm poisons a compute-mode read of an INVALID cell (the same `cellState` gate as `evalExpr(Field)`, IF85). Kernel gate learned en route: `FieldValueAsString(stringField)` is model-REJECTED (`MVK_NO_NUMBER` — the coercion is number/amount→string only; a string copies bare `[S]`).

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.FieldValueAsStringComputeDiffTest` (tri-check: filled controls, invalid-in-concat, invalid-standalone, empty-in-concat `""`, empty-standalone CLEARED) + multiplatform `interpreter … FieldValueAsStringComputeTest` (JVM + JS). Kernel facts: KERNEL-FINDINGS §11 supplements. Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF110 — a COMPUTED value is formally checked with the kernel's BASIC check (no charset/blank baseline) (FIXED 2026-07-10, surfaced by the IF109 lock's `" G"` arm)

`computedOutcome` ran the FULL stored-input `formalError` — including `baselineValueError`'s legal-charset / leading-blank / trailing-blank checks — on the computed rendering, so a computed string like `" G"` (an empty operand concatenated before a literal) was bucketed ERRORED where the kernel stores a plain VALUE. Source-confirmed in BOTH strategies (they share `CalculationController`): a computed value goes through `checkField` → **`checkFieldValueFormatBasic`** — only `IFormatDefinition.validiereFormat` (type format + declared constraints) — while stored INPUT values get `checkFieldValueFormat` = charset → leading blank → trailing blank → Basic.

**The fix.** `formalError` split: the type-format half extracted as `typeFormatError` (the Basic check), `formalError` = baseline + `typeFormatError`; `computedOutcome` calls `typeFormatError`. Validation-side input checks untouched.

*Locked:* the `anEmptySourceInTheConcatContributesBlank` arm of `adapter.laws.FieldValueAsStringComputeDiffTest` (the computed `" G"` → VALUE, tri-checked) + its multiplatform twin. Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §11 supplement "computed values are checked with the BASIC format check". Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF111 — a `FirstFilledValue` computation ignores its star's cells AFTER the first filled one; an invalid TAIL cell does not poison (FIXED 2026-07-10, graduated from the IG56 E4 hostile VCV atom)

The last hostile compute atom (kernel=VALUE, interp=CLEARED) shrank to `Result = FirstFilledValue(Items* / Qty)` over rows `[-504, 09]` — the leading-zero `09` is formally invalid, but it sits AFTER the first filled cell. The interpreter's generic compute-mode star poison (`starOperandVals`: ANY invalid selected cell → `PoisonedReadException`) over-suppressed to CLEARED; the kernel computes `VALUE|-504`.

**The kernel mechanism (source, `FirstValueCombiner` — shared by both strategies).** `addValue` ignores every cell once the first FILLED value is captured; an invalid cell sets `nonRelevantFieldBeforeFirstGiven` (→ the non-relevant result → the computation clears) only when it precedes the first given cell, and an empty cell before it sets only the displaceability flag (`notGivenFieldBeforeFirstGiven`), not a poison. So the any-invalid rule is the CONSUME-EVERY-ROW combiners' (Sum/Min/Max/NumberOfDifferentValues — their `combineWith` folds each row); `FirstFilledValue` is the one stop-at-first combiner, the value-aggregate analog of the fill-quantifier scan stops (IF85's `computeFillQuantifier`).

**The fix.** `firstFilledValue` gained a compute-mode ordered scan: an invalid cell before the first filled one poisons; the first filled cell returns; an all-empty selection keeps the per-kind not-given identity (IF80/IF83). IF184 later generalized that mechanism to every operand and made value and polarity share it. An INDEX-field operand keeps the conservative whole-column gate (`indexOperandNonEvaluable` — its not-relevant states are a column property; no corpus unit distinguishes the orders, so no untested per-cell logic shipped).

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.FirstFilledValueInvalidTailDiffTest` (tri-check: invalid tail invisible, invalid-before-first poisons — also after an empty row —, empty-before-first computes, all-empty fillable 0, control) + multiplatform `interpreter … FirstFilledValueInvalidTailTest` (JVM + JS). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §11 supplement. Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS. **Sweep: the hostile compute-atom classes are DRY** (CVV 0 after IF108–IF110, VCV 0 after this — from the inherited VCV 13 / CVV 6).

### IF112 — a FRAGMENT-format DATE_RANGE (`yyyy` year-range) is well-formed; the range side-parse completes by the declared format (FIXED 2026-07-10, graduated from the IG56 E3 `datumBereichFormatFalsch` interp-extra)

The nice-mode hunt shrank the two `datumBereichFormatFalsch` interp-extra units to one shape: a `DateRangeType` with **format `yyyy`**, separator `/`, value `2024/2024` (a year-range — a real workspace idiom). The interpreter's DATE_RANGE formal check parsed each side with the full-format-only `parseDateToIso` → both sides "malformed" → it fired `datumBereichFormatFalsch` on every such value; the kernel parses each side **by the declared format at whatever granularity** and accepts. The fix: a side that fails the full parse completes via `completeFragmentToIso` (the same earliest-date completion a fragment DATE field gets — missing month/day → 01, `baseYear` for a missing year), and the ordering (`datumBereichNichtGueltig`) and Gregorian-floor (`datumBereichDatumFalsch`) checks ride the completed sides — all six arms kernel-confirmed first-pass.

**Sibling site recorded, not blind-fixed (criterion a) — since CLOSED as IF118.** The EVAL read of such a value (`Document.resolveValue` → `parseRangeToIso` → `buildIso`, which required all of y/M/d) resolved `Val.Unknown`, silently suppressing a rule reading a year-range. The kernel's per-side EVAL completion was pinned from source + differential and fixed in **IF118** (start → earliest, end → latest); it was recorded as IG58 first (research trigger) rather than shipping an untested completion choice.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.FragmentRangeFormalDiffTest` (kernel differential over `Trip/Season yyyy` `/`: the corpus instance, ordinary range, start-after-end, malformed side, missing separator, pre-Gregorian) + multiplatform `interpreter … FragmentRangeFormalTest` (JVM + JS). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §6 supplement. Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF113 — a fired string comparison over a CONCAT with a NOT-GIVEN part is OMISSION, not VALUE (FIXED 2026-07-10, graduated from the IG56 E3 nice-mode flip class)

The nice-mode flip hunt shrank a `[9] polarity-flip OMISSION→VALUE` unit to the implicit computation-validation of a concat over a NO-MATCH semantic-index read: stored `Info` ≠ computed `"P " + [Items/Label For Key] + " Q"` where no row matches the key — kernel `OMISSION_ERROR`, interp `VALUE_ERROR`.

**The kernel mechanism (source, both strategies).** `VkString.add` (the concat) ORs the `nichtAngegeben` flags of its parts — an empty check-relevant field read, a no-match/empty indexed cell, a not-given `FieldValueAsString` (and, flag-preserving, `getSubstring`/`bereichAlsString`) keep the whole concatenation "not given" — and `RuntimeController.vergleicheSTRING(op, v1, v2, isNichtAngegeben)` types a FIRED comparison **`TRUE_AF` → OMISSION iff either operand carries the flag** (filling could still change the concatenated value), else `TRUE_WF` → VALUE. The interpreter's `arithFill` typed every fired string-concat comparison VALUE (`Fill.of(false, false)`, "a fired string comparison is VALUE" — a wrong generalization). Second instance predicted+reproduced before fixing: an explicit rule `[Label] != [Code] + "-suffix"` over an EMPTY `Code` — kernel OMISSION, interp VALUE.

**The fix.** `arithFill`'s string-concat arm returns `Fill.of(na, na)` where `na` = any part is a not-given read (`concatNotGiven`: empty field / no-match indexed cell / not-given `FieldValueAsString` / nested concat) — the symmetric fillable Fill flows through the existing `leafType`/`Polarity` machinery and reproduces the kernel's string rule for `!=` and `==` exactly (the directional arms reduce to the flag on either side). A fully-given concat keeps the fixed leaf (fired → VALUE). `Substring`/`RangeAsString` parts preserve the flag in the kernel but are unpinned and absent from the corpora — noted in `concatNotGiven`, extend when an instance is pinned.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ConcatNotGivenPolarityDiffTest` (kernel differential, both shapes: the explicit rule with empty/filled/matching arms; the implicit computation-validation with no-match/matched arms) + multiplatform `interpreter … ConcatNotGivenPolarityTest` (JVM + JS). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §12 supplement. Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF114 — a `Having`-filtered entity COUNT does not ride the value-combiner escalation; its filter flags are the kernel count rule (FIXED 2026-07-10, graduated from the IG56 E3 V→O flip class)

The last nice-mode class ([3] polarity-flip VALUE→OMISSION) shrank to the implicit validation of `Count = NumberOfFilledFields(Items* / Type Having […→Cat] == "Non-Food")` where the filter keeps NOTHING: kernel `VALUE_ERROR`, interp `OMISSION_ERROR`. The IF104 blanket (`hasHavingStar → Fill.of(true, true)`) escalated every filtered-star comparison — right for the VALUE combiners (`getValue(containsFilter)` sets both directions unconditionally) but wrong for the ENTITY COUNTS, which have their own rule (this is the count-vs-combiner split IF104's finding didn't know: these flips APPEARED with IF104).

**The kernel mechanism (source, `RuntimeController.anzahlAngegebenerEntities` — both strategies).** The `(kannGroesser=true, kannKleiner=true)` filter arm fires only when a filtered operand actually COUNTED a value (`containsFilteredValue`); a filter that keeps nothing returns the ordinary count flags — grow-only while `anzAllerFelder > anzAngFelder` (any cell remains uncounted, INCLUDING a filled cell the filter dropped), never shrink. So a fired kept-nothing mismatch is DIRECTIONAL: stored below the count → VALUE (the count cannot shrink), stored above → OMISSION (it could still grow) — kernel-pinned in both directions, plus the kept-something both-directions-OMISSION arms, the no-rows declared-tail arm, and the EXHAUSTED-repetition kept-nothing arm (the filtered-out filled cells keep the count growable — `leafOmission` alone would miss it).

**The fix.** `fill()`'s blanket exempts the entity counts (`isEntityCount`); `funcFill` routes `NumberOfFilledFields`/`NumberOfFilledGroups` to `countFill` — the counted-filtered escalation, else grow-only with `total > counted || leafOmission`. `NumberOfDifferentValues` stays under the blanket (its combiner escalates unconditionally, kernel source). A count NESTED in arithmetic with a filtered star stays blanketed — unpinned, no corpus instance.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.FilteredCountPolarityDiffTest` (7 kernel-pinned arms) + multiplatform `interpreter … FilteredCountPolarityTest` (JVM + JS). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §12 supplement. Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS. **Sweep: the nice-mode residual is expected DRY with this** (10 → 3 → 0 across IF112/IF113/IF114 — confirmed 0 by the EXIT sweep).

### IF115 — the VALIDATION-side not-check-relevant states of an indexed read: a clean match resolves; an unresolvable lookup suppresses (FIXED 2026-07-10, graduated from the IG56 EXIT hostile interp-extra class)

The EXIT hostile sweep's last class ([3] interp-extra OMISSION units) shrank to the `InfosMax` concat family with a 16-digit cell in the index COLUMN and a no-match key: the kernel emits nothing (the lookup is not-check-relevant, the `np` flag ORs through `VkString.add` and `isStringNotRelevant` suppresses the comparison), the interpreter fired the implicit rule with the reads as `""`. The compute-mode gates (IF107/IF108) did not cover validation — and the first fix attempt (the compute column marking applied verbatim) was REFUTED by the differential within minutes: validation is **per-lookup**, not whole-column. The kernel-pinned matrix (9 arms):

- a match on a **CLEAN key cell resolves the read** no matter what the other cells carry — a malformed cell elsewhere does not suppress (kernel fires; where compute would poison);
- a match on a **DUPLICATED key cell** (uniqueIndex marks both cells) and, defensively, a malformed raw-equal key cell → unresolvable → suppressed;
- a **NO-match over a column carrying any not-check-relevant cell** — malformed (a maybe-match), EMPTY (auto-required), or duplicated — → unresolvable → suppressed;
- a **clean no-match stays the NOT-GIVEN read** (na, not np): the concat fires with `""` parts and OMISSION polarity (IF113), the sharp na-vs-np edge.

**The fix.** `computeGuardedIndexCell`'s validation branch: the index-field self-lookup decides a definite match (a matched key cell is by definition filled); clean-matched → the target cell; dup/malformed-matched or unresolvable-no-match → `Val.Unknown`; clean no-match → null (not-given). `concatOperandInvalid` grew the `Expr.SemanticIndex` and `FieldValueAsString` arms so the np-Unknown suppresses a concatenation instead of being swallowed as `""` (the validation twin of IF108's compute poison, riding the same shared guarded read).

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.IndexedReadSuppressionDiffTest` (the 9-arm matrix incl. the direct-comparison sibling `PRC` rule) + multiplatform `interpreter … IndexedReadSuppressionTest` (JVM + JS). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §11 supplement extension. Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS. **Sweep: with this the hostile residual is expected DRY** (3 → 0; the drill's last open class).

### IF116 — the indexed-read COLUMN state is memoized per (column, scope) at the DOCUMENT level; the per-lookup rebuild was O(rows²) (FIXED 2026-07-10, caught by the master gate's independent perf re-run)

The IF107/IF115 gates — and the older IF15/IF60 index-aware aggregate/quantifier classification — consulted the index column by REBUILDING it per lookup: a fresh `starCells` list plus a fresh duplicate-value set per call. A `validateFull` over a per-row indexed rule therefore scanned the whole column once per row — **O(rows²)** with a heavy allocation constant. The perf gate's `semanticIndexStaysSubQuadratic` (N=20 000) stalled 20+ minutes, twice, deterministically; the thread dump pinned `indexColumnInvalid → starCells`. (The Set-E worker's own perf-gate run predated IF115's wiring of the guard into the VALIDATION path — the change that put the scan on the per-row hot path.) The fix is pure memoization: no differential outcome changes.

**The fix (the `indexedCell` pattern).** `EvalContext.indexColumn(path, bindDepth)` returns an `IndexColumn` — the cells, the `duplicated` value set, and the derived flags/tally (`anyNotCheckRelevant` = empty|malformed|dup, IF107/IF115; `anyEmptyOrDuplicated` — malformed not re-counted, IF15/IF60; `cleanFilled`) — ALL derived once at construction, so a consulting site is O(1). The RAW row context memoizes it per (column, star-scope prefix) at the DOCUMENT level (`Index.indexColumnByScope`, the twin of `indexedCell`'s `indexRowByValue` map — shared across every row context of the scope). All five former scan sites ride it: `indexColumnInvalid`, the matched-key duplicate check, `indexOperandNonEvaluable`, `indexStarFillScope`, and the VLQ duplicate-marking.

**The overlay cannot delegate to the raw memo (the `by d` delegation trap), so it caches per computation STEP.** `OverlayContext` overrides `indexColumn` to build from its own overlaid cells — interface delegation would silently route to the raw context's memo (stale/raw cells whenever the cascade computed/cleared/poisoned an index-column cell), and the overlay's view legitimately changes as the cascade lands. It therefore keeps its OWN per-step cache keyed by the same `(column, scope)` (IF143, was IG60): a COMPUTE-mode indexed read builds once per computation step and is shared across the step's outer rows, bypassed only for the step's own write column.

*Locked:* the perf gate `adapter.perf.PerfGateTest.semanticIndexStaysSubQuadratic` (red: two deterministic multi-minute stalls; green: linear-band growth) + the index differentials re-run green (`SemanticIndexComputeNoMatchDiffTest`, `SemanticIndexMatchedInvalidCellDiffTest`, `IndexedReadSuppressionDiffTest`, `FieldValueAsStringComputeDiffTest`, `IndexAggregateDiffTest`, `ValueListQuantifierStarDiffTest`) + the full suites. Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF117 — the shared eval-state seam: the state's LIFETIME picks the collaborator shape (DESIGNED 2026-07-11, the IG59 Set-K redesign)

The IG59 remainder — I1's polarity walk / value functions / value-list quantifiers, I2's computation engine / auto-check emitter / `RepetitionNotUnique` evaluator — does not extract behavior-neutrally into standalone classes: each cluster is resolution-coupled (it reads the row context, spawns `Having` sub-interpreters, and mutually recurses with the truth walk), so a standalone class needs either a fat call-back interface (~15+ hooks) or a flat ~40-member private→internal widening, both rejected in the IG59 row (binding). The third shape is a **shared eval-state seam** the strategy and its collaborators both hold. The design key: the two god-files hold state of **different lifetimes**, and the lifetime dictates the only collaborator shape that is both clean and allocation-neutral.

**I1 (`eval/Interpreter.kt`) — the state is PER-ROW, so the strategy instance IS the seam.** An `Interpreter` is one immutable per-row eval-state — `(ctx, config, customErrorField, invalid, outer, outerUnbound)` — allocated once per iteration row (and once per `Having`/overlap sub-row via the spawners). Any collaborator must share that state without adding per-row allocation (the enemy — the whole point of `EvalConfig`, IG59 I4). The chosen shape:

- The **`Interpreter` instance stays the single shared eval-state**; the two genuinely per-row inputs (`customErrorField`, `invalid` — they close over `ctx`) stay constructor args, exactly as `EvalConfig` left them (perf-load-bearing).
- The three clusters move to **same-package extension-function families** on `Interpreter`, one file per collaborator: `ExprEval.kt` (value-expression resolution — `evalExpr`/`evalFunc` + the ~30 value helpers, arith/const/semantic-index/date machinery), `PolarityWalk.kt` (the typed truth+polarity walks + the `Fill` resolution machinery + comparison/predicate/tolerance polarity; owns the interned `Res`), `ValueListQuantEval.kt` (VLQ truth + polarity + `VlqSide` — sliced VERTICALLY because VLQ's truth and polarity share `vlqSide`, unlike the fill machinery, which is ONE kernel subsystem (`VkBigDecimal` fill propagation) serving several constructs and therefore stays horizontal).
- The **seam contract** is `Interpreter`'s `internal` surface, in two designed groups: the state reads (`ctx`, `invalid`, `clock`, `baseYear`, `indexFields`, `cellRelevant`, `starRelevant`, `computeMode`) and the cross-cluster dispatch (`ctxOf`/`outerUnknown`/`havingInterp`/`pathOf`/`presenceTri`, the truth leaves `evalCmp`/`evalTolerance`/`evalPred`/`evalPattern`/`evalCustom`, and the shared tallies `overlapAny`/`atLeastOneOverlap`/`fillScopeInfo`/`hasFillScopeOperand`/`quantCounts`). Everything else stays `private` — to the class (the truth walk, the gates, the scans, the SPI registries) or to the collaborator file (top-level `private fun Interpreter.…`), which is TIGHTER than today's class-wide visibility. `internal` is module-scoped, so nothing widens outside `:interpreter`; the public API (constructor + `fires`/`eval`/`firedType`/`evalExpression`) is byte-identical.
- Polarity ownership: `PolarityWalk` owns the generic walk + the fill machinery; a construct whose polarity is computed IN the truth scan keeps it vertical (the overlap predicates return `Res` from one scan and stay in the strategy; VLQ owns both halves in its file). `Res` is the shared truth+polarity currency (internal).

**Why extensions and not `internal object` collaborators taking a state parameter:** the two compile to the same shape (a static function whose first argument is the receiver — no dispatch or allocation difference), but the extension form keeps every moved body **textually identical** (implicit `this` resolves `ctx.filled(…)` exactly as the member did) — the lowest-transcription-risk move for ~1,400 lines of semantics-dense, findings-annotated code, and call sites keep reading as members. The collaborator identity lives in the file + its KDoc header.

**I2 (`DmInterpreter.kt`) — the state is CALL-INVARIANT or PER-CALL, so real injected collaborators + a per-call pass object.** `DmInterpreter` holds model+SPI state built once per engine, plus a per-validation bundle currently THREADED as ~8 parameters through every signature (`doc, locale, dup, cache, isRelevant, customRelevant, starRelevant`) — the parameter-threading is the file's real smell. The chosen shape:

- **Call-invariant collaborators, constructor-injected once per `DmInterpreter`** (no per-row/per-call cost): `ParsedModel` (the parse-once caches + the implicit computation-validation rules), `AutoChecks` (the auto-generated checks + the formal-status oracle `formalErrorAt`/`computeFormalErrorAt` — the one service every other unit consults), `RnuEvaluator` (recognizer + emitter; receives the rule's AST from the caller, so it needs no parse-cache dependency), `ComputationEngine` (the cascade — overlay/poison/parallel), and `IterationScopes` (scope resolution + parallel-join context building, shared by the rule path and the engine). A DAG — nothing calls back into `DmInterpreter`.
- **The per-call seam is `ValidationPass`**: doc, locale, the relevance trio, the duplicate-index set, the complete `FormalResultCache`, the ONCE-PER-CALL `EvalConfig` (hoisted from once-per-rule — content-identical, strictly fewer allocations), and the per-row factory `interpreterFor`. The formal cache retains the full result rather than only a code; relevance is checked before it can invoke a custom validator, and emission, suppression, RNU, and computation consume the same cached result. Overlay-aware computation keys include the effective raw value so a later cascade value cannot inherit an earlier cell result. `testRule`/`evalCandidate` and computation build fresh call-local caches; none escape to the reusable engine.
- Pure string/AST helpers (`pathParts`/`parentGroup`/`ancestorGroups`/`rootGroupOf`/`relativePath`/`resolveErrorPath`; the `nonStarredRefs`/`condDepRefs` ref walks) become top-level `internal` functions — they carry no state at all.
- The compute cascade's own per-call state (overlay/cleared/poisoned/decided) is already reified in `OverlayContext` (IG59 I2 slice) and stays local to `computeInstances` — no new seam object; the engine extraction moves it verbatim (which is why the IG60 overlay-cache rider did NOT land here: the overlay lifecycle is not reshaped — the cache later landed on its own as IF143).

**Rejected alternatives (recorded so the shape isn't re-litigated):**
1. **Fat call-back interface** (IG59, binding) — ~15+ hooks of inversion ceremony; megamorphic dispatch on the hottest walk; the seam becomes a lattice nobody can read.
2. **Flat private→internal widening with the god-class intact** (IG59, binding) — relocation, not decoupling: ~40 helpers become module-callable while the class keeps every responsibility. The seam differs in kind, not just count: the widened surface is the *designed contract only* (state + dispatch), the moved clusters' ~90 helpers become FILE-private, and ~1,400 lines leave the class — the module-visible surface shrinks relative to today's one-class-sees-all.
3. **A separate `EvalState` class + `Interpreter` façade** — +1 allocation on the per-row hot path (façade + state) and a hop on every state read; buys nothing the receiver-as-seam shape doesn't.
4. **Collaborators as per-row instances** (the `Interpreter` holding walk objects) — 4–5× per-row allocation; exactly the cost `EvalConfig` was built to remove.
5. **Collaborators as per-call instances with the row threaded as a parameter** — the row bundle IS `(ctx, customErrorField, invalid, outer)`, i.e. the `Interpreter`; threading it as an explicit parameter is the receiver shape with signature churn on every hot call and the same internal surface.
6. **Vertical slicing per operator family for I1** — the fill machinery is one kernel subsystem shared by comparison/tolerance/predicate polarity; slicing it per construct would duplicate it across files. VLQ is the deliberate exception (its truth and polarity share `vlqSide` and nothing else uses them).
7. **One idiom for both files** — rejected because the lifetimes differ: I2's call-invariant units make real constructor-injected classes free (allocated once per engine), which is strictly better decoupling than extensions; I1's per-row state makes real classes an allocation regression, which is why the extension seam exists there.

Acceptance stays IG59's: strictly behavior-neutral, landed one cluster per commit under the full nets (differential + fuzz + the perf gate); any behavior delta is a stop-the-line finding. The as-is structure lands in [INTERPRETER-ARCHITECTURE.md](INTERPRETER-ARCHITECTURE.md) §2/§4 with the code.

**GRADUATED 2026-07-11 (Set K — the IG59 remainder fully landed; the row is deleted, this note + git are the record).** The seam landed in six behavior-neutral steps — `62356ee0` (VLQ) → `c302a6f3` (value-expression resolution) → `39fa875d` (polarity walk) → `35367e0b` (ValidationPass + AutoChecks) → `8fa4ade0` (RepetitionNotUniqueEvaluator + IterationScopes) → `9ed737bb` (ComputationEngine + ParsedModel) — each under the full nets (differential + fuzz suites forced fresh, the perf gate), with `perf.sh bench` + both real-corpus sweeps (plain + hostile, skip-count-verified) at exit. `eval/Interpreter.kt` went 2,294 → 824 lines and `DmInterpreter.kt` 1,368 → 339, with no public-API or behavior change. Residual dispositions absorbed from the deleted IG59 row: the **overlap cluster deliberately stays in the strategy** (its polarity is computed in the same scan as its truth — the vertical exception), so `rangesOverlap` keeps its home there and `power` its home in `ExprEval.kt` (the I5 "fold into a DateRangeOverlap collaborator if/when extracted" trigger resolved as won't-extract); the **`SingleRowDoc`/`RepDoc` doubles stay in `commonMain`** as deliberate cross-module shared doubles (I8 — 8 `:adapter` differential tests construct them; honoring a test-only move needs a shared-test-fixtures build change, not a source move; their KDocs record it). The IG60 overlay-cache rider was NOT taken here: the engine extraction moved `computeInstances` verbatim, so the cache's safety precondition (a reshaped overlay lifecycle) never flipped — the cache later landed on its own (IF143, a per-computation-step cache, no lifecycle reshape needed).

### IF118 — a FRAGMENT-format DATE_RANGE (`yyyy` year-range) completes its endpoints ASYMMETRICALLY at EVAL: start → earliest, end → latest (FIXED 2026-07-11, graduated from IG58)

The EVAL twin of IF112 (which fixed the FORMAL side of the same shape). `Document.resolveValue` canonicalized a DATE_RANGE via `parseRangeToIso`, whose `buildIso` required all of y/M/d — so a fragment-format range (`yyyy`, value `2024/2025`) produced no `Val.Range` and resolved `Val.Unknown`, silently SUPPRESSING any rule that read it (overlap, `StartOfDateRange`/`EndOfDateRange`) where the kernel evaluates. This was recorded as IG58 (not blind-fixed) because the kernel's per-side completion was unpinned by any differential.

**The kernel mechanism (source, `kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime`).** `RuntimeController.datesAndFormatsToDateRange` completes the two endpoints by OPPOSITE interval rules — `DateUtil.createDateFromDateFragment(dates[0], INTERVAL_START, …)` for the start, `INTERVAL_END` for the end — and `DateAndFormat.addMonthAndDay(intervalSpec)` fills a missing MONTH → `01` (start) / `12` (end) and a missing DAY → `01` (start) / the completed month's LAST day (end, `getLastDayOfMonth`, leap-aware); a missing YEAR → the base year on both. So `2024/2025` (format `yyyy`) reads as the widest interval `2024-01-01 .. 2025-12-31`. A plain fragment DATE field always completes `INTERVAL_START` (earliest) — only a range's END side gets the latest-date completion; this is the runtime analog of the pinned `EndOfDateRange(BaseYear)` = 31.12 rule. Read the source to learn the mechanism; the Kotlin is original.

**The fix.** `DateMath.parseRangeToIso` gained a `baseYear` and completes each endpoint via `buildRangeEndpoint(values, comps, baseYear, end)` — missing month → `01`/`12`, missing day → `01`/last-day, missing year → base year; `Document.resolveValue(DATE_RANGE)` threads the model's base year. `buildIso` (the single-date path, `parseDateToIso`) is unchanged, and a full-format range (`dd.MM.yyyy`) completes to itself on both sides, so this is a no-op there — the FORMAL check (its own per-side parse, IF112) is untouched. Sub-year extraction (`Month/DayFromDate`) over a `yyyy` range is authoring-rejected by the kernel's granularity gate, so the sub-year 01-01/12-31 completion is observable only through overlap with a full-format range at the year's far ends — the differential pins it that way.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.FragmentRangeEvalDiffTest` (kernel differential: per-side `YearFromDate` + overlap with a late-December / early-January `dd.MM.yyyy` window pinning end→12-31 & start→01-01 + two-year-range overlap, both polarities) + multiplatform `interpreter.FragmentRangeEvalTest` (JVM + JS). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §6 supplement. Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF119 — a bare `[Name]` ref resolves DECLARING-GROUP-FIRST, then a model-wide UNIQUE field short name (FIXED 2026-07-11, graduated from IG57)

The parser (`parse/Parser.kt`) absolutized a bare relative single-segment ref (`[Name]` / a bare `Name` arg) against the rule's group unconditionally — `base/Name`. When `Name` was a field in ANOTHER group (a bare cross-group short name — legal when the model sets `fieldRefByShortNameAllowed`, and RETAINED in the serialized condition the interpreter loads), it mis-bound to a nonexistent `base/Name` and failed LOUDLY at eval ("no kind declared for …" — never a silent wrong value). This was recorded as IG57 pending kernel research.

**The kernel mechanism (source, `CheckIdNameRefImpl.getEntityFromConditionText`, `kernel-core-parser`).** A bare ref resolves declaring-group-FIRST: build `<declaringGroup>/<name>`, look it up; ONLY if that misses AND the ref is not absolute AND `modelConfig.fieldRefByShortNameAllowed` is on, search `shortName2FullName(name)` over FIELDS ONLY — >1 → `MVK_FIELDNAME_NOT_UNIQUE` (never picks one), 1 → resolve, 0 → `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY`. So the flag gates only the cross-group fallback; a same-group ref resolves regardless. Read the source to learn the order; the Kotlin is original. KERNEL-FINDINGS §10.

**The fix.** The parser gained a `RefResolver` seam (default: the pre-fix `base/name`; `parseCondition`/`parseExpression` take it, so the direct parse tests are unchanged). `ParsedModel` builds a model-aware resolver from the `ModelDef`: `base/name` used as-is when it is a declared entity (`ModelDef.entityPaths` — fields + their ancestor groups + repeatable + rule/computation groups, giving the declaring-group precedence); else, iff `fieldRefByShortNameAllowed`, resolve to a UNIQUE `fieldsByShortName[name]` (fields only, matching the kernel's index); else/ambiguous/absent stays `base/name` (the loud failure — an ambiguous model is a kernel reject, so no valid model reaches here with one, and the interpreter never silently picks). `ModelLoader` reads the flag from `content.modelConfig` (default false, matching the kernel). Only a BARE relative single-segment unstarred ref reaches the resolver — an absolute, `..`-relative, multi-segment, or starred ref is absolutized structurally, matching the kernel's fallback gate.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ShortNameRefDiffTest` (kernel differential: a cross-group `[PostalCode]`/`FieldFilled(PostalCode)` on `order.dm.json`, both engines loaded from one model JSON via `ModelLoader`, fires/does-not-fire on presence + value) + `validate.laws.PathLawsTest` (kernel accept for a unique cross-group ref, `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` with the flag off, `MVK_FIELDNAME_NOT_UNIQUE` for an ambiguous one) + multiplatform `interpreter.ShortNameRefTest` (resolution + the loud flag-off/ambiguous guards). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §10 supplement. Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF120 — the model-declared legal charset (`modelConfig.supportedCharacters`) is wired into the loader, taking precedence over the consumer charset SPI (FIXED 2026-07-11, graduated from IG61)

The interpreter's `LegalCharset` modeled only the kernel's project-level restricted charset as a **consumer-supplied SPI** (default BMP, IF43), and its KDoc actively claimed "a DM-JSON model carries no charset." But the DM-JSON **model-config** slot `content.modelConfig.supportedCharacters` IS a real runtime charset the kernel enforces (`ZeichenNichtImZeichensatz` — KERNEL-FINDINGS "`modelConfig.supportedCharacters` is enforced at runtime"), and no interpreter loader read it. So a model *declaring* the slot diverged: the differential's kernel side fired, the interpreter stayed silent (it only honored the SPI). Recorded as IG61 pending the differential + the composition research.

**The kernel mechanism (source, pinned 2026-07-11; combined-sequence correction 2026-07-19).** The slot is NOT a second charset composed with the §3 project charset—it IS that charset: `ExternalDocumentModelService.createCodeGeneratorParameters` passes `getSupportedCharacters()` into `CodeGeneratorParameters`, codegen bakes it into the generated meta-model's `getLegalCodepoints()`, and `FormalChecker`/`LegalCharTester` performs the one check. In the dynamic-codegen path the slot is the sole source—`DocumentProcessingConfig` carries no charset override. `CharsetUtil.parse` classifies an entry as a range iff `length()==3 && charAt(1)=='-'`, as an atomic singleton iff `length()==1`, and otherwise as a legal combined grapheme/sequence after validation; `LegalCharTester` matches those combined entries atomically through a trie. Empty means BMP; surrogate-bearing definitions are invalid. The earlier singleton/range-only summary was incomplete. Read the source to learn the flow; the Kotlin remains original. KERNEL-FINDINGS §3.

**The shipped fix and its corrected boundary.** `LegalCharset.ofSupportedCharacters(entries)` parses the list into the interpreter charset; `ModelLoader` reads `content.modelConfig.supportedCharacters` into `ModelDef.legalCharset`; `DmInterpreter` resolves the effective charset as `model.legalCharset ?: legalCharset` (model slot wins, SPI is the fallback). IF151 completes both halves of the combined-sequence contract: one shared construction boundary rejects every pinned-kernel-invalid definition before a trie exists, and the matcher stores each legal sequence atomically, including legal shared and terminal prefixes, so components are never admitted alone.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.SupportedCharactersDiffTest` (kernel differential, own-domain insurance: a policy `Ref` restricted to `A-Z`/`0-9`, both engines from one model JSON via `ModelLoader`, agree on the charset code@pointer for in-set/lower-case/punctuation/accented values) + multiplatform `interpreter.SupportedCharactersTest` (loader reads the slot; fires/does-not-fire; empty/absent → null → BMP). Kernel-only fires/does-not-fire stays `adapter.laws.SupportedCharactersLawsTest`. Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §3. Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF121 — message interpolation of an UNSPECIFIED `$field.value$`: `0` for a NUMBER, empty otherwise (FIXED 2026-07-12, graduated from IG64)

`renderMessage` substituted the empty string for *every* unspecified `$field.value$` token, but the kernel's scale-zero Number default is `0`. The first fix therefore selected `0` for `Kind.NUMBER` and `""` otherwise. **Corrected by IF201 / SPEC-2026-07-21-01:** that per-kind shortcut was only a narrow control, not the governing rule. Missing and caller-empty display values select the field format's exact default, including a Number's minimum fractional digits, while message display reads the raw immutable input rather than the normalized evaluation cache. The current modern `DocumentV2` route's default separator is `.`; comma-by-model-config was refuted. The original `UnspecifiedValueInterpolationDiffTest` remains the scale-zero/String control; `MessageValueDisplayPolicyDiffTest` and multiplatform `MessageDisplayPolicyTest` carry the complete admitted policy.

### IF122 — the `--kernel` eval flag is retired; the interpreter is the sole CLI runtime eval engine (SHIPPED 2026-07-12, Set V, graduated from IG67)

The drop-in certificate ([`INTERPRETER-DROPIN-CERTIFICATE.md`](INTERPRETER-DROPIN-CERTIFICATE.md), SPEC §8a) was owner-ratified as green-or-documented-equivalent, so the terminal step landed: the user-facing `--kernel` option was removed from `model eval` / `rule eval` / `model compute`. Evaluation is now interpreter-only on every target—JVM and native alike—with no default-vs-`--kernel` engine switch. The 2026-07-19 audit corrected the later “fully GREEN” shorthand and found additional legal-input, platform, mutation-lifecycle, and certificate-coverage gaps; the then-open G1′ included-reference class subsequently closed as IF146. The authoritative current inventory is the live [`INTERPRETER-GAPS.md`](INTERPRETER-GAPS.md) ledger; the certificate records the historical retirement decision and its exercised evidence, not a whole-domain proof.

**Scope A only — the EVAL role.** Only the kernel's *evaluation* path is retired. The kernel stays bundled for its *consistency / MVK acceptance* role (`rule check` / `model check`, `checkConsistency` / `RuleValidator`, the F4 guarantee) — reproducing that oracle clean-room ("Scope B") is a separate, deferred question, never folded in.

**Verification stays kernel-backed — via the adapter API, not a CLI flag.** The CLI-level interpreter-vs-kernel differential (the parity/verb tests) now reaches the kernel through the `KernelAdapter` API directly (the test-only `KernelEval` harness in `:cli` test source, mirroring how `:adapter`'s `*DiffTest`s do it), so the differential is preserved with **zero kernel-eval code in production `:cli`**. The kernel remains the `:adapter` differential oracle — verification is never kernel-free.

**Residuals are independent of the historical retirement.** The 2026-07-19 source/certificate audit found additional legal-input, platform, preparation, API-safety, mutation, and evidence gaps beyond the then-known G1′/JS-expander/timezone residuals. None reverses the fact that the flag was retired; none may be omitted from a current exactness report. [INTERPRETER-GAPS.md](INTERPRETER-GAPS.md) is authoritative rather than this historical finding.

**Mechanics.** `Cli.NATIVE_REMOVED_OPTIONS` + `removeNativeUnavailableOptions` (which existed solely to strip `--kernel` in native) were deleted; `CustomSupportOptions.kernelConflict` went with the flag; the eval commands lost their `useKernel` branches (interpreter path only). *Locked:* the existing parity estate — `cli.EvalDocVerbTest` / `ComputeDocVerbTest` / `rule.RuleTestVerbTest` / `EvalUnknownRuleRefusalTest` (kernel leg via `KernelEval`) + `cli.IncludeEvalDefaultPathTest` (the G1 differential: default path == kernel) + `cli.NativeImageRuntimeVerbsTest` (the retired flag is rejected on every profile).

### IF123 — a final EMPTY String computation result stores NO value (the kernel's second root-store decision) (FIXED 2026-07-15, graduated from IG69; feedback F10)

The interpreter correctly substituted `""` for clean empty operands INSIDE a concatenation (IF81) but then **stored** an all-empty final result as `VALUE ""` — where kernel 30.8.1 takes a second decision at the root store: `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert(VkString…)` returns before the check-and-store on an empty value (*"for empty values, do like no calculation was done"*; `VkString.hasValue()` = non-empty text — shared runtime code both strategies' generated alternatives call). Found by the a12-kernel-lean feedback (F10) with a retained 13-case receipt (`string-computation-2026-07-15`): both kernel strategies unanimous on all 13, the interpreter split on exactly the 3 all-empty-concat cases (stale target → kernel `CLEARED` vs interp `VALUE ""`; absent target → kernel silent vs interp `VALUE ""`). **Why the nets missed it:** the real-corpus computations produce no final-empty concat, and the compute fuzz generated NUMBER chains only — a number renders empty-as-0, never `""` — so the shape was structurally ungenerable. Kernel mechanism + scope ruling (String-only in effect; date/date-range no-value is a null date = the already-CLEARED unevaluated op; a number has no empty arm): KERNEL-FINDINGS §11 "the root store takes a SECOND decision".

**The fix — at the store boundary, expression evaluation untouched.** `ComputationEngine.toComputed` converts a final empty rendered TEXT to the no-value CLEARED decision *before* the target format check (the kernel never checks a value it does not store — an empty result into a `minLength` target CLEARS, never ERRORED), and `computeInstances`' cascade bookkeeping now keys on the **outcome** instead of the raw `Val`: an empty result is a not-decided **tentative clear** — dependents read the target EMPTY, a later computation for the same cell can still win (the IF87 fall-through), exactly the kernel state of "nothing entered the calc cache." The kernel delta granularity applies afterward (the existing `kernelComputeGranularity` projection: a stale-filled target reports CLEARED, an absent one reports nothing — the receipt's shape). ERRORED/poison stay their own outcomes (checked before the empty gate can see them; IF85 unchanged).

*Locked (the class, not the 3 cases):* `adapter.laws.EmptyConcatStoreDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter raw **and** projected) over filled+empty, empty+nonempty-literal, all-empty-with-stale-target, all-empty-with-absent-target (content-bearing + empty row), and the minLength gate-order arm — + multiplatform `interpreter … EmptyStringComputationStoreTest` (JVM+JS: the store decision, the fall-through, the cascade EMPTY read, gate order, ERRORED preservation) + the new **string-concat compute-fuzz family** `ComputationFuzz.stringModel` in `ComputationFuzzDiffTest` (often-empty operands, rare literals, stale-seeded computed targets — mutation-checked: disabling the empty gate diverges on 17/200 (model, doc) pairs). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF124 — CRLF is normalized to LF at eval ingestion (format measure, rule reads, compute reads) (FIXED 2026-07-15, graduated from IG70; feedback F12)

F12 flagged a source-level candidate: the kernel normalizes CRLF→LF (`RuntimeUtils.normalizeLinebreaks` — the `\r\n` pair only, a lone CR stays) *before* the String min/max length clauses, while our `stringConstraintError` measured the raw text — a permitted `"\r\n"` near a one-unit boundary would measure 1 (kernel) vs 2 (us). The probe (the IG57/IG58 fix-or-refute mold, focused paired arms, both kernel strategies) **confirmed the divergence and found the mechanism BROADER than the F12 hypothesis**: the normalization is not measure-local. `CheckCommand` (the formal-check stage of every run) feeds the eval caches `checkAndConvertFieldValue.normalizedValue()`, so **every evaluation-side read** of a valid string sees `\n` — the format measure (length AND pattern, which follows the normalization inside `validiereFormat`), **rule reads** (`Length` over the permitted CRLF reads a REAL normalized 5 — `> 5` silent, `> 4` fires, the positive discriminator), and **computation reads** (a bare copy *stores* `"AB\nCD"`). A `noValueValidation` field turned out to be OUT of this class — its rule read never surfaces the stored text (the raw-type read semantics: value ops `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`-rejected, `Length(f) > c` the only authorable form and unconditionally non-evaluable at runtime) — a separate, pre-existing divergence opened as IG71 during the review of this fix (since closed: IF125 — the shape is an eliminated max-length declaration). Two adjacent clause-order facts fell out of the same probe: the line-break gate runs on the RAW text before everything else, and it **covers ENUM fields** (their kernel format definition hard-codes `lineBreakAllowed = false`) — a break-carrying out-of-domain enum value is `stringMitNewline`, where we reported `stringFalschesMuster`. Kernel mechanism: KERNEL-FINDINGS §3 "CRLF is normalized to LF at EVAL INGESTION".

**The fix — one normalization at the Document's eval-index ingestion + the clause reorder.** Value-bearing placements are CRLF-normalized once at index build (the exact twin of the kernel's cache fill): the format measure, rule operands, and compute operands read normalized text, while the consumer's raw placements stay untouched (`copy()` reproduces them verbatim, and the compute delta projection keeps comparing against the raw seed — matching the kernel's change-vs-input-document equality, which likewise sees a normalized copy of a raw CRLF input as a CHANGE). `stringConstraintError`'s newline gate moved before the enum-domain arm (kernel clause order); the gate itself is normalization-insensitive (`\r\n → \n` preserves "contains a break", a lone CR is untouched), so checking it on the normalized text is exact. **IF201 separates the one sibling consumer that must not use this cache:** `$field.value$` message interpolation reads `Placement.raw`, so it retains the input CRLF while evaluation continues to observe LF.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.CrlfLengthNormalizationDiffTest` — 12 tri-check arms (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter): the min/max boundary pair around the normalized length, LF + lone-CR controls, the no-breaks clause-order control, the pattern-sees-normalized arm (`AB\sCD`; a literal-break pattern is model-rejected), the enum newline-before-domain arm, the nvv read, the `Length`-rule read, and the compute copy (stored value witnesses the read form) — + multiplatform `interpreter … CrlfNormalizationTest` (JVM+JS). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF125 — a `Length(nvv) > c` rule is ELIMINATED (the kernel's disguised max-length declaration); no other value read of a raw-type field exists (FIXED 2026-07-15, Set AA, graduated from IG71)

IG71 (opened at the Set Y review gate) recorded that our interpreter evaluated `Length(nvv) > c` from the stored text while the kernel stayed silent. The Set AA research found the mechanism is NOT an np value state: a `noValueValidation` STRING field (an `IStringType`-only property — no other kind can be nvv) is a **raw-type** whose value is never interned for evaluation (`CheckCommand` caches `ValidationCache.PSEUDO_VALUE`), and the kernel closes **every** value window at the model tier — value operations/lists/computation operands are `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`, any `Length` use except the whole-condition `Length(f) > <constant>` (or mirrored `<constant> < Length(f)`, strict GT/LT only) is `MVK_INVALID_LENGTH_OF_RAW_TYPE` (computation operations AND preconditions reject it too), and `$field.value$` interpolation is `INVALID_NO_VALUE_VALIDATION`. **The one accepted shape is not a rule at runtime:** `ValidationCodeGenerator.handleLengthOfAllowedValueValidationRule` ELIMINATES it in both strategies and lifts its `(field, c)` into the generated meta-model's max length (forms/host metadata — the runtime format check still short-circuits on nvv, IF29). Presence semantics over the same cell are ordinary (the pseudo-interned cell is content). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §3 "raw-type (`noValueValidation`) fields".

**The fix — mechanism-exact rule elimination, no new value state.** `DmInterpreter.rowOutcomes` returns no rows for the eliminated shape (`eliminatedNvvMetaLengthRule`: the whole parsed condition is a GT/LT-mirrored `Cmp` of `Length(field)` vs a numeric literal, with the field's declared constraints carrying `noValueValidation`), so `validateFull`/`validatePart` never fire it and `testRule` reports PASSED — exactly a rule that does not exist in the generated validation. On kernel-legal inputs every other nvv value read is unreachable (the MVK gates above), so `Val`/`resolveValue` are untouched — per the representation rule, an np state would have modeled constructs no legal model can contain. (The interpreter still evaluates such reads on MVK-illegal models fed directly to the library — out of the rule-#1 contract, which is kernel-legal inputs; the kernel-backed `rule check` is the MVK gate.)

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.NvvRawTypeDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter): the eliminated shape at `> 5`/`> -1`/mirrored `-1 <`, presence, quantifiers (`AllFieldsFilled`/`NoFieldFilled`), a `NumberOfFilledFields` count over nvv cells in a repeatable group, and partial validation (elimination is mode-independent) — + `validate.laws.NvvRawTypeLawsTest` (accept: the two mirror forms + presence; reject: value compare, `Length` in a larger condition, the `>=` precision arm, `FieldValuesNotUnique`, computation copy/`Length`/precondition) + multiplatform `interpreter … NvvEliminatedLengthRuleTest` (JVM+JS, incl. the plain-field control and the `testRule` PASSED verdict) + the **`ModelFuzz.nvvModel` fuzz family** (random legal nvv rules — eliminated shapes must stay silent, presence/count shapes must fire; mutation-checked: the gate disabled diverges on 26/200 (model, doc) pairs; no prior net could generate an nvv field). The `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`/`MVK_INVALID_LENGTH_OF_RAW_TYPE` correctives ride the diagnostic enricher (`StructuralKernelFix`, locked `DiagnosticEnricherTest`) and the `Length` catalog gotcha (N7-validated). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF126 — `Document.cellStateAt` exposes ABSENT vs PRESENT-EMPTY vs PRESENT-VALUE(raw) on read-back (SHIPPED 2026-07-17, Set AB, graduated from IG72)

IG72 recorded that `DmInterpreter.applyComputations` returns a `Document` a consumer cannot fully inspect: the public surface exposed only `valuedFields()` and `filledAt` (value-or-not), so an ABSENT cell and a PRESENT-BUT-VALUELESS one were indistinguishable on read-back. The apply-then-inspect loop (`validate(applyComputations(doc))`) is a natural consumer loop, so the missing read was a real library gap.

**The API — a small public read, kernel-apply-contract exact.** `Document.cellStateAt(path, reps): StoredCellState` — a sealed type (`Absent` / `PresentEmpty` / `PresentValue(raw)`, exhaustive `when`; illegal states unrepresentable) in `commonMain` (JVM + JS). The states mirror what the kernel's `IDocumentComputationResult.applyTo(DocumentV2)` leaves per cell — the per-result-kind table is KERNEL-FINDINGS §11 "the post-application cell STATE" (a VALUE creates/replaces; cleared/errored **empty a present cell but never create one**; untouched cells keep their placement state). Two deliberate contract points: `raw` is the **stored** text as placed/computed — the eval-side CRLF→LF normalization (IF124) does not leak into the read (the internal `Placement` now carries the pre-normalization `raw` alongside the eval `value`; no eval read changed) — and a `""` value reads `PresentEmpty` (the kernel's `DocumentUtils.valueExists` no-value notion; every supported ingestion path already maps `""` to an empty placement).

**Found and fixed in the same blast radius: the applied copy dropped `fieldConstraints`.** `Document.emptyCopy()` — the config-threading seam `withComputed` rebuilds through — omitted the constructor's 13th parameter, exactly the silently-dropped-trailing-map trap `newDocument`'s own KDoc warns about. Observable divergence (differentially red before the fix): on the applied document a constraint-violating stored value (e.g. `"5"` under `minFractionalDigits 2`) resolved as VALID on the Document-gated resolution paths (aggregate cells, IF20/IF84), so `validate(applyComputations(doc))` fired an aggregate rule the kernel's form-engine flow suppresses. One-line fix (pass `fieldConstraints`); the apply path now reproduces the kernel's post-apply validation on constraint-carrying documents.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.AppliedCellStateDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter `cellStateAt`) over all nine post-application shapes (VALUE into absent/stale/typed-equal; cleared stale → present-empty; computed-nothing over empty-placed → present-empty and over absent → absent; ERRORED over stale/empty-placed → present-empty and over **absent → absent**, the kernel's guarded branch) + the untouched-CRLF raw read + `theAppliedCopyKeepsDeclaredConstraints` (suppressed-after-apply, with the live-rule control) — + multiplatform `interpreter … CellStateAtTest` (JVM+JS: the plain reads incl. `""`→PresentEmpty and the group-path→Absent edge, the post-apply states, apply-immutability) + `interpreter … AppliedCopyConstraintsTest` (JVM+JS, red/green-proven).

### IF127 — the STAR-ONLY iteration scope: a rule with no per-row repeatable ref scopes at the starred refs' BINDING levels, else fires ONCE root-content-gated (FIXED 2026-07-18, Set SK2, graduated from IG73)

IG73 recorded the G42 investigation's residual `validate` divergence: a rule whose declared group IS a repeatable group and whose condition references ONLY a starred aggregate over that same group — `Sum(/Order/Items*/Count) > -1`, error field `/Order/Items/Count` — fired per Items row in the interpreter (3 messages) where the kernel fires ONCE, anchored at the first row. Root cause: `IterationScopes.iterationContexts` excluded starred refs from scope determination (correct — a star collapses) but then fell back to the DECLARING group, making iteration placement-driven exactly where the kernel is reference-driven. The class was wider than the reported shape: the same fallback over-fired a star-only rule at a repeatable group that is NOT the starred group (3 duplicate messages at one pointer), mis-scoped a deep star (`Items/Lines*` iterated per Lines row instead of per Items row), and even hit star-FREE rules whose only refs are non-repeatable (a rule at repeatable `Items` referencing only a root field: 3 duplicates vs the kernel's 1).

**The kernel law (source + 16-arm tri-differential; KERNEL-FINDINGS §9 supplement "a STAR-ONLY condition scopes at the star's BINDING levels").** Iteration is reference-driven per error-entity context level (`RuleIterationLimitCreator` × `AnalyseService.isIteration`): a star is a WILDCARD — never ITERATION — so only the unindexed repeatable levels STRICTLY ABOVE the first `*`-marked group (the star's BINDING levels, IF93/IF94) drive per-row iteration; with no repeatable reference level anywhere the rule evaluates exactly ONCE, content-gated at the ROOT instance, every unreferenced repeatable level pinned to row 1 — phantom anchors included (the kernel fires at an uninstantiated `Fees[1]`).

**The fix (one mechanism, three tiers).** `iterationContexts` now derives scope as: (1) the deepest repeatable ancestor-or-self of a non-starred ref (unchanged — IF106); (2) else the deepest repeatable BINDING level across the condition's stars (`starRefs`/`exprStars` in `AstRefs` — not descending into `Having`, matching the kernel's filter-condition exclusion — with the binding prefix from `Expr.Star.bindDepth`, the same cap `Document.groupScopePrefix` uses); (3) else ONE context at the error path's root group — whose existing `evaluated` flag is precisely the kernel's root content gate, and whose `repsFor` pins deeper levels to row 1 (the phantom anchor). Tier 3 SUBSUMES the IF103 error-parent re-anchor (deleted): the root context covers the cross-root once-fire with the same observable, and the IF103 locks stay green. Candidate-mode parity is unaffected by construction (`EvalCandidatePersistedParityTest.starAggregateCandidateMatchesPersisted` is equality-based — green at 3-vs-3 before, 1-vs-1 after). 0 corpus footprint (both real corpora sweep at 0 divergences nice + hostile, before and after).

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.StarOnlyIterationScopeDiffTest` — tri-check (groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter), 16 arms: the reported own-group shape (once + zero-rows silence + root-content phantom + false-control), the other-group shape (once despite a repeatable declaring group + phantom at a missing anchor row + empty-declaring-group fire), multi-star collapse, deep-star binding-level iteration (+ phantom on a Lines-less row + placement-irrelevance + relative form + mixed non-repeatable-ref-plus-star), the once-gate root-vs-collapse discrimination pair, and the star-free duplicate-collapse arm — + multiplatform `interpreter … StarOnlyIterationScopeTest` (JVM + JS). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF128 — the model time zone is a modeled axis: Europe/Berlin datetime arithmetic is DST-exact for the 1996+ regime (SHIPPED 2026-07-18, graduated from IG62; historical residual promoted to IG83)

**Superseded detail (2026-07-20):** IF186 replaces the historical “zone-independent `Δ wall-seconds / 86 400`” day-difference mechanism below. IF155/IF165 already superseded the modern-only Berlin scope. This section remains the point-in-time IF128 landing record.

The kernel applies the model's configured time zone to every DATE/DATE_TIME parse (`ValidationDateParser` — `SimpleDateFormat(format, Locale.US)`, `setTimeZone(model zone)`, `setLenient(false)`) and evaluates the datetime operator family on the resulting `VkDate` INSTANTS, so under a `Europe/Berlin` model wall clock and instants diverge across the DST transitions. The interpreter previously computed on naive 86 400 s/day epoch seconds — exact for `UTC` (the DM-JSON default) but divergent for a Berlin model across a transition (the accepted certificate caveat G2). Now modeled, clean-room:

- **`ModelTimeZone`** (`UTC` / `EUROPE_BERLIN`): `ModelLoader` reads `content.modelConfig.timeZone` (absent/`UTC`/`GMT` → UTC — the kernel's own GMT fallback; anything else is REFUSED LOUD, since silently evaluating a zone we don't model would be wrong); threaded `ModelDef` → `EvalConfig` → `Interpreter`, and to `Document`/the formal check via `newDocument()`.
- **The zone math lives in `DateMath`** (pure `commonMain` — no `java.time`, rides JVM + JS): the current EU rule (CEST between the last Sundays of March and October, 01:00Z transitions; **pre-1996 Berlin datetimes are an explicit residual** — older tzdb rules only the kernel knows), `localToInstant` with the kernel's PARSE resolution (ambiguous fall-back hour → STANDARD time; the nonexistent spring-gap hour → null), `instantToLocal`, and the `AddDays` landing rule.
- **The spring gap is the formal error `datumFormatFalsch`** (probe-verified — the kernel's non-lenient round-trip rejects it): `typeFormatError(DATE_TIME)` flags it, so the value resolves `Val.Unknown` and suppresses, exactly like any malformed datetime.
- **Instant evaluation is STRUCTURAL** (`dateTimeInstant` in `ExprEval`): `DifferenceIn{Hours,Minutes,Seconds}`, `Add{Hours,Minutes,Seconds}`, and datetime comparison under a non-UTC zone derive operand instants by walking `Add*` chains — the kernel's `VkDate` identity, where a chained result landing in the ambiguous hour is NOT the instant its equal-looking rendering re-parses to. Under UTC every function is the identity, so the historical arithmetic (and the corpus) is bit-for-bit unchanged.
- **Wall-day arithmetic corrected in the same family (zone-independent kernel truths the probes exposed):** `DifferenceInDays` is a TIME-OF-DAY-AWARE whole-wall-day count (Δ wall-seconds / 86 400, truncated toward zero — `23:00 → 01:00` is 0 days; the interpreter's old date-part subtraction was a live silent divergence for datetime operands, mixed `(datetime, date)` pairs included), and `AddDays` over a DATE_TIME (legal in the kernel; the old interpreter yielded `Unknown` — a silent suppression divergence) is time-preserving with the NO-OVERSHOOT landing rule for the special hours (forward → daylight/earlier instant, backward → standard/later; all four combinations kernel-probed). `DifferenceIn{Months,Years}`/`AddMonths`/`AddYears` reject DATE_TIME operands at MVK, `DifferenceIn{Hours,…}` reject plain DATE — unreachable, unchanged.
- **The CLI loader rode along:** `InterpreterModelLoader.document` hand-listed the `Document` constructor and had silently dropped `baseYear` + `fieldConstraints` (exactly the trap `newDocument()`'s KDoc names) — switched to `newDocument()`, which also threads the zone.

This closed the certificate's exercised modern-Berlin **G2** regime, not the timezone axis as a whole. The 2026-07-19 source audit broadened [IG83](INTERPRETER-GAPS.md): kernel validation accepts the wider Java `TimeZone` id domain (unknown ids that collapse to GMT are rejected; literal GMT is valid), while `ModelTimeZone` accepts only UTC/GMT/Berlin, and its Berlin rules start in 1996. Pre-1996 Berlin is one discriminator, not the whole gap. The kernel's own JS/TS runtime may resolve edge hours differently from its Java strategies; on a legal split the ratified oracle (IF136) makes Groovy-dynamic normative. Kernel truths: KERNEL-FINDINGS §6 supplements.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.DstTimeZoneDiffTest` (instant diffs across both transitions, ambiguous→standard, gap formal error + suppression, chained-Add instant identity, UTC control, extraction control) + `adapter.laws.WallDayArithmeticDiffTest` (time-of-day-aware day counts incl. reverse truncation + mixed operands, time-preserving `AddDays`, both gap-landing directions) — kernel differentials, red-first (10 arms kernel-true/interpreter-false before the fix) — + multiplatform `interpreter … ModelTimeZoneTest` (loader mapping incl. the loud refusal, and the full Berlin semantics kernel-free, JVM + JS).

*Perf (2026-07-18, historical state at this finding):* `berlinOffsetSeconds` (`DateMath`) is on the hot path for every DATE/DATE_TIME conversion under a non-UTC model, yet it originally derived the year and DST boundaries via string round-trips. Replacing those with pure-integer civil-calendar math cut an ad-hoc Berlin/UTC probe from ~2.1× to ~1.26×; the helpers are equivalence-locked across the four-digit-year civil-calendar domain by `DateMathCivilArithmeticTest` on JVM + JS. At the IF128 landing this remained probe-only and the perf harness still dropped the model zone. IF131 subsequently fixed complete `DocumentConfig` propagation, IF132 added the committed Berlin + UTC scale scenarios and guards, and IF172 supplied the structured fair generated-Java evidence lane. Owner policy settled 2026-07-21: no candidate ratio is promoted now.

### IF129 — `computeField(fieldPath, doc): List<Computed>`, the single-computation library primitive (SHIPPED 2026-07-18, graduated from IG74)

`DmInterpreter` now exposes the single-*computation* twin of `testRule` — "run this computed field like a function" — closing the named module goal ("single-rule / **single-computation** eval", `interpreter/CLAUDE.md`). Before, computing one field meant `compute(doc).first { it.path == target }`: correct but it evaluated *every* sibling computation.

- **The mechanism is prune-and-reuse, not new semantics.** `computeField` runs the SAME cascade engine as `compute` (the extracted `ComputationEngine.computeOver(doc, comps)` — overlay / poison / cleared, first-non-empty-wins IF87, the parallel join IF96) over a **pruned** computation list: the target's transitive computed-dependency **closure**, in dependency order. The closure comes from the shared topo-DFS `orderedFrom(roots)` (roots = every target → the whole-model order; root = one field → its upstream closure); `computeField` then projects `outcomeByCell` to the target field's own instances.
- **The governing invariant: `computeField(t, doc) == compute(doc).filter { it.path == t }`** — same instances, order, per-row indices, outcome bucket. Pruning to the UPSTREAM closure is behaviour-preserving because an unrelated computation cannot feed the target (downstream dependents are irrelevant to the target's value; a non-computed operand is just read). This is the cheap, kernel-free regression net; the kernel agreement rides on the whole-document `compute()` differential (already both-strategies-locked), projected per field.
- **Return shape corrected the ledger.** IG74 wrote `: Computed` (singular); the shipped signature is **`List<Computed>`** — a computed field in a **repeatable** group has one outcome per row, exactly why `compute()` returns a list, so the singular could not represent the common per-row case (owner-decided). A non-computed `fieldPath` (no computation targets it) throws `IllegalArgumentException` — a non-computed field has no outcome, mirroring `testRule`'s "no such rule" fail-fast.
- **Library-only.** A CLI surface (a verb / `--field` option) trips the earns-a-verb + conformance-matrix discipline (CLI-SPEC §6) and is scoped separately; no dmtool change. The kernel has no single-computation entry either (`IDocumentRtService.compute` is whole-document) — irrelevant since kernel eval retired (Set V) and the capability lives in `:interpreter`.

*Locked:* multiplatform `interpreter … ComputeFieldInvariantTest` (JVM + JS — the `computeField == compute`-projected invariant across a repeated target, a depth-2 cascade, poison propagation, empty-operand clearing, and the non-computed-field throw) + `adapter.laws.ComputeFieldDiffTest` (the compute differential: `computeField` == the real kernel's `compute()` projected per field, over clean / poison / empty scenarios).

### IF130 — low startup, high performance, and low RELATIVE footprint are separate regimes; bounded caches are an intended optimization (DECIDED 2026-07-18; numerical status corrected 2026-07-19)

The durable decision separates three regimes that earlier performance prose mixed together: **first-answer latency** (fresh process, engine construction and lazy initialization included), **warm throughput/scaling** (prepared engines, correctness checked before timing, with generated Java as the fair relative baseline), and **relative footprint** (dependency/artifact size, cache ownership and plateau, retained-memory/allocation growth, and native-vs-JVM delivery). [INTERPRETER-SPEC.md](INTERPRETER-SPEC.md) owns the product contract and [INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md](INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md) the method/evidence. The exact numbers introduced with this finding were not backed by an earlier owner decision or protocol-compliant series; they are informative candidate budgets until per-number certified (IF137), not settled acceptance bars.

**“Low footprint” does not mean minimum memory or an arbitrary small heap.** Parsed-AST caches, document indices, hash maps, row-context memoization, and other bounded memory-intensive structures are allowed and advised when they reduce repeated work, improve the complexity class, or materially improve startup/throughput. The invariant is that cached state has a clear owner/invalidation boundary, reaches a stable warm plateau, grows in its declared space class, and earns regressions against the previous release or equivalent kernel-backed evaluator. The proposed 10%/20% review triggers belong to the numerical decision (IF137), not this timeless principle. The invalidation premise is now upheld (IF142 closed the one violation). Exact kernel parity remains prior to every NFR.

The historical timings make several candidates plausible but certify none: warm growth used best minima rather than the later ratified median estimator, startup samples excluded required work, and the 33/119 MB delivery pair predates equivalent interpreter capability. The numerical/product decision is ratified (IF137); IF172 supplies the executable performance evidence lane and IF173 the same-collector delivery comparison. No candidate may be reported as a green release gate without its six-point certification and owner ratification.

### IF131 — `DocumentConfig`: the evaluator configuration is ONE immutable value, so every `Document` reconstruction preserves it wholesale (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, graduated from IG75)

The 14 evaluator-configuration inputs a `Document` carries (kinds / reps / formats / scale / enum / precision / base-year / constraint / **time-zone**) are now a single immutable `DocumentConfig`. `Document`'s only constructors are `Document(DocumentConfig)` and a kinds-only convenience `Document(kinds)`; the former 14-argument positional constructor is **removed**. This makes the IG75 class of bug unrepresentable: a copy passes the one `config` object, so it cannot drop a slot.

- **The defect it fixes.** `Document.emptyCopy()` (behind `withComputed` → `applyComputations`) rebuilt positionally through only 13 of 14 arguments and silently dropped `timeZone` → `UTC`. So a `Europe/Berlin` document became UTC after compute→apply.
- **Reachable impact is narrow (the corrected scope, IG75 review 2026-07-19).** Ordinary evaluation and the emitted formal-error diagnostics read `EvalConfig.timeZone` / `AutoChecks.formalErrors` — both from the (unaffected) `ModelDef` — so `validate` is bit-identical before/after `applyComputations` (probe-verified: the spring-forward `DifferenceInHours` and the spring-gap `datumFormatFalsch` both agree). The Document's `timeZone` feeds only `Document.formallyInvalid`/`cellState` (the not-check-relevant gate + the IF85 compute-poison trigger), so the only reachable divergence is datetime VALUE-resolution inside that gate on an applied document — an aggregate/fill over a spring-gap DATE_TIME, narrow and not exhibited by a simple validate. The **perf-harness sibling was the more material defect**: `InterpreterPerfEngine` (and `scenario.interpModel`) built a `Document` from six inputs, dropping constraints/zone/etc., so "real Document" perf scenarios ran semantically incomplete — now they build via `newDocument()`.
- **The migration.** ~85 call sites moved off the positional constructor: `Document(def.kinds, def.declaredReps, …)` → `def.newDocument()` (the blessed full-config path), and the handful of ad-hoc maps → `DocumentConfig(…)` with named arguments (conspicuous, never a silent positional drop). Partial construction is now impossible or explicit — the standing "make illegal states unrepresentable" rule applied to `Document`.

*Locked:* `interpreter … DocumentConfigPreservationTest` (JVM + JS—every slot preserved through `withComputed` by data-class equality, plus an explicit pin that a Europe/Berlin document stays Berlin); the full `:adapter` differential estate stays green across the migrated call sites. At this landing it unblocked the Document side of the then-open IG80; IF132 subsequently completed the model/perf side.

### IF132 — the Europe/Berlin DATE_TIME instant path is perf-gated: a committed scenario exercises `berlinOffsetSeconds` at scale (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, graduated from IG80)

The datetime-instant hot path — `berlinOffsetSeconds` (the civil-calendar offset `epochDayOfCivil`/`yearOfEpochDay`, IF128/IF131) run once per DATE/DATE_TIME conversion under a non-UTC model — is now exercised by a **committed** benchmark, not just a discarded hand-probe. Before this the whole `PerfCatalog` carried no DATE_TIME field, so the zone axis was inert and the IF128 optimization's win was free to silently regress.

- **The scenario.** `PerfCatalog.berlinDateTime()` — a per-row `DifferenceInHours(StartedAt, EndedAt) > 0` rule over `Europe/Berlin` DATE_TIME fields on a large repetition list — plus `utcDateTimeControl()` (the same shape with the zone inert). Values are mid-June (constant CEST, **non-DST-straddling**), so Berlin and UTC agree on the difference (interp == kernel holds bit-for-bit) while the offset path still runs per conversion.
- **Two committed guards.** `PerfGateTest.berlinDateTimeStaysSubQuadratic` is the interpreter-only declared-linear catastrophe guard under the shared paired-median estimator and loose 9× trip line; it is not a certified NFR-P1 result. The B9-berlin case in `PerfBenchTest.gatesEveryInventoryScenario` (derived from `PerfInventory`) is the Groovy comparison: interp == kernel, with the historical pre-correction run recording ratio ≈0.29 at N=3000 and Berlin/UTC delta ≈1.37×.
- **The performance handoff.** At the IF132 landing, the routine bench was Groovy-only and no fair kernel-Java B9 result existed. IF158 subsequently put the B9 Berlin/UTC pair in the closed declared inventory with its generated-Java leg, and IF172 added the pinned-runner NFR-P2 reporting lane. The candidate ratio remains informative; owner policy currently promotes no candidate number.

*Locked:* `adapter.perf.PerfGateTest.berlinDateTimeStaysSubQuadratic` (declared-linear growth) + the B9-berlin case in `adapter.perf.PerfBenchTest.gatesEveryInventoryScenario` (interp == kernel + ratio + Berlin/UTC delta; the case is registered in `PerfInventory`); the two civil-calendar helpers' correctness is separately locked by `interpreter … DateMathCivilArithmeticTest` (JVM + JS).

### IF133 — the perf comments describe the current architecture: parse-once, IF20 constraint parity, IF116-memoized indexed reads (SHIPPED 2026-07-19, graduated from IG78; follow-up cleanup closed by IF141)

The `:adapter/perf` comments had drifted behind three shipped changes; they now match the code:

- **Parse-once, not per-call re-parse.** `ParsedModel` parses each rule's condition (and each computation) to an AST **exactly once, cached** at `DmInterpreter` construction (`ruleCondAst` = `Map<RuleDef, Lazy<Cond>>`) — the "#31 per-call re-parse" cost is retired. The kernel's edge in the many-rules / batch regimes is now stated correctly: its compile-once codegen (JIT-compiled rules) vs the interpreter's per-call **tree-walk of the cached ASTs** (`PerfHarness`, `PerfCatalog` B6/`manyRules`, `KernelEdgeTest`).
- **IF20 constraint parity.** The interpreter auto-validates the same declared field-type constraints as the kernel (range/length/pattern/enum-domain — IF20, locked by `FieldConstraintFormalDiffTest`), so `PerfBounds.CAVEAT` dropped its stale "kernel also validates field-type constraints" fairness clause (the ratio's residual unfairness is only the excluded codegen + the Groovy tax).
- **IF116-memoized indexed reads.** `PerfCatalog.semanticIndexPerRow`'s comment framed the raw indexed read as *present-tense* O(rows²); IF116 memoizes the indexed-read column per (column, scope) at the document level, so it is O(rows) now — reworded to the historical + guarded framing (`PerfGateTest.semanticIndexStaysSubQuadratic` locks it).

**Three of IG78's cited items were verified NOT stale** (so they were left unchanged, recorded here to prevent re-investigation): `PerfScalingTest` already says the interpreter pays "a lazy index build + first parse" (one-time, correct); `EvalProfileTest` attributes cost to eval / `firedType` / `iterationContexts` / allocations (not re-parse or codegen); and `DmInterpreter.validatePart`'s KDoc has two distinct paragraphs, not a duplicated sentence. Comments-only—no behavior changed. A separate lifecycle defect—six Kotlin comment references that still named deleted IG80 rather than IF132—was subsequently closed by IF141.

### IF134 — the 2026-07-18 NFR commit was a useful regime split but not a ratified or certified numerical contract (DOCUMENTATION AUDIT 2026-07-19)

Commit `e594f594` (`docs(interpreter): define realistic non-functional requirements`, 2026-07-18 23:33 +0200) is the only commit that introduced or changed the NFR contract in `INTERPRETER-SPEC.md`; `git log -S` confirms every exact latency, ratio, footprint, exponent, and review-trigger number first appeared there.
