- **The guards.** `ScenarioIdentityFingerprintTest` proves that a changed model, legal-charset definition, execution locale, one generated value, semantic regime, or N fails under the old version/fingerprint, distinguishes computation VALUE/CLEARED/ERRORED identities, and locks structured and direct row labels. `PerfPreflightTest` locks the scenario locale received by the interpreter engine. `ScenarioInventoryTest` recomputes every stored fingerprint and expected cardinality from the current scenarios.
- **The closed declared inventory.** `BenchmarkScenario` enumerates the exact B1–B8 set, B9 Europe/Berlin plus its UTC control, and the tiered/parallel cases. An exhaustive `when` maps every declared enum entry to `InventoryDisposition.Included` or a non-blank reasoned exclusion; `PerfInventory.cases` is derived from that map. Adding an enum entry therefore requires a disposition at compile time, while the exact-set guard catches removal of B1, B8, B9, or UTC. This closes omissions inside the declared benchmark catalog; it does not claim that an unrelated ad-hoc helper is auto-discovered. B8 is the full combined scenario at N=500, the scale already known to support generated-Java codegen.
- **Java and targeted execution are live contracts.** `PerfBenchTest` passes each row's `fairJavaCapable` value to `PerfHarness.runScenario`, which performs complete interpreter/dynamic-Groovy/generated-Java agreement before reporting `ratioJava`. `scripts/perf.sh bench <scenario-id>` forwards an exact `perf.scenario` selector into the single inventory-derived test; an unmatched id fails rather than silently selecting nothing.
- **Governance resolution.** IF170–IF172 completed the remaining mechanics and executable evidence lane. Owner policy settled 2026-07-21: promote no candidate number now; keep `-PrunPerf` nightly/manual and the complete IF172 collector manual.

*Locked:* `adapter.perf.ScenarioIdentityFingerprintTest` + `adapter.perf.ScenarioInventoryTest`; `scripts/perf.sh bench B8-combined` and `scripts/perf.sh bench B9-berlin-datetime` exercise the generated-Java/target-selection lane.

### IF159 — comparator-only `$` correlations use ordered tallies under exact tri-engine and sub-quadratic guards (REVIEW-CORRECTED 2026-07-20; executable lane completed by IF172)

`OuterCorrelationPerfTest` first exposed `[Count] > [$Count]` with an all-equal/no-witness document, but its initial guard required growth above 6× and described the observed ≈15.7× as inherently pairwise. RV-2026-07-20-03 corrected the mechanism: one ordering comparison does not require testing every pair.

- **The pre-existing fair-model repair remains.** `OuterCorrelationPerfTest` and `GroupScopedFillPerfTest` build one high-repeatability minimal model (`Items.repeatable(200_000)`) and load it into all engines. This preserves IF159's fix for the old `order.dm.json` cap of 50, which had made the kernel skip over-limit rows while the interpreter evaluated them.
- **Closed indexed boundary.** `CorrelationJoin` returns a sealed keyed/ordered plan or no plan. Equality conjunctions retain hash buckets. Exactly one comparable plain inner field versus one `$` outer field with `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`, or applicable `!=` uses `OrderedCorrelation`: values sorted/grouped once per immutable `(group, scope, innerField, starPath)` view with prefix `(t, f, n)` sums, then binary-bound queries per outer row. Equality remains keyed. Same-kind NUMBER/STRING/DATE/TIME and UTC DATE_TIME are order-compatible; boolean/confirm/date-range inequality, non-UTC DATE_TIME, compound predicates/expression operands, and multi-star multi-row self-exclusion use the general nested fallback. The compute overlay owns the same per-step cache lifetime and bypasses it when the step writes a contributing column.
- **Semantics before speed.** `OrderedOuterCorrelationDiffTest` authors one typed `Rule` for every comparator × `AtLeastOneFieldFilled`/`NoFieldFilled` pair and tri-checks interpreter, dynamic Groovy, and generated Java over duplicates and boundary witnesses, all-equal/no-witness values, one-row physical self-exclusion, and malformed/empty 3VL. `CorrelationPlanDispatchTest` locks all six comparator strategies, operand reversal, and fallback cases. `DocumentMutationCacheInvalidationTest` proves mutation clears the ordered cache.
- **Exact curve.** The all-equal `>` N=400/N=1,600 documents pass full indexed signatures across all three engines before timing. The lower bound is gone; the only assertion is the shared `<9×` catastrophe ceiling over the paired median. The corrected run measured 2.2 ms → 8.4 ms, 4.1× growth. Dynamic-Groovy timings remain informative, not permission for interpreter slowness.
- **Governance resolution.** IF170–IF172 completed the remaining mechanics and executable evidence lane. Owner policy settled 2026-07-21: promote no candidate number now; keep `-PrunPerf` nightly/manual and the complete IF172 collector manual.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.OrderedOuterCorrelationDiffTest`, `interpreter.CorrelationPlanDispatchTest`, `interpreter.DocumentMutationCacheInvalidationTest.orderedCorrelationCacheIsInvalidatedOnMutation`, and `adapter.perf.OuterCorrelationPerfTest.nonEqualityOuterCorrelationIsExactAndSubQuadratic`.

### IF160 — the JVM + JS/Node production dependency hard-zero is fully reconciled and reproducibly reported (REVIEW-CORRECTED 2026-07-20; delivery completed by IF173)

NFR-F1's hard-zero — the interpreter carries NO runtime dependency on the kernel, Groovy, dmtool, `:adapter`, or the other kits — was originally guarded only at the import level. The first IF160 increment inspected JVM `resolvedArtifacts`, but review showed that self-resolving files, graph-only components, published kit coordinates, and the whole production JS/npm graph escaped that view.

- **One exact policy, two host collectors.** `interpreterRuntimeClosure` canonicalizes every `jvmRuntimeClasspath` file, requires exactly one matching resolved artifact, reports graph-only components, and records the own JVM jar separately. `interpreterJsRuntimeClosure` applies the same reconciliation to `jsRuntimeClasspath`, records its own KLIB separately, and recursively walks only `jsPublicPackageJson`'s installed production `dependencies`, `optionalDependencies`, and `peerDependencies` rather than counting the hoisted test toolchain. An absent forbidden optional/peer declaration remains visible to the policy. Both reject intra-repo projects, published `io.github.mbackschat.a12.dm` kit/dmtool/adapter modules, `com.mgmtp` kernel modules, Groovy, opaque files/directories, and forbidden npm package identities including scoped adapter/kernel names.
- **The collector path itself is locked.** The does-fire task resolves detached Gradle graphs and requires real `ProjectComponentIdentifier` and `ModuleComponentIdentifier` instances to survive classifier → file reconciliation → policy, while actual and unmatched file/directory dependencies become rejected opaque entries. Synthetic cases retain precise coverage of every forbidden class, duplicate artifact ownership, and recursive dependency/optional/peer npm declarations; package lookup follows nearest-ancestor Node resolution, and `optionalDependencies` correctly override a same-named required declaration.
- **Separate reproducible evidence.** `jvm-runtime-closure.txt` and `js-runtime-closure.txt` name their host graph, distinguish reconciled files from graph-only components and the own artifact, and emit stable component/package identities rather than machine-local cache paths. The generated clean-tree reports are the authoritative inventories and byte totals; current-facing prose intentionally does not duplicate those derived values.
- **The comparator executes equivalent capability but remains informative.** `adapter:kernelRuntimeClosureReport` first runs a fresh consumer that deserializes and expands a self-contained model, builds a document, invokes the dynamic kernel's `validateFull`, and requires the exact expected diagnostic. Its isolated source set declares only the facade compile dependency and dynamic runtime implementation; the report derives their full runtime graph, reconciles every file, accounts graph-only components, and records the consumer jar separately. It is a capability-shaped comparator, not a promoted `≤50%` release result; IF173 separately owns same-collector artifact/delivery comparisons.
- **The delivery residual graduated as IF173.** Thin/packed JVM, JS KLIB, native, and native-equivalent trimmed-JVM bases now rebuild from the current and previous clean revisions and report exact identities/deltas. The attempted Kotlin/JS production library exposed a genuine boundary: without IG85's exported/publication API it DCEs to an empty module and lacks its declared `.d.ts`; counting it would be fictional, so production npm measurement is mandatory in IG85's future publication increment rather than an ordinary residual here.

*Locked:* `:interpreter:interpreterRuntimeClosurePolicyTest`, `:interpreter:interpreterRuntimeClosure`, and `:interpreter:interpreterJsRuntimeClosure` (all in `:interpreter:check`) plus the informative `:adapter:kernelRuntimeClosureReport`.

### IF161 — deterministic cache-lifetime guards complement the informative retained-heap endpoint smoke (REVIEW-CORRECTED 2026-07-20)

NFR-F2 requires the interpreter's runtime state to grow with the model/document and **reach a plateau** without leaking discarded owners. IF161 first added useful heap calibration, but review showed that one in-process same-owner before/after delta cannot prove that contract: a small per-evaluation leak can remain below its slack, a global cache can retain each discarded document while one reused document appears flat, and reachability/GC noise can move either endpoint. The correction separates deterministic structural lifetime guards from informative heap measurement.

- **The same-owner endpoint smoke (diagnostic, not proof).** `RetainedHeapProbeTest.sameOwnerRetainedEndpointsStayWithinDiagnosticSlack` warms one engine + document, samples retained heap, evaluates it **400×** more, and compares one later endpoint under a generous 24 MB diagnostic slack (the reviewed run displayed Δ ≈ **0.0 MB**). It can catch catastrophic growth in that one shape; it is not a plateau/no-leak invariant or an NFR/release bar.
- **Deterministic cache-owner lifetime guards.** An internal, nullable, per-`DmInterpreter` observer reports logical entries for the real `ParsedModel`, `Document`, and computation-step `OverlayViews` caches without exposing a public API or retaining their owners. Five post-warm checkpoints must have an exact zero slope while reachability fences prove the engine/document remain live. Twelve discarded engine/document pairs must clear a weak observer back to baseline and reach a `ReferenceQueue`; overlay views clear in `finally` at the end of every computation step. Executable positive controls prove that one planted entry per evaluation breaks the plateau guard and a global list retaining discarded documents breaks the release guard.

*Locked:* `interpreter.CacheLifetimeGuardTest`. *Diagnostic smoke:* `adapter.perf.RetainedHeapProbeTest.sameOwnerRetainedEndpointsStayWithinDiagnosticSlack`; it certifies neither allocation volume nor an NFR number. The valid multi-scale host lane is IF162.

### IF162 — the forked JVM/Node runtime-footprint lane rejects invalid evidence and reports informative curves (DELIVERED 2026-07-20; delivery completed by IF173)

The earlier in-process JVM scale recording silently clamped non-positive retained deltas and could not distinguish independent forks, Node behavior, allocation volume, semantic drift, or owner leakage. The replacement is a local opt-in evidence lane whose protocol fails before aggregation when any of those prerequisites is missing.

- **Independent, explicit hosts.** `:interpreter:interpreterFootprint` requires an explicit commit, exact `N, 2N, 4N, 8N` scales, positive warmup/repetition counts, and at least five workers per host. Each JVM and Node worker is a separate fixed-heap process with a unique PID and invocation id. JVM uses explicit settled `System.gc()`, post-GC used heap, and `ThreadMXBean` thread-allocation counters; Node requires `--expose-gc`, uses settled `global.gc()`, post-GC V8 heap, and V8 Inspector allocation sampling. The fork record carries runtime/version/OS/architecture/collector/heap/GC/allocation method metadata.
- **Semantics and ownership precede numbers.** Three canonical models are authored through modelkit/EditSession/rulekit on the JVM, freshness-guarded there, and embedded unchanged into common tests so JVM and Node consume one serialization without acquiring a kit runtime dependency. They exercise simple validation, equality `Having` correlation, and a computation cascade whose overlay cache is observed non-empty. The scenario identity hashes that complete model artifact plus explicit workload-generator metadata, not a parallel sketch. Each exact scale is semantically preflighted before measurement; compute preflight covers every target/value/row identity in the cascade; setup and warmup stay outside the allocation bracket; result code/value and cardinality are part of the sample contract. Logical `Document`, `ParsedModel`, and `OverlayViews` counts are recorded, overlay and discarded-owner counts must return to zero, and weak references must prove the measured owners release.
- **Bad evidence is unrepresentable downstream.** The protocol rejects missing explicit-GC support, missing metadata, reused process/invocation identities, incomplete or duplicate scales/scenarios, fingerprint or commit disagreement, non-positive retained/allocation observations, absent cache counts, unsupported/wrong outcomes, wrong cardinality, and leaking owners. No measurement is clamped.
- **One structured, informative artifact.** The aggregate preserves every raw fork and sample, takes medians at each scale, and reports per-host/per-scenario retained and allocated log-log exponents plus worst 4× steps. It explicitly marks itself `informativeOnly`; it promotes no candidate threshold and has no `check` or CI dependency. `scripts/interpreter-footprint.sh` is the clean-commit entry point and the report lives under `interpreter/build/reports/interpreter-footprint/`.

*Locked:* `FootprintScenarioFixtureGuardTest`, `FootprintProtocolTest`, `FootprintScenariosTest`, `FootprintWorkerTest`, `FootprintAggregationTest`, the JVM/Node worker implementations, and the opt-in Gradle orchestration.

### IF165 — Route-B timezone classification and complete transition evidence are enforced end to end (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; completes RV-2026-07-20-02 issues 1–4/6–7)

The preparation boundary now preserves four distinct domains: absent→UTC default, exact supported `{UTC,GMT,Europe/Berlin}`, JVM-proven kernel-legal/outside-set, and invalid blank/whitespace/GMT-fallback ids. Common code makes no unchecked legality claim; every JVM runtime verb uses the one modelkit classifier before loading. The authoring surfaces use the same contract: invalid ids get corrective `MVK_INVALID_TIME_ZONE` enrichment, while a successful outside-set `config modify`/apply carries a nonblocking evaluator-scope warning. Public setters/help/schema describe the distinction and the regenerated API golden records the deliberate public classifier/diagnostic surface.

The Berlin certificate now covers the exact pinned JDK 21.0.11/tzdb2026a vector rather than representative dates: the common JVM/Node guard compares all 62 pairs, immediately before/at/after each transition, every gap/overlap class, both outer regimes, and seeded mutation classes; the adapter guard checks that same independent vector through one prepared dynamic-Groovy engine and one prepared generated-Java engine. All three runtime verbs produce the exact structured New York refusal and the same unmodified model is proven cleanly check-valid.

*Locked:* `ModelTimeZoneContractTest`, `ModelTimeZoneTest` (JVM+Node), `BerlinHistoricalTzTest` (JVM+Node), `BerlinTransitionOracleDiffTest`, `EvalUnsupportedTimeZoneRefusalTest`, `ConfigTimeZoneContractTest`, and `apiGuardTest`.

### IF166 — Berlin DATE_TIME `AddDays` uses an allocation-free landing loop with historical scale evidence (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; completes RV-2026-07-20-02 issue 5)

`wallDayLandingInstant` tracks earliest/latest consistent instants and gap-bracketing offsets in one scalar pass over the three Berlin offsets; the per-row `filter`/`map` collections are gone. The typed DSL now preserves DATE_TIME output for `AddDays(DateTimeOperand, …)`, allowing the benchmark to author the real expression instead of dropping to raw text. `PerfCatalog.berlinAddDays` cycles ordinary/gap/overlap landings in both directions, including CEMT, proves interpreter/dynamic-Groovy/generated-Java signatures at N/4N, then uses the IF137 paired estimator and unchanged `<9×` catastrophe floor.

*Measured:* on the same Apple Silicon runner, the allocating path was 12.3/50.4 ms at 1,000/4,000 rows (4.14×); the scalar path was 12.6/49.4 ms (3.93×). This is implementation evidence, not a promoted release bar. *Locked:* `DateTimeAddDaysTypingTest`, `WallDayArithmeticDiffTest`, `ModelTimeZoneTest`, and `PerfGateTest.berlinAddDaysStaysSubQuadratic`.

### IF163 — both decimal actuals reproduce one `MathContext(50)` contract with bounded JS work (SHIPPED 2026-07-20, graduated from IG89)

The review of IF153 found four coupled residuals in the platform primitive: `+`/`−` omitted the kernel context on both hosts; JS positive power rounded an exact result instead of using bounded working precision; JS division rounded by decimal places before significant digits and could exceed big.js's magnitude-dependent `DP` limit; and finite `PE`/`NE` sentinels could still select exponent notation. Those were one abstraction failure: the actuals exposed host-library defaults instead of one explicit `VkBigDecimal` contract.

- **One arithmetic context.** JVM `BigDecimal.add`/`subtract` now take the same `MathContext(50, HALF_UP)` already used by multiply/divide/power. JS caps all five operators to 50 significant digits. Positive power scans the exponent from its most-significant bit while retaining only `50 + exponent-digit-count + 1` working digits, then rounds once to 50; reciprocal-first negative power feeds the same bounded algorithm. A test-only peak counter proves `base^1000` never materializes the exact result.
- **One-round, magnitude-safe division.** JS normalizes both operands to one-digit mantissas, divides them under a fixed truncating guard budget, performs the single significant-digit `HALF_UP` rounding, and restores the base-10 exponent structurally. The budget is independent of result magnitude, so legal nested magnitudes cannot hit big.js's one-million-decimal-place ceiling and no prior decimal-place rounding can alter the 50th significant digit.
- **Unconditional plain rendering.** `display`, scale/precision accounting, and hashing read big.js's normalized public coefficient/exponent/sign state. Plain notation no longer depends on any finite `PE`/`NE` switch; the inclusive `±1,000,000` boundaries are locked.
- **Evidence.** `DecimalHostParityTest` runs exact `+`/`−`, positive/negative power, one-round division, zero/exponent edges, magnitude-safe division, and plain-boundary vectors on JVM and Node. `DecimalPowerGrowthTest` locks bounded JS work; `DecimalHostIsolationTest` retains the hostile-global lock. `DecimalPrecisionParityDiffTest` tri-checks observable addition, subtraction, division, positive-power, and reciprocal-first negative-power splits against Groovy-dynamic and generated Java.

### IF179 — cached arithmetic ASTs receive the kernel's exact one-pass division lowering (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; SPEC-2026-07-19-08)

The decimal actuals already apply `MathContext(50, HALF_UP)` to all five arithmetic nodes, but the interpreter still evaluated the authored tree directly. The kernel instead performs one order-sensitive post-order transform before either dynamic-Groovy or generated-Java evaluation. `parseCondition` and `parseExpression` now run one shared model-static `lowerArithmetic` pass before `ParsedModel` caches the AST: each original multiplication visits its children, pulls only immediate division-root operands, keeps ordinary factors first, appends numerators and denominators in encounter order, left-folds products, and returns without revisiting the generated nodes.

This changes legal interpreter results exactly where operation-local precision makes the authored tree, one-pass tree, and fixed-point tree observably different; JVM and Node share the common lowering. No public interpreter type or DM-JSON shape changed. `model eval`, `rule eval`, and computation evaluation all consume the corrected cached AST. The typed rulekit renderer still preserves braces for authoring legality and fold structure; braces deliberately do not make a division opaque to this later runtime transform.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ArithmeticLoweringDiffTest` tri-checks direct-vs-lowered, one-pass-vs-fixed-point, and the corrected three-thirds pre-round witness against dynamic Groovy and generated Java; multiplatform `interpreter.ArithmeticLoweringTest` locks the exact prepared tree on JVM+Node. `RoundingLawsTest` now uses the real three-thirds discriminator instead of the superseded multiplied-third example.

### IF180 — typed numeric equality carries signed scale and constant capability (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; SPEC-2026-07-19-09)

The former D13 precheck encoded the stale rule “field equality needs equal declared scales; literals are exempt.” The missing inverse probe refuted it: a scale-2 field may equal literal `0`, but a scale-0 field may not equal literal `0.00`, in either operand order. The kernel checks a shared expression summary with two independent dimensions: known signed scale or unknown, and multiplicative-constant capability. Only a capable operand on the smaller-scale side may be padded; ordering bypasses the gate.

`NumericScaleSummary` now composes that metadata once across the typed surface. Fractional literals retain authored scale, integer literals strip trailing zeros, fields carry declared scale, addition/subtraction take maximum scale with capability AND, multiplication sums scales with capability OR, division is unknown/non-capable, explicit rounding supplies its result scale, and power is unknown/non-capable except for a known scale-0 base with a simple nonnegative constant exponent whose derived scale is ≤ 0. Count/integer functions are known scale 0. A generic `NumberExpr(Calculation)` whose model-derived scale cannot be recovered is `DEFERRED`: the live kernel remains the oracle instead of rulekit fabricating a precheck.

This deliberately changes typed authoring acceptance: known-illegal equalities fail earlier, and legal constant-padding shapes no longer fail the old equal-scale shortcut; runtime values for already-legal models do not change. Raw/parser/read paths still preserve and kernel-check conditions, while the catalog, diagnostic enrichment, schema, and CLI `allowDifferingDecimals` language now teach the derived rule. DM-JSON syntax is unchanged.

Public compatibility is explicit. `NumberExpr` retains the public `(Calculation)` constructor, `expression()` accessor, comparison/arithmetic method descriptors, value equality/hash, and record-style text, so ordinary source and JVM call sites remain compatible. It is now a final value class rather than a Java record so it can retain private summary metadata: record patterns/deconstruction stop compiling; reflection/serialization consumers that require `isRecord()`/record components or `java.lang.Record` as the superclass must migrate to `expression()`. The API golden records that deliberate boundary.

*Locked:* real-kernel `NumberLawsTest` carries both operand orders, signed literal scale, the narrow power exception, fractional-exponent authoring rejection, and ordering bypass; `NumericScaleGateTest` locks the typed summary and no-capability-through-power rule.

### IF181 — power evidence names staged positive precision and closes the legal endpoint matrix (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; SPEC-2026-07-19-10)

IF163 already put both decimal actuals on the correct mechanism: positive power follows OpenJDK 21 X3.274 `BigDecimal.pow(int, MathContext)` numeric-value staging with working precision `50 + decimalDigits(exponent) + 1`, then rounds to 50; a negative exponent first rounds `1/base` at precision 50 and feeds that reciprocal into the positive algorithm. The named positive separator ends `…3319` rather than exact-power/final-round `…3318`, and the negative separator ends `…7036` rather than reciprocal-after-power `…7037`.

The remaining evidence is now closed against both kernel strategies: `+1000` and `−1000` execute, `+1001` and `−1001` are quiet invalids, `0^0 = 1`, and `0^-1` is quiet invalid. A fractional exponent is not a runtime endpoint: the shared authoring parser rejects it with `MVK_INVALID_NUMBER_FOR_EXP`, locked beside a legal scale-0 field exponent in `NumberLawsTest`. The peer interpreter is triangulation only; dynamic Groovy remains the oracle and generated Java agrees on every legal matrix arm.

This increment adds evidence and corrects prose; it does not change the already-shipped JVM/Node arithmetic mechanism, public interpreter API, serialized model form, rulekit factories, or dmtool behavior.

*Locked:* multiplatform `DecimalHostParityTest` holds the staged positive/negative primitive separators; `DecimalPrecisionParityDiffTest` carries the complete legal dual-route endpoint matrix; `NumberLawsTest.powerExponentMustHaveNoDecimalPlaces` owns the fractional authoring boundary.

### IF182 — arithmetic fillability has executable joint-term and conservative-power branch coverage (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; SPEC-2026-07-19-11)

The existing `ArithFill` implementation was source-aligned, but its evidence and canonical account reduced multiplication/division to “sign-dependent” and power to “parity.” The completed pure matrix now locks both direction bits for multiplication's joint terms (including current zero signs), reciprocal/division directions, exponent zero, fixed bases `1`, `0.5`, `0`, and `−1`, negative reciprocal staging, the both-variable grow-only branch, and every invalid domain. Domain-invalid arithmetic returns unknown/fixed fill and never reaches comparison typing as an ordinary value.

The surprising policy separator is intentional and shared: both kernel strategies and the interpreter compute `0^0 = 1`, yet `0 ^ empty-unsigned > 0` fires OMISSION while the signed-exponent twin fires VALUE. This is a conservative dispatch heuristic over current value, fill directions, base regions, and parity—not exact reachable-set analysis.

No runtime result, public API, authoring legality, serialized form, or dmtool surface changed; this increment hardens the already-correct common JVM/Node implementation and replaces non-executable prose with the exact formulas.

*Locked:* `ArithmeticFillBranchTest` is the multiplatform two-bit branch matrix; `ArithmeticFillPolarityDiffTest` tri-checks the zero-base discriminator against dynamic Groovy and generated Java.

### IF183 — typed arithmetic rendering preserves fold order and exact plain authoring regions (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; SPEC-2026-07-19-14)

The former renderer grouped same-precedence children only for conventional subtraction/division associativity. That is insufficient when every arithmetic node rounds independently, and it could flatten a typed nested-division tree into a kernel-illegal region. `DslRenderer` now groups every right same-precedence arithmetic child, a left power child, and a left division-bearing region before an enclosing division. Its open-division summary models the exact plain rule after the renderer's own child groups: grouping/addition/subtraction/power reset, multiplication/division combine ungrouped child contributions, and division adds one.

This preserves both typed fold and parse legality: `(8/4)/2` renders `{ 8 / 4 } / 2`; `(8/4*2)/3` renders `{ 8 / 4 * 2 } / 3`; `8*(4/2)/3` needs only `8 * { 4 / 2 } / 3`; and either nested power side is grouped. Braces remain an authoring/reset boundary only and do not block IF179's later runtime lowering. Operation-valued wrappers remain kernel-delegated because their legacy reset control flow is not covered by the plain summary.

The public AST and method descriptors are unchanged. Serialized/rendered condition text gains braces for affected typed trees, which is a behaviorally compatible correction: it preserves the original tree and changes formerly illegal or incorrectly re-associated output into kernel-accepted output. Raw dmtool conditions and foreign-rule text are not rewritten merely by evaluation; typed authoring/formatting that invokes the renderer receives the corrected canonical grouping.

The IF179 differential now fails before runtime if its exact rendered witness produces any kernel consistency diagnostic, closing the earlier evidence hole where an illegal model could be passed to both engines. The renderer locks the two exact witness shapes independently.

*Locked:* `ArithmeticGroupingRenderTest`, real-kernel `NumberLawsTest.plainArithmeticCountsDivisionContributionsUntilAReset`/`powerOperatorRejectsUnparenthesizedNesting`, and the authoring-guarded dual-route `ArithmeticLoweringDiffTest`.

### IF164 — one ES2018 parser/translator and one distinct-pattern registry reproduce admitted Java-pattern semantics on JVM and Node (SHIPPED 2026-07-20, graduated from IG90)

The IF154 increment translated five Java whitespace escapes and removed per-row compilation, but the review found one shared abstraction missing: a legal pattern needed one closed construct model for translation and unsupported classification, and every model-static pattern locus needed one owner-scoped compilation registry. Independent heuristics and per-occurrence caches could still misparse legal syntax, throw a host `SyntaxError`, or repeat compilation.

- **One closed dialect model.** `JavaPatternToJs` parses class boundaries, captures/backreferences, Java default classes and dot behavior, non-nested intersections, code-point/named-character/grapheme constructs, and global/scoped/negative/combined inline flags into ES2018-safe output. `containsResidual` invokes that same parser; a construct cannot pass an independent heuristic and then fail inside host compilation. The translator preserves leading-literal-`]` and negated-class boundaries and handles the standalone `[\H]`/`[\V]` forms.
- **One engine-owned registry.** `PatternRegistry` keys compiled patterns by source. Condition literals, declared STRING patterns, predefined-type patterns, computation formal checks, and seed verification all resolve through it, so compilation count is bounded by the number of distinct model-static sources per interpreter owner rather than AST occurrences or rows.
- **Evidence boundary.** `PatternDialectParityTest` is the common JVM/Node primitive lock, including `\s`, NEL/supplementary dot behavior, unmatched backreferences, intersection, character/class boundaries, flags, and Java Unicode constructs. `PatternDialectDiffTest` tri-checks the model-observable admitted cases against Groovy-dynamic and generated Java; `FieldConstraintFormalDiffTest` and `PredefinedTypeValidityDiffTest` cover declared and predefined loci, including the EMail/NBSP discriminator. Supplementary-dot and `\x{1F600}` remain primitive locks because the kernel's default legal-charset formal check prevents those values reaching rule evaluation; they are not misrepresented as tri-engine rule fixtures.
- **Direct-oracle correction.** The A12K-30 source blacklist does not list uppercase `\P`, but a direct typed/full kernel model rejects `\P{L}` with `MVK_INVALID_PATTERN`. The translator defensively supports the primitive on both hosts; neither this finding nor the evidence matrix classifies it as kernel-admitted.
- **Performance.** `PatternRegistryTest` locks one compilation per distinct source across rule/declared occurrences and the dialect-sensitive seed path. `PatternPerfTest` uses exact tri-engine semantic preflight, then the ratified paired N/4N estimator under the existing catastrophe floor; it protects the registry and per-row match path without promoting a new NFR bar.

### IF170 — the Node performance launcher propagates its test-process exit (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; graduated from IG77)

`scripts/perf.sh js` used to append `|| true` to the Node/Mocha invocation. Compilation failures were already fatal, but a compiled JS suite that failed assertions or crashed still fell through to the result extractor and exited successfully, turning the Node performance lane into false evidence.

The launcher now retains the Node command's nonzero case status, prints the captured result lines for diagnosis, and exits with that status. `PerfScriptContractTest` runs in a dedicated input-declared `perfScriptGuardTest` task, so editing `scripts/perf.sh` rechecks only this contract and the broad kernel-backed suite cannot restore a stale cached green.

*Locked:* `:adapter:perfScriptGuardTest`.

### IF171 — declared performance regimes and model-static preparation work are executable contracts (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; graduated from IG77)

A closed benchmark inventory is incomplete if its metadata permits required semantic regimes but no reviewed scenario actually exercises them. The inventory now carries both validation regimes (firing and low-firing) plus computation VALUE, CLEARED, and ERRORED. The added cases are authored through rulekit/modelkit, carry content-bound identities and exact expected cardinalities, and cross the same interpreter/dynamic-Groovy/generated-Java semantic preflight before timing. `ScenarioInventoryTest` fails if any required regime disappears.

Source inspection is likewise not evidence that model-static work stays model-static. An internal, nullable observer exposes a closed set of preparation events without changing the public API or evaluation semantics: stored rule/computation ASTs, implicit-computation ASTs, prepared rule/computation plans, the whole-model computation order, and per-target dependency closures. `PreparationWorkCounterTest` forces every class, then runs `validate`, `compute`, and `computeField` again on a different document and requires the exact counts to remain unchanged on JVM and Node.

The counter exposed one genuine repeated mechanism: `ComputationEngine` recomputed the same topological dependency DFS for every whole-model and single-field computation call. The engine now lazily caches the whole-model order and each declared target's transitive closure. Those artifacts are model-owned; document values, overlays, and outcomes remain call-owned.

*Locked:* common JVM+Node `PreparationWorkCounterTest`; `adapter.perf.ScenarioInventoryTest`; exact tri-engine inventory execution of `B6-many-rules-low-firing`, `compute-cleared`, and `compute-errored`.

### IF172 — one structured lane measures independent JVM, Node, native, and fair-Java performance (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; graduated from IG77)

The former in-process startup helpers could not establish first-answer latency: their clocks began after engine/document construction and their samples shared a process. The replacement keeps those diagnostic helpers but adds a separate opt-in evidence protocol whose unit of independence is an operating-system process.

- **One exact workload definition.** The B1/B2 and 48/192-rule models are generated from the typed `PerfCatalog`; a freshness guard rejects drift in their committed JVM/Node/native serialization. Before timing, representative scales are checked for complete indexed outcomes and zero unsupported across interpreter, dynamic Groovy, and generated Java.
- **The clock matches each contract.** JVM and Node first-answer samples start before `DmInterpreter(model)` and end after complete validation-result materialization, with one timing per fresh process. The native parent clock covers process launch through a complete successful JSON envelope and exit after one discarded OS-cache warm invocation. Warm JVM/Node samples keep preparation outside the clock and use the ratified interleaved N/4N median-of-within-pair estimator where a growth pair exists.
- **Evidence is self-identifying and fail-closed.** Every raw fork and summary carries commit, explicit runner, runtime/host/heap/process identity, `id@version`, a SHA-256 identity computed from every actual scale fixture used: typed model JSON, the document's ordered valued-field path/repetition/value sequence, scenario/scale/locale/zone, and the exact expected support/cardinality/outcome. The worker hashes those same fixture objects rather than reconstructing an approximation. Aggregation rejects reused processes, incomplete scenario sets, fingerprint/commit/runner disagreement, unsupported or wrong outcomes, and invalid timings. The fair-Java report derives its cases from `PerfInventory`, primes code generation outside all clocks, and retains exact tri-engine preflight.
- **Posture is unchanged.** `scripts/interpreter-performance.sh` runs the complete local bundle and writes structured reports, but it promotes no number and changes no CI trigger. An actual candidate becomes a release bar only through IF137's six-point run and owner ratification.

*Locked:* common JVM+Node `PerformanceProtocolTest`; `PerformanceScenarioFixtureGuardTest`; `PerformanceScenarioEligibilityTest`; `NativePerformanceRunnerTest`; opt-in `:interpreter:interpreterPerformance`, `:adapter:interpreterFairJavaReport`, and `:adapter:interpreterNativePerformance`.

### IF173 — one collector rebuilds and executes current/previous functional delivery bases (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; graduated from IG79)

Artifact bytes are comparable only when the base, capability, build definition, and revision identity are explicit. `scripts/interpreter-delivery-footprint.sh` therefore requires a clean committed tree, resolves a distinct previous-release commit, injects the current versioned collector definition into both source revisions, forces both builds to rerun, and records the commit, runner, host/toolchain identity, collector SHA-256, artifact bytes, and artifact/tree SHA-256 in structured reports.

- **Five honest bases.** The collector records the thin interpreter JVM jar, a reproducible ZIP of that jar plus its exact non-JDK runtime files, the interpreter JS KLIB as a separate development artifact, the native dmtool executable, and a `jpackage`/`jlink` app image staged from the same Groovy-excluded classpath as native dmtool. It never calls the full Groovy-bearing `installDist` image “equivalent.”
- **Capability is executed, not inferred from files.** The packed JVM runtime is unpacked, its thin jar is byte-checked against the measured `jvm-thin` base, and a Java source outside the project is compiled and run using only the packed `lib/*.jar`; it must load the committed model, build a document, validate it, and return exactly zero messages and zero unsupported rules. An archived baseline without `ModelLoader` records the precise `API_PREDATES_SMOKE` status rather than claiming execution. Both the native and trimmed-JVM launchers must report the expected project version and run the same committed typed low-firing model/instance through `model eval --no-computations`; success requires a valid envelope with no firing or diagnostic. A missing/non-executable artifact, wrong version, wrong outcome, or mismatched base set fails before comparison.
- **Previous-release ratchet is structured and informative.** The reports must share collector, runner, and host/toolchain identity. Each current base carries previous/current bytes, delta, ratio, and an informative `>10%` review signal. The native/trimmed-JVM ratio is also recorded. No signal promotes a number or changes CI scheduling; IF137's six-point gate and owner ratification still control that.
- **Production npm fails closed at the real boundary.** The present Kotlin/JS production-library experiment DCEs to an effectively empty module because no public JS API is exported, while its package metadata names a missing `.d.ts`. Packing that output—or substituting the KLIB plus unpacked `node_modules`—would fabricate a product artifact. The report therefore emits no npm bytes and names IG85 as the owner-ratified publication trigger. In that same future increment, the real package must prove a non-empty exported API and `.d.ts`, pass a non-repo consumer smoke, and join this current/previous report.

*Executable protocol:* [`scripts/interpreter-delivery-footprint.sh`](../scripts/interpreter-delivery-footprint.sh) + [`scripts/interpreter-delivery-footprint.init.gradle`](../scripts/interpreter-delivery-footprint.init.gradle). The local collector-smoke path may skip artifact execution only when an explicit test override supplies a stand-in native binary; the public script exposes no such bypass.

### IF174 — tolerance independently normalizes operands and uses directional inequality polarity (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; completes SPEC-2026-07-19-13)

The earlier tolerance implementation got the ordinary integer boundaries right but encoded two false mechanisms: `evalTolerance` subtracted first and rounded the completed difference, while `toleranceType` treated any operand movement as equality-like fillability. Kernel source and the Lean handback supplied separators the existing tests did not: the runtime first applies scale-19 `HALF_UP` to each operand and only then subtracts, and every tolerance operator routes through the directional numeric-`!=` polarity helper.

`ToleranceOps` is now the one pure runtime source for both decisions. `differs` computes `|R₁₉(left) − R₁₉(right)| > band`; `fillable` orders those normalized operands and delegates to `Polarity.neFillable`, so only movement of the smaller side upward or the larger side downward can close a firing gap. Both truth and polarity resolve operands through `evalCmpOperand`, retaining numeric `BaseYear` polymorphism and the tolerance family's scale-gate bypass. IF148's per-alternative metadata and synthesized-condition path is unchanged and now executes the corrected shared primitive.

*Locked:* multiplatform `ToleranceOpsTest` carries the independent-rounding and unsigned directional primitives. `ToleranceExactnessDiffTest` authors through rulekit and compares Groovy-dynamic, generated Java, and the interpreter for the `49/10²¹` separator, unsigned `0` versus `−2` VALUE and `+2` OMISSION, malformed suppression, all four strict bands, mixed declared scales, and numeric `BaseYear`. Existing `ToleranceDiffTest`, `ToleranceComparisonLawsTest`, and `ComputationToleranceValidationDiffTest` retain the broader boundary, empty-row, and implicit-computation matrix.

### IF175 — computation selection and implicit validation are distinct consumers of one alternative table (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; completes SPEC-2026-07-19-06)

The computation engine already scans alternatives in declaration order and stops at the first known-true guard. The implicit validation already synthesizes one guarded stored-value mismatch per alternative and ORs them all. The stale defect was explanatory: comments and authoring guidance described those two correct implementations as one shared selector, which would make a future refactor erase legal overlap behavior.

The composed discriminator has two simultaneously holding guards, operations `1` and `2`, and stored value `1`: computation returns the first result, while validation fires because the later holding operation still disagrees. Equal operations stay clean, and a false second guard contributes nothing. The linter/schema/API wording now says why alternatives should be mutually exclusive without claiming the later row is semantically dead.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ComputationOverlapValidationDiffTest` tri-checks the discriminator and both controls against dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter.

### IF176 — expression and field-value rounding preserve the optional `0..14` places argument (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; completes SPEC-2026-07-19-07)

Both grammar forms accept an optional decimal-places argument: `RoundDown/Up/Accounting(expr[, n])` and `RoundDown/Up/AccountingValue(field[, n])`, with `n ∈ 0..14` and omission exactly equivalent to zero. `Rounding` and `FieldRounding` now carry `Optional<Integer>`; renderers omit an absent argument, and typed factories expose both one-argument and explicit-places forms. Read-back preserves positive places but canonicalizes persisted explicit zero to omission because the kernel parse objects retain only the integer default and cannot distinguish the two spellings.

The runtime arithmetic was already correct: scale-19 `HALF_UP` pre-round, then `FLOOR`, `CEILING`, or `HALF_UP` at the requested scale. The catalog, canonical semantics, MVK ledger, Javadocs, and API guide now agree on `0`/`14` acceptance, `15` rejection, negative direction, and non-banker's ties. API compatibility is recorded in API-FINDINGS 79: old factories/constructors are retained where possible, while the public record-component changes are explicitly classified.

*Locked:* `adapter.FieldRoundingPlacesReadTest`, the existing `RoundingLawsTest` / `RoundDiffTest` / `SingleArgRoundDiffTest`, and the public API/doc guards.

### IF177 — division domain failure has a cleared public delta but an invalid target and poisoned dependents (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; completes SPEC-2026-07-19-12)

The former `Val.Unknown` model collapsed two distinct kernel values. `VkBigDecimal.divide` returns an invalid numeric sentinel on zero division, and numeric arithmetic plus `Abs`, operand-list `Min`/`Max`, and rounding preserve it. A rule comparison projects the sentinel to UNKNOWN/no fire. A legal `RoundAccounting(div, 2)` computation reaches the numeric store boundary as invalid: no fresh value is reported, a stale value is reported CLEARED, `formalErrorsInOperands` carries `berechnungsWertFehler` at the target, and a dependent read poisons and clears transitively. The public delta is therefore not evidence of a clean empty.

`Val.DomainFailure` now preserves that distinction through the numeric expression tree. `ComputationEngine` returns the existing public CLEARED/no-value outcome but marks the target internally poisoned and decided, so a later same-target computation cannot replace it and a dependent cannot read it as empty. Comparisons explicitly project it to UNKNOWN. Numeric list/unary consumers were swept at the shared gates so `Abs` and either-position `Min`/`Max` cannot erase or crash on it. The source-supported boundary is explicit and differential-locked: a date shift receiving an invalid numeric amount produces a valueless date and clears without `berechnungsWertFehler`; invalid-power computation reuses this exact mechanism and is classified by [IF203](#if203--runtime-invalid-integral-power-poisons-a-number-computation-target-exactly-like-division-shipped-2026-07-22-completes-spec-2026-07-21-05).

**Public API compatibility.** `Val` is a public sealed `commonMain` hierarchy, so the added subtype is not an ABI-invisible implementation detail. Existing JVM binaries remain linkage-compatible because no prior class or descriptor changed, but a previously compiled exhaustive dispatch can fail at runtime when handed `DomainFailure`; recompiling an exhaustive Kotlin common/JVM/JS `when` or Java sealed switch requires a new case. Kotlin/JS KLIB consumers must be rebuilt; raw JS/TypeScript has no supported `Val` export, and no stable JS/KLIB ABI is promised before [IG85](INTERPRETER-GAPS.md). Sealed-subtype reflection and consumer-owned polymorphic serializers/closed schemas also gain a case, although `Val` has no repository-owned serialization or CLI/DM-JSON wire contract. [API-FINDINGS](API-FINDINGS.md) 80 carries the complete compatibility classification.

The one-pass lowered arithmetic tree evaluates left-to-right. A domain-failed numeric left subtree still evaluates a later operand, so that later read can poison; a poison thrown by the lowered left subtree aborts before the right. The shared SPEC-08 lowering pass therefore also owns which authored operand becomes the first read after transformation.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.DivisionDomainFailureComputationDiffTest` retains formal operand errors and tri-checks fresh/stale target invalidity, dependent poison, `Abs`/`Min`/`Max`, the date-shift boundary, and the runtime-observable ordered separator against dynamic Groovy and generated Java: a domain-failed lowered left still reaches a poisoned right. The requested first-poison runtime separator is not observable through the kernel's public computation result: swapping which raw field is invalid leaves the same clear/error-code projection, and when both are invalid `formalErrorsInOperands` inventories both even though the generated `CalculationFieldException` catch aborts on the first read. The class locks that refutation and the generated expression order/catch. Multiplatform `interpreter.DivisionDomainFailureComputationTest` uses an observing poison gate to assert the interpreter's actually selected `Later`-before-`Source` read on JVM+Node. Thus the second ordering rule is source-defined and interpreter-observed, not overclaimed as a public kernel runtime projection.

### IF178 — `$` resolves each marked path from the complete captured outer repetition environment (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; completes SPEC-2026-07-19-16)

The interpreter implementation was already structurally correct: `havingInterp` carries the full outer `EvalContext`, and a `$` field or `CurrentRepetition` resolves its own named path through that captured context. The stale risk was documentation that could be read as one scalar outer row number.

The focused nested discriminator holds the captured parent row at coordinate `1` and varies its descendant coordinate. Its filter requires a candidate parent coordinate to equal both the captured parent and captured descendant coordinates. The diagonal outer row `(1,1)` selects parent candidate `1`; the off-diagonal row `(1,2)` selects none. A marker-bearing candidate parent row `2` has no outer `Probe`: an implementation that reused descendant scalar `2` for both `$` paths would select that marker and falsely fire the second child, so the off-diagonal arm distinguishes the faulty scalar capture. Dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the unchanged interpreter agree. This proves path-specific coordinates in one capture; it makes no broader inference about multi-star binding beyond the separately locked IF144 rules.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.CapturedOuterEnvironmentDiffTest`.

### IF184 — `FirstFilledValue` observes filters only when their operand slot is reached (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; completes SPEC-2026-07-20-09)

The previous IF104 implementation treated every `Having` anywhere below a comparison operand as encountered. That was exact for its one-slot evidence but wrong for a legal multi-operand `FirstFilledValue`: the kernel presents each slot's filter marker immediately before scanning that slot and `FirstValueCombiner` terminates the whole walk at the first filled or formally unavailable value. A later filtered slot is therefore unreachable and cannot change either the value or its polarity.

`firstFilledScan` now owns the ordered walk for both value evaluation and directional fillability. It visits operands and star rows in authored order, marks a filter only upon entering its slot, stops on the first terminal value, and preserves the existing empty-prefix, partial-relevance, and compute-poison rules. `hasHavingStar` and `leafOmission` delegate the `FirstFilledValue` subtree to that scan, so wrappers and arithmetic cannot reintroduce the structural global-OR bug. The rulekit node and typed factories accept a repetition-list first operand plus homogeneous trailing field/list operands; the renderer, reference walk, operator-usage walk, catalog, and public API golden carry the same ordered shape.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.FirstFilledValueFilterEncounterDiffTest` authors both conditions through the typed dmkits surface and tri-checks dynamic Groovy, generated static Java, and the interpreter: a filter on a later slot after `Primary=9` leaves a VALUE error, while the same filter on the reached first slot makes the firing OMISSION.

### IF185 — constructed dates retain model-zone legacy-calendar identity and reason-bearing no-value semantics (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; completes SPEC-2026-07-20-06/07)

`Date(...)` had used the shared proleptic-Gregorian `isRealDate`. Real-kernel source and both generated strategies instead construct a non-lenient default `GregorianCalendar` in the model zone. The separator is concrete: UTC `1500-02-29` is real on the Julian side; `1582-10-05..14` do not exist; an Apia model rejects its skipped `2011-12-30`. The stored/computed-value `1583-10-16` floor is a separate later check.

The correction is constructor-specific. `isConstructibleDate` models the default hybrid cutover only for supported UTC/GMT/Berlin; unsupported zones remain loud-refused before evaluation. A `DateCalendarBasis` and reason-bearing no-value state follow `Date(...)` through every kernel-legal consumer family and legal composition: component extraction, `DifferenceIn{Days,Months,Years}`, `Add{Days,Months,Years}`, direct date `Min`/`Max` operands, and `DateTime(Date(...), Time(...))` with its legal extraction/shift descendants. A date `Min`/`Max` result is not thereby a legal component-extractor source (`YearFromDate(Min(...))` is grammar-rejected). Julian-day conversion preserves absolute instants and the cutover step without changing the proleptic helpers used by stored dates, ranges, fragments, or their independently locked arithmetic.

The same reason-bearing construction state closes a masked no-value divergence. Present-but-unreal and incomplete constructed dates have no date value, but direct numeric extractors and date differences return the kernel's numeric default 0; malformed components remain Unknown. Date Min/Max skips a valueless unreal/incomplete operand but still propagates malformed non-relevance. At the truth projection, `Valid` and `Invalid` are exact strong-Kleene complements; only the full `Invalid` OMISSION-versus-VALUE verdict cannot be derived from `Valid`.

*Locked:* both-strategy + interpreter `ConstructedDateCalendarDiffTest` (Julian/cutover/Apia separators, all consumer families, DateTime composition, valueless skip/default-zero, malformed control) and multiplatform `ConstructedDateCalendarTest`.

### IF186 — DifferenceInDays reproduces stateful model-zone legacy-calendar stepping (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; completes SPEC-2026-07-20-08)

The prior wall-coordinate quotient was wrong. The kernel orders the two instants, computes a whole-year lower bound, makes one `years × 365` `Calendar.DAY_OF_MONTH` jump, then applies stateful `+1 day` landings until the next would pass the later instant; authored order restores the sign. The clock is retained and can change at a special-hour landing: Berlin `2024-03-30T02:30 → 2024-03-31T01:45` is 1, and the gap landing's `01:30` clock persists so `→ 2024-04-01T02:00` is 2. Apia noon Dec 29 → noon Dec 31 is one step in the wider kernel zone domain.

The commonMain implementation preserves the exact stateful loop and the runtime's February-28 year correction. It does not expand the supported zone domain. Its seed bounds the residual instead of walking every elapsed day; `CalendarDayDifferenceDetail` structurally locks fewer than 2,800 successful residual steps across the full four-digit hybrid-calendar label domain, avoiding a flaky timing threshold. Sub-day differences remain instant-millisecond quotients and other date operations retain their own contracts.

The adjacent false-green AddDays guard was corrected too: a forward Berlin gap landing is the earlier no-overshoot `01:30` instant, not `03:30`, and the differential now asserts that the expected rule actually fires in Groovy-dynamic, generated Java, and the interpreter.

*Locked:* `CalendarStepDifferenceInDaysDiffTest`, `WallDayArithmeticDiffTest.addDaysLandingInTheGapUsesTheEarlierNoOvershootInstant`, multiplatform `CalendarStepDifferenceInDaysTest`, and the structural long-span arm in `ConstructedDateCalendarTest`.

### IF187 — a reached CustomCondition callback owns formal-error and relevance decisions (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; completes SPEC-2026-07-20-11)

The interpreter reconstructed `formallyIncorrect` inside `Interpreter.evalCustom` by enumerating only valued fields and calling the row-relative `invalid(path)` predicate. That broke three parts of the kernel contract at one mechanism: required-empty cells were absent, each repeated pointer was re-resolved through the current rule row instead of retaining its own indices, and partial non-relevance was misreported as formal invalidity. The same implementation hard-coded every custom leaf as unable to fire empty, masking the legal `CustomCondition X Or FieldFilled(error)` blank-row shape behind the common guarded `FieldFilled(error) And CustomCondition X` idiom.

The root fix moves formal-address ownership to the call-local formal pass. The document-wide valued-field pass and required-field pass feed the shared `FormalResultCache`, which records each reached `FormalResult.Invalid` pointer at its concrete indices and relevance boundary. `ValidationPass` exposes that exact set to callbacks while ordinary operand invalidity remains a separate path predicate. The earlier implementation re-enumerated every declared field address lazily when a custom condition or group predicate was reached; IF193 removed that sparse-model cost because the mandatory/formal pre-pass had already classified every invalid candidate. Any number of callbacks now share the cache-owned snapshot without a second declared-domain walk. Unsupported checks and group-only errors are not invented as field pointers. Full validation passes `relevant = null`; partial validation passes the external relevant set and only formal pointers that call actually checked. The current error pointer continues to come from the rule row's exact repetitions.

`Cond.Custom` is now structurally empty-eligible. Existing `Or` recursion already admits any eligible child and existing `And` recursion requires both, matching `CanConditionFireEmptyVisitor` without special cases. The callback may therefore run despite formal errors or on a blank row, inspect its four inputs, and decide its Boolean; a true leaf contributes VALUE and a false leaf remains non-firing. The complete rule still must reference its error field somewhere (`MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED`).

This is an intentional behavior correction for consumers: callbacks that inspect `formallyIncorrect` through the model-aware `DmInterpreter` validation path can now see required-empty and correctly indexed repeated fields, and a blank-row custom `Or` can now invoke project code, return true, or throw where the interpreter previously skipped it. The public `Interpreter`, `EvalConfig`, and `CustomCondition` signatures remain ABI-identical. A direct low-level `Interpreter` has no `ModelDef` or formal pass from which to synthesize missing required cells, so its existing valued-field-plus-`invalid` best-effort fallback is preserved rather than silently changing to an empty set; kernel-exact callback inputs are the `DmInterpreter` contract.

*Locked:* `CustomConditionInvocationDiffTest` tri-checks Groovy-dynamic, generated static Java, and the interpreter for required-empty plus one malformed repeated pointer, exact current-row error pointer, full/null versus partial relevance, clean out-of-relevance exclusion, callback-owned firing despite the supplied formal set, and blank-row custom `Or` versus guarded `And`. `CustomConditionInvocationTest` repeats the formal-address and empty-eligibility mechanisms in commonTest on JVM and Node and locks the direct low-level constructor's compatibility fallback. Existing `CustomConditionDiffTest` and `PartialValidationCustomConditionDiffTest` retain the common guarded shape and relevance controls.

### IF188 — validation tallies and computation scans are separate fill-quantifier contracts (SHIPPED 2026-07-20; reconciles SPEC-2026-07-20-12/13)

Unfiltered validation classifies the complete selected range extensionally: known-filled and known-empty cells enter separate buckets, a formally unavailable cell remains in the range but enters neither, and a declared-but-uninstantiated slot is empty only for a declared-range clause. `quantifierFires` encodes all seven exact formulas; the caller exposes only a fired VALUE/OMISSION or the kernel-visible collapsed `FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN`. Whole-condition empty-row eligibility and `Having` remain separate consumers. `ValidationFillQuantifierProjectionDiffTest` tri-checks the sufficient-witness separators—including VALUE at `t=2,u=1`, OMISSION at `t=1,e=1,u=1`, and unavailable-only silence—against both kernel strategies.

Computation consumes the ordered expanded cell stream instead. Every reached invalid cell poisons; each operator stops at its own decision; `NotExactlyOneFieldFilled` and `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled` restart their second component scan from the beginning. `ComputationFillQuantifierRestartDiffTest` tri-checks the observable `[empty, filled, invalid]` restart separator, while `ComputationFillQuantifierScanContractTest` records the exact visit trace for all seven on JVM+Node and proves the composites remain bounded by two linear passes. That guard exposed the one path still losing cell identity: a kept `Having`-filtered star candidate used only `cellState(path)`, bypassing the call's complete `FormalResultCache`, so a custom-validator/charset-style invalid cell could count as filled. The root fix carries `path + repetitions + raw + baseState` through the same `ScanCell` refinement as every other scan; `FilteredStarComputeFormalInvalidityTest` locks kept custom-validator and charset invalidity, dropped-invalid non-read, and early-stop before a later invalid candidate.

### IF189 — seed generation prepares each distinct model-static pattern once per generator (SHIPPED 2026-07-20)

`ValidValues.patternSample` formerly compiled a field pattern for every generated cell, so setup work scaled with patterned fields × rows × regex compilation and could dominate the evaluation whose performance the generated document was meant to measure. The generator-owned `PreparedValidValues` now prepares each distinct pattern source once, reuses the shared `PatternRegistry`, and caches both successful and unavailable samples. The cache is scoped to one `SeedGenerator`; independent generators share no mutable state.

*Locked:* `SeedGeneratorPatternPreparationTest` generates 2,000 rows across three fields with two distinct pattern sources, proves exactly two preparations and compilations, locks deterministic reuse, and proves an independent generator receives its own preparation cache on JVM and Node.

### IF190 — structural `CurrentRepetition` operands do not create computation dependencies (SHIPPED 2026-07-21)

A synthesized depth-shaped model first exposed a false computation-cycle rejection. The dependency walk treated the group operand of `CurrentRepetition(group)` like a value reference, after which group expansion added an edge to every computed field below that group. A legal pair—one computation guarded by the current row coordinate and a second computation reading the first target—therefore became a manufactured cycle.

The dependency projection now excludes only `CurrentRepetition`'s structural group operand. Ordinary function operands remain value dependencies, and the independent scope/message reference walks still retain the group path. This matches the kernel's calculation-operand collector and fixes the dependency mechanism rather than suppressing one generated model.

*Locked:* `CurrentRepetitionComputationDependencyDiffTest` authors a small shipment model through the typed dmkits surface and tri-checks dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter; multiplatform `CurrentRepetitionComputationDependencyTest` locks the same structural guard plus real downstream dependency on JVM and Node.

### IF191 — dense RNU messages share their output-sized peer projection (SHIPPED 2026-07-21)

The compact synthesized profile's dense `RepetitionNotUnique` branch made every one of N rows fire with N+4 referenced pointers. The logical result is necessarily O(N²), but the interpreter also rebuilt, formatted, de-duplicated, and copied the identical N-key peer list for every firing row. JFR located that avoidable physical amplification in `DmInterpreter.rowOutcomes`: repeated arrays, `ArrayList`s, and `LinkedHashMap` entries dominated allocation, while the hash-grouped duplicate detector itself stayed linear.

`RepetitionNotUniqueEvaluator` now formats and de-duplicates one immutable peer-pointer projection per duplicate cluster. Every row outcome shares it. `rowOutcomes` removes same-key ordinary occurrences by path and presents the remaining small current-row prefix plus the shared cluster through an immutable O(1) joined list view; no N-entry list is copied per message. Canonical pointer text is cached in the document's already mutation-invalidated derived index, so formal, auto-check, error, ordinary-reference, and RNU projection paths reuse one rendering until the document changes. Observable ordering, de-duplication, VALUE/OMISSION metadata, and complete result cardinality are unchanged.

On the exact compact synthesized profile at N=100/400 (490/1,800 firings), after 12 warmups and with identical fixed clock, custom callback, model, document, and complete tri-engine signatures, interpreter medians were 16.85/27.28 ms versus dynamic Groovy 55.25/302.17 ms and generated Java 37.98/318.26 ms. Exact growth was 1.62× versus 5.47× and 8.38×; the independent paired interpreter estimator was 3.95×. These are diagnostic same-run measurements, not promoted NFR bars.

*Locked:* `RepetitionNotUniqueProjectionReuseTest` proves one duplicate cluster's rich-message projection is shared across every pure-RNU firing on JVM and Node; `DocumentPointerFormatCacheTest` proves one canonical rendering per address and complete invalidation on mutation. `RepetitionNotUniqueExactnessTest` and the both-strategy `RepetitionNotUniqueExactnessDiffTest`, `RepetitionNotUniqueGuardedDiffTest`, and `RepetitionNotUniqueCompositionDiffTest` retain exact message semantics.

### IF192 — synthesized performance curves bind equivalent runtime work and pair all three engines (SHIPPED 2026-07-21)

The first synthesized-model curve could configure an interpreter clock/callback independently from the kernel clock/callback, omitted custom field-type factories on the kernel side, fingerprinted neither runtime configuration, and applied IF137's paired estimator only to the interpreter. A green row could therefore compare different work or accept a worse kernel-relative curve. The external synthesized model path was also only a system-property string, so replacing its bytes at the same path could leave Gradle's test result up-to-date.

`PerfRuntimeBindings` is now the one immutable source for paired clocks, custom conditions, and custom field types. Each registration carries both interpreter and kernel implementations plus a stable contract id; it derives both engines' configuration, owns defensive copies, creates a fresh kernel `Date` per call, and contributes its descriptors to the scenario fingerprint. Comparative harness methods reject the explicitly separate interpreter-only preflight seam. Validation, computation, and custom-field rejection locks prove the bindings reach dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter with complete equal signatures.

`pairedScenarioGrowth` builds each engine and exact N/4N instance once outside timing, tri-checks both scales, then applies the interleaved median-of-within-pair estimator independently to all three engines. It fails unless the interpreter median beats both kernel strategies at both scales and interpreter growth is no worse than either. Synthesized profiles must declare their complexity and generator metadata, mark generated Java as present, emit both scale fingerprints, and register the model file itself as a Gradle input. The G52 `:adapter:synthesizedModelPerfTest` workflow now starts directly from a closed enum registry over the committed compact, breadth-shaped, and depth-shaped synthesis-profile fixtures, treats them as diagnostic stress shapes rather than fidelity-certified calibration evidence, synthesizes into ignored test output before evaluation, and separately reports model loading, interpreter construction, exact-preflighted first answer, warm evaluation, and the paired curve. An explicit synthesized-model seam remains for focused profiling.

On the compact dense-RNU profile at N=100/400 with one diagnostic pair and 12 warmups, the corrected lane measured interpreter 9.91/24.82 ms, dynamic Groovy 41.07/256.69 ms, and generated Java 30.16/235.13 ms; growth was 2.51×/6.25×/7.79×. The numbers remain diagnostic rather than promoted release bars, while the kernel-beating development assertions are mandatory.

*Locked:* `PerfRuntimeBindingsTest`, `ScenarioIdentityFingerprintTest.runtimeBindingsArePartOfTheFingerprint`, `SynthesizedProfileScenarioRegistryTest`, and the opt-in `SynthesizedModelPerfTest`; `:adapter:synthesizedModelPerfTest` declares all committed profile fixtures as inputs, while the external-model seam binds its selected model by content.

### IF193 — validation group presence preserves admitted content and erroneous state independently (SHIPPED 2026-07-21)

The breadth synthesized profile exposed 26 interpreter-only rules whose non-repeatable group contained only a number rejected by `minFractionalDigits`. `Document.groupFilled` indexed every raw valued placement, so the interpreter called the group filled; the kernel first admits formally converted data into its group-fill cache and records rejected cells on an independent erroneous-entity path.

The fix is one call-local staged index. `AdmittedGroupContentIndex` propagates valid values, duplicate-index values admitted before uniqueness checking, and instantiated repeatable rows to ancestor group instances in O(placements × depth), reading group rows directly from the document index rather than constructing row contexts or rescanning content per model group. The formal-result cache uses that admitted-content view for requiredness, so an invalid-only parent no longer activates a `relativeToParent` sibling, and now owns the already-reached invalid-pointer set instead of triggering a second declared-field expansion. `GroupPresenceIndex` overlays content, error, and NONE/PARTIAL/FULL relevance dimensions. Positive presence is knowable from any relevant descendant; negative absence requires full relevance and no formal error. Group-list quantifiers use the resulting true/false buckets, while the plain multi-group `NumberOfFilledGroups` becomes unavailable if any operand group is erroneous or not fully relevant. A live tri-engine discriminator refuted the review-time source prediction that an over-limit repeat row is absent from ancestor presence: creating the row still fills its non-repeatable ancestor, while its structural diagnostic and ordinary-row suppression remain separate. The raw document content index remains unchanged for rule-row eligibility and compute mode.

*Locked:* typed own-domain `GroupPresenceFormalInvalidDiffTest` tri-checks empty, invalid-only, valid-only, valid-plus-invalid, all five group-list quantifiers, the plain count, and relative-requiredness. `GroupPresenceAdmissionDiffTest` tri-checks positive-vs-negative partial relevance, complete descendant/group relevance, and the live over-limit-row behavior. Both compare dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter.

### IF194 — multi-star declared-tail polarity is hierarchical from the first star (SHIPPED 2026-07-21)

The breadth profile's `G19*/G20*/G21*/F149` rule exposed a value/omission type split: the interpreter compared the flattened leaf-row count only with `G21`'s per-parent repeatability. One hundred leaf rows therefore looked complete against a cap of two even though the outer `G19` declared 961 rows and actual branches were missing. The same shortcut loses a missing middle branch.

`Document.RowContext.starOmission` now follows the parser's existing first-star bind depth and checks each reopened repeatable level per actual parent. Missing capacity at the outer, middle, or leaf level makes the aggregate fillable; levels above the first star stay bound. `DocumentConfig` now retains the model's complete repeatable-group set, so an unbounded group with no declared cap correctly retains an open tail instead of disappearing from this proof. The proof walks only actual indexed rows, never materializes the declared Cartesian domain, and caches the complete Boolean per `(path, bindDepth, scope)` in the document's mutation-invalidated index. This is O(actual rows × star depth) once per scope and O(1) for every subsequent rule-row read.

*Locked:* typed own-domain `MultiStarDeclaredTailPolarityDiffTest` tri-checks missing outer, middle, and leaf capacity, full saturation, and a bound-outer control against both kernel strategies. The existing document-cache invalidation boundary owns the new memo.

### IF195 — valid number seeding honors the complete fractional-digit interval (SHIPPED 2026-07-21)

The breadth synthesized profile exposed a formally invalid number produced by the generator itself. `ValidValues.number` treated `maxFractionalDigits` as a range from zero, ignored `minFractionalDigits`, and returned a selected `minValue`/`maxValue` bound verbatim. A fixed two-decimal field could therefore receive an integer or one-decimal sample and suppress the rule whose performance the document was intended to exercise.

NUMBER seeding now validates the declared `[minFractionalDigits, maxFractionalDigits]` interval before producing a value. An unbounded value with a positive minimum chooses its emitted scale from that inclusive interval; a selected numeric bound retains its value and is padded to the declared minimum. The `minFractionalDigits = 0` branch retains the former random calls and output sequence exactly, so existing seeded fixtures remain deterministic. An inconsistent minimum above the maximum fails loudly instead of emitting a known-invalid value.

*Locked:* multiplatform `ValidValuesFractionDigitsTest` covers fixed and variable unbounded scales, lower-only/upper-only/two-bound padding, an inconsistent interval, and exact legacy random-sequence preservation on JVM and Node. `ValidValuesConstraintTest` retains the range and unconstrained-number controls.

### IF196 — breadth validation prepares model paths once and reads structural rows directly (SHIPPED 2026-07-21)

The exact breadth-shaped synthesized curve exposed a repeatable growth loss: at 100→400 selected leaf rows the interpreter grew 1.73–1.82× while dynamic Groovy grew 1.59–1.69× in the failing runs. An interpreter-only JFR attributed the largest avoidable clusters to two sibling validation passes rebuilding model-static path structure. `AutoChecks.overLimitLevel` split every valued-field path and joined each prefix once during formal checking and again during structural over-repetition emission; the group branch also called `contextsFor` merely to recover row repetitions, which needlessly built content-aware evaluation contexts. IF193's admitted-content pass independently split and rejoined the same path for every admitted cell while propagating it to ancestor groups.

`ModelPathIndex` now prepares each declared entity's group prefixes and ordered repetition bounds once per `DmInterpreter`. Formal checking, over-repetition emission, and admitted-content propagation share those immutable entries. The structural group scan reads `Document.instantiatedRows` directly because it needs row identity, not an evaluated `EvalContext`; this removes the sibling content-index work without changing which rows or errors are observed. The call-local content/error indexes and all public results remain unchanged.

On the exact profile-derived breadth model, fixed clock/callback, exact N/4N documents, complete tri-engine signature preflight, three interleaved pairs, four warmups, and two timed iterations, the corrected curve measured 23.04→35.90 ms for the interpreter (1.43×), 127.15→200.31 ms for dynamic Groovy (1.53×), and 53.90→109.59 ms for generated Java (1.84×). The interpreter remained faster at both scales and now grew better than both kernel strategies. These numbers are diagnostic development evidence, not promoted NFR bars.

*Locked:* multiplatform `ModelPathIndexTest` checks prepared ancestor/group-prefix lookup and outermost-bound selection; `OverRepetitionDiffTest`, `GroupPresenceAdmissionDiffTest`, and `GroupPresenceFormalInvalidDiffTest` retain exact dynamic-Groovy/generated-Java/interpreter semantics. The opt-in `SynthesizedModelPerfTest` is the decisive complete-result, paired tri-engine breadth guard.

### IF197 — performance compute comparisons project report-all results to the kernel's delta boundary (SHIPPED 2026-07-21)

The first depth-shaped COMPUTE preflight produced one interpreter-only `VALUE|V1` for a computed STRING whose input seed was already `V1`. This was a harness artifact, not an evaluator divergence: the interpreter's public compute API deliberately reports every computed instance, while both kernel strategies report only changes from the input. Corpus comparison paths already applied `kernelComputeGranularity`; `InterpreterPerfEngine` had compared its report-all signatures directly.

The interpreter performance instance captures the exact source placements while it materializes the document, outside every clock, and applies the shared kernel-delta projection while materializing each timed COMPUTE result. Validation is unchanged. IF199 subsequently split the input-static pointer lookup from the result-side projection: only the former prepares with the document, while computation, complete signature materialization, filtering, and sorting remain inside every clock. Failed paired-growth assertions also retain both N/4N rows' exact interpreter/Groovy/Java medians, so a curve cannot hide its absolute comparison when the growth gate fires.

*Locked:* `PerfComputeGranularityTest` authors an unchanged STRING computation through the typed dmkits surface and proves that the interpreter engine presents the empty kernel-delta signature set before any cross-engine comparison. The corrected depth-shaped `SynthesizedModelPerfTest` preflight agrees completely across the interpreter, dynamic Groovy, and generated Java at N=100 and N=400.

### IF198 — empty-string ingestion materializes an empty cell, never a filled value (SHIPPED 2026-07-21)

The regenerated compact profile exposed five interpreter-only firings (C101, C103, C105, C108, and C113) before performance timing could start. Their shared operand was a STRING cell stored as `""`: dynamic Groovy and generated Java treated it as present-but-unfilled, while `Document.field` retained `value = ""`, so direct `FieldFilled`, requiredness, and the document content index all treated it as filled. `cellStateAt` happened to report `PresentEmpty` because it independently checked `raw.isNullOrEmpty()`, leaving the public stored-state read correct while evaluation consumers disagreed with it.

The root fix is at the one document-ingestion boundary.
